## Summary

This system records where everything in a ship is: which rooms exist, where their walls, doors and machinery stand as exact integer rectangles, where another ship section can be welded on, and which sections are joined or cut apart. One set of rectangles is what stops the player's body, what stops their lamp's light and what is drawn, so the walls are the one thing in the game that never lies, and the seams and cap plates left by welds and cuts stay as visible history. It exists so that the life-support graph, lighting, trails, ship physics, assembly and saving all share one answer to "where is it?" instead of each inventing their own.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer` (designed), `Life-Support Graph` (designed; the two additive asks OQ-3 and OQ-4 are answered and closed), `Item & Module Database` (designed). Downstream: `Ship Body & Interior Physics`, `Modular Ship Assembly`, `Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)` (all designed; only `Procedural Derelict Generation` is still undesigned). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

> **MVP slice (this GDD):** schema v1 and the structural lint; four sections on four bodies built at `init_world` (home-ship 5 rooms, derelict A 4, derelict B 5, plus the one-room 2 × 2-tile `pod_cabin` section, always dormant and outside the ship totals; registry `mvp_template_roster`); exactly one socket pair (home-ship airlock ↔ derelict B); one weld, one cut (SEALED or RAGGED), cap plate and scar; static props (no prop mutator); door leaves from the Graph's `area`; `foundry` dialect only; story-slot list reserved and empty; no engine tile collision, no navigation mesh. **Deferred (Vertical Slice):** the socket economy (OQ-1), `exposed_tiles` re-base (OQ-11), `template_ref` source `GENERATED` and story slots (Procgen), movable props (one deliberate bump-list entry), `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` / `can_detach` CAPACITY (OQ-8; the deferral is confirmed by Modular Ship Assembly, T15 L4), co-op catalogue-hash refusal (OQ-24). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Scope ruled (X1, D52):** the one cut, the cap plate and scar, and module install at a hardpoint are MVP by user ruling; the concept's MVP item 3 and the systems index are amended to "weld + player seam cut + install" (by their owners). Co-op stays out of the MVP.

## Overview

The Ship Section & Layout Data Model is the game's single answer to the question **"where is everything in a ship?"** — a scene-tree-free, integer-only simulation block (`layout.state`) plus a static catalogue of authored **section templates**, a small set of pure queries and four state-changing mutators. A *section* is one authored piece of ship (a home-ship hull, a derelict wreck, a graftable module block): its rooms as sets of floor tiles, its wall and door and prop footprints as integer rectangles, its sockets (the only places another section can be welded on), its hardpoints (where a module can be installed), its loose-item and story slots, and the anchors where pipes, fixtures and trails attach. Layout records which sections exist, which body (rigid frame) each belongs to, how far each is offset, and which welds and cuts have been made between them. It is the single owner of geometry and placement, so that the Graph, Emitter State, Failure & Trail, Ship Body, Assembly, Derelict Authoring and Save/Load never each invent their own socket, room id or rectangle.

**What it owns, in one sentence:** Layout owns *where things are* — room identity and tile sets, the volume `V`, `exposed_tiles`, footprint rectangles, sockets, hardpoints, slots, anchors, body → section → welded-socket membership, section offsets and the weld/cut history — and owns nothing physical, nothing perceptual and no gameplay state. The routed ownership clause is part of this paragraph and is quoted from its source, `life-support-graph.md` LS-6 (LSG:74; routed again by LSG:1158 Open Question 9), word for word:

> **Layout owns** room identity, polygon, `V` (tiles) and `exposed_tiles`; the Graph caches `V` and `exposed_tiles` as derived fields (not hashed; rebuilt in `post_restore`, which is why `restore_after: [layout.state]`). **The Graph owns** the atmosphere — `n_o2`, `n_inert`, `T`, `t_rem`, `fuel_puddle_ml` — and every opening/hatch `area` and `door_state`, every VALVE `open` and every breaker `closed`; Interaction issues the commands, the Graph publishes the mutators (R11; one writer per fact, kernel H3).

Stated as the Layout side of the same clause: **sections own compartment/room GEOMETRY and the volume `V` in tiles; the Graph owns atmosphere and node/edge state.** Layout only *projects* the Graph's opening `area` into a door-leaf rectangle (LY-9); it never stores, mutates or mirrors it. Body owns motion and pose, Assembly owns the weld and cut commands and weld quality `q`, the Item & Module Database owns the vocabulary of what can be installed, Interaction owns every loose item instance. Every table in this GDD has an owner column so that none of those rows is ever read as Layout's.

**How the player meets it.** Never as a menu, a map or an editor. The player meets Layout as the *truth of the space*: a wall that stops their body, their lamp's beam and a spreading frost trail at the same line; a door whose leaf is exactly as open to the eye as it is to the foot and to the light; a Hull Grey seam bead on the floor and up the wall where a wreck was welded onto the home-ship, bright when new and dulled once lived with; a cap plate and nothing beyond it where a section was cut away. The only things the player ever *does* with it are the things Assembly and Interaction let them do on top of it — walk, open a door, weld at a socket, cut along a seam — and each of those lands in Layout's data as an atomic, replayable change.

**What the game loses without it.** Without a single owner of geometry, three GDDs would each define a socket (the index's own reason for this system, `TD-SYSTEM-BOUNDARY`, IDX:28); collision and light would be computed from different rectangles and a wall could be seen but walked through (a Pillar 1 failure); the Graph's compartments would have nothing to be keyed by and `restore_after: [layout.state]` would have no root; the weld — the riskiest MVP task, merging a section into a moving ship's collision, tile map and graph — would have no data to merge; and a save could not say which parts of the ship were welded to which. Layout is the `restore_after` root of three registered or drafted blocks (`graph.state`, `emitter.state`, and Interaction's `interaction.state`, IA-36), which makes the **stability of room identity across versions** the top risk of Save/Load (SL:274, SL:472, SL:1300 Open Question 12) and the first thing this GDD pins down (LY-6).

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index describes this system as "the runtime section schema shared by authoring, procgen, assembly and save: tile layer reference, collision shapes, sockets, seams, graph-node references, salvage/module slots, and the pure-data ship layout (sections, transforms, welds) that is projected onto the scene tree" (IDX:28). Read through this GDD: the *tile layer reference* is the room tile set on the `TILE_PX` 48 floor tile (LY-5); the *collision shapes* are the integer footprint rectangles consumed as **simulation data** by Body's `LocomotionStep` and by the Emitter's segment test — Layout never calls them engine collision shapes and the avatar has no physics body (LY-7; wording refresh routed in Cross-References); *sockets* and *seams* are LY-13 to LY-17; *graph-node references* are the template-local `node_ref` keys mapped to numeric Graph ids through a persisted `graph_ref_base` (LY-6); *salvage/module slots* are two distinct nouns — **hardpoints** (module slots, node-backed, LY-20) and **loose slots** (authored start positions for items, geometry only, LY-22); the *pure-data ship layout projected onto the scene tree* is the `layout.state` block and its one-way PROJECTOR (LY-31, kernel H10).

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (→ `/architecture-decision`): the layout block shape — instances by reference against immutable templates (ADR-LAY-01); rectangle and broadphase ownership and the `OccluderSource` seam, joint with the Emitter's ADR candidate 4 (ADR-LAY-02); the PROJECTOR scene shape — per-body against per-section tile layers (ADR-LAY-03); `room_key` / `template_id` stability and migration (ADR-LAY-04); the id-allocation split between Layout and the Graph's `next_*` counters (ADR-LAY-05); the field order of the two template-local byte strings (ADR-LAY-06; the hash function is the kernel's ratified H64 and the block byte form is the kernel's TLV, *Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix*); and the kernel's `post_restore` ordering (ADR-LAY-07, routed to the kernel owner).*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: indirect.** The player never sees "the layout", a section, a socket list or a tile. They live inside what it makes true: a ship whose shape is the record of what the crew did to it, and whose walls are the one thing in the game that cannot be argued with. *(creative-director consulted; Framing A "The Ship Is Its History" adopted as the headline, Framing B "The Wall Is the Last Witness" as its guarantee under Pillar 4; Framing C "The Shipwright" rejected — it breeds a build mode, a blueprint, a ship overview and rotation tools, every one of them a guard-rail violation below. Logged.)* Like the Graph and the Emitter, Layout is felt as properties of other systems' moments — a trail that stops at a sill, a beam that stops at a bulkhead, a seam bead on the floor — and a direct framing would need a direct interaction whose only candidate is building, which the anti-pillar forbids.

> The home-ship is not a place the crew found. It is a body they assembled from other ships' corpses, and its shape is the record of how they did it. Every room is a room some dead crew built; every seam between two rooms is a decision this crew made — a Hull Grey bead on the floor and up the wall where they welded a wreck to their own hull, bright when it was new and dull once they had lived with it. A section they cut away leaves a seam with a cap plate and nothing beyond it. The ship forgets nothing it was made from, and nothing that was done to it.
>
> Competence is reading a space, not arranging it. A veteran does not build a better ship; they read one faster: where the original hull ends and the graft begins, which hatch in a seam is the one that was welded shut, which corridor is too narrow to outrun a breach in, which wall of a room faces the void. The crew chooses *what* to graft and *whether* to cut. Where a section can go was decided by the dead people who built its sockets.
>
> And the walls never lie. Gauges can be wrong, sprites can be wrong, eyes can be wrong, but a wall stops your body, stops your light and stops the frost at the same line, for every player, on every frame. When nothing else can be trusted, you can still walk into it.

**The emotion named: belonging to a made thing.** Not ownership-as-possession and not mastery-as-design, but the feeling of living inside something you assembled with your own hands, whose scars you can point to and name ("that's where we took the engine room off the hot wreck"). The power is not control over the ship's shape. It is the small, durable authority of a crew whose history is written in steel and cannot be edited — not by them, and not by the web.

**Noun guard.** "Body" in the fantasy ("a body they assembled") is the concept's word for the composite ship and lives in prose only. Everywhere else in this GDD `body` and `body_id` mean the *rigid moving frame* that a set of welded sections shares — the Ship Body & Interior Physics system's noun — and the two are never mixed. The body metaphor never becomes visual or textual flesh: flesh tones and violet appear nowhere in the sane world (concept, Visual Identity Anchor; art bible 6.0), materials are cold metals, and a seam is a Hull Grey bead, not a scar drawn as tissue.

**Language.** In player-facing text and in this section use: *section, graft, seam, weld, bead, cut, cap plate, socket, hatch, bulkhead, hull, wreck, donor, the join, the old ship / the new part, the wall faces the void.* Never: *build, construct, design, place, blueprint, layout (as a player noun), room editor, tile, grid, map, minimap, schematic, floor plan, snap, rotate, mirror, module grid, base-building.* "Tile" and "grid" are correct internal nouns in the rules and formulas; they never describe what the player experiences. The player discovers a socket as a visible hull feature — a hatch ring, a mating collar — never as a highlighted list.

**Anchor moment — "the graft" (MVP slice, solo; distances and durations illustrative, none is a constant).**
1. *Arrival.* The home-ship and derelict B lie hull to hull at the one socket the home-ship has (LSG:228). On your side the socket is a hatch ring in the airlock-side bulkhead you have walked past every day — closed, dark, a door frame with nothing behind it. You always knew that was where something could go.
2. *The weld.* The weld itself is Assembly's verb. What Layout guarantees is what you see when it finishes: nothing moved. The room around you is the room it was. Along the join, on the floor and up the wall face, a raised Hull Grey bead runs the width of the socket — bright, clean, *fresh*. Two door frames sit in line across it, one built by your ship's makers, one by the wreck's.
3. *The hatch you have not opened.* The weld connects nothing yet: the hatch is sealed, the valves across the seam are shut, the breaker is open (LS-15, "a weld moves nothing"). The graft is part of your ship and still a stranger. Opening it is a choice you make with your hands (Interaction's verb, the Graph's state). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the MVP door is binary, so there is no sliding leaf to promise.)* You press, the motor whines for half a second with the leaf unmoved, and then the leaf is simply gone from the doorway in one tick: from that frame the gap is open to your body, your light and your eye at once — there is no frame in which you can see through a door you cannot walk through, and no frame in which a half-drawn leaf says one thing and the wall says another.
4. *Living with it.* Later — ten minutes of ship time by default — the bead has gone *old*: dulled, scuffed, part of the ship. Nobody flagged the change. It is just how a seam looks once you have lived with it.
5. *The cut (the cost).* The graft went bad — its engine room is a fire the crew cannot hold — and you cut it away along the seam (never through a room; cuts happen only along seams, LSG:172, LSG:849). Done cleanly, the hatch ring on your side becomes a cap plate with nothing beyond it. Done in a hurry, it is a ragged stub that keeps leaking until the crew deals with it. Either way the socket does not go back to how it was before the weld: within a saved world the ship carries that scar for as long as this ship exists, and the crew will walk past it every day. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "for the rest of the ship's life" ignored that a death restores the last autosave, which is most likely right after a crisis cut; permanence is scoped to a saved world below, and the weld and the cut are both autosave event triggers — Save/Load `ASSEMBLY_WELD` and `ASSEMBLY_CUT`, SL `EVENT_TRIGGERS`, which Open Questions asks to be in the MVP baseline — so the scar is written to disk the moment it is made.)*

*Scope of the scar promise.* "The rest of the ship's life" means the life of this ship's saved world. It is not a promise that a scar outlives a crew: losing the home-ship costs ship and body and only knowledge persists (Pillar 5), and the binding MVP death rule is restart from the last autosave, owned by Save/Load. `layout.state` is WORLD-scoped and rolls back on `request_restore` (LY-35), so a restore to an autosave taken before the cut returns the socket to its pre-cut state and the scar is gone. Whether a given scar survives a death-restore therefore belongs to Save/Load's autosave cadence and is not a Layout promise.

The arc is anticipation (the closed ring you always walked past) → commitment (the weld) → wariness (the sealed stranger) → belonging (the bead goes old) → loss that leaves a mark (the cap plate). It is the Project Zomboid base-with-friends feeling given a hull: the base remembers.

*MVP honesty note.* The MVP kit is Foundry only (all 24 MVP defs are `dialect: foundry`, IDB), so in MVP the graft does **not** read by a dialect change; it reads by the doubled bulkhead and the wreck's own wear and dust (art bible 6.1 "Wear and age"), with the seam bead marking only *where* the join is, not which side is the original ship (the bead is symmetric). Because X-1 depends on that wear, donor-wear differentiation is an explicit **ASK to the art-director and an MVP requirement for X-1** (OQ-25, Visual/Audio Requirements); if it is not committed, X-1 drops to Vertical Slice and this note says so. "A graft keeps its donor dialect" (art bible 3.3) becomes visible from Alpha, when three dialects exist; this GDD does not promise a dialect contrast at MVP. Likewise "you are standing where you stood" after a weld depends on the Pipeline's "where the layout allows" clause (PIPE:694) and is Body's and the Pipeline's to deliver, not a Layout rule.

**Pillar alignment.**
- **Pillar 1, The World Never Lies (primary), verbatim:** *"Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong."* Layout is the "physical world" layer of that sentence. One set of integer rectangles is what stops a body (Body's sweep, PCI:103), what stops light (the Emitter's segment test, EM-20) and what the projection draws; a trail stops at the sill of its own room (FT:705). Geometry cannot disagree with itself, and there is no "perceived" variant of any Layout query, mirroring LSG:199 ("there is no 'perceived' variant anywhere in the Graph").
- **Pillar 5, Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total (serves), verbatim:** *"Failure contract: losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge."* Layout's share is the ship-side half: the ship's history is persisted as geometry — welds with the tick they were made, cap plates, cut scars — not as a flag or a blueprint. What the ship loses, it shows it lost.
- **Pillar 4, Sanity Rewrites Reality (a constraint, not a feature), verbatim:** *"No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time — and checking costs time, risk or trust. At least one channel of truth always remains."* Layout owns no sanity input and no hallucination; it is the falsification ground for spatial lies.
- **Pillar 3, The Web Watches (indirect):** Layout owns no stimulus. It supplies the geometry stimulus owners read: which wall tiles face the void, which section an emitter belongs to, where light is stopped.
- **Pillar 2, Dread Before Chaos: none.** Layout has no tell and no tempo. Indirect support only: the trail-edge bound and corridor clearance rules keep other systems' tells drawable.
- **Anti-pillar served:** *"NOT a freeform ship-building sandbox: welding is functional and modules are pre-shaped."* Layout is where that anti-pillar is enforced in data: sections are authored whole and attach only at pre-declared sockets.
- **MDA (concept):** Layout's felt contribution is **Fantasy** ("a ship that is ours because we welded it"), **Discovery** (reading who built a room, what was done to it, and whether the web has come in — art bible 6.0's three questions in one beam-sweep) and **Expression** bounded to "which modules to graft and where — functional, not cosmetic", where "where" is limited to authored sockets. It also serves **Challenge**, the concept's #2 aesthetic, through spatial literacy: corridor clearance against breach escape is held by the escape-route budget (D.19) and clear-path (D.17) lints, and "which wall faces the void" is geometry the player must read under pressure, so Layout's Competence is the footing that Challenge stands on (advisory trace; Layout supplies the lengths and clearances, the pressure is other systems'). It delivers **Competence** as spatial literacy and **Autonomy** as "which section, and whether to cut" — never freedom of shape.

**Design test.** *A ship whose shape can be re-derived from a blueprint vs. a ship that remembers the welds and cuts that made it → the ship that remembers.* It resolves live decisions in this GDD: rotation and mirroring of sections → no (a section attaches only by its authored socket facing; a rotated copy is a blueprint operation, LY-3); does a cut socket return to its pre-weld state → no, it becomes a cap plate or a ragged stub (LY-13); is seam age a stored flag or derived from when the weld was made → derived from the weld record's tick (LY-33); does the save store a ship design → no, it stores instances, welds and scars against immutable templates (LY-2). *Secondary (mirrors Pillar 1's):* **a wall the player can see but walk through vs. a wall the player cannot see but collides → neither is permitted.** It resolves one rectangle set for collision, light and drawing (LY-7, LY-12), the door leaf as one rectangle derived from the Graph's opening (LY-9) — never see-through-but-solid or solid-but-see-through, including a ragged cut hatch (LY-15) — and a boundary convention that lets a player standing at a wall's base still light the room in front of them (LY-12).
Corollaries: nothing moves when a weld happens, only seams, hatches and connections appear; a cut follows a seam and no room is ever split; the player is never displaced by a door — a leaf that would close on a foot box is refused (the verb owner's immediate reject, player origin) or holds (`CLOSING_BLOCKED`, system origin) and never pushes (PCI:650, LY-11); a trail never crosses into a room it did not start in — it stops at the sill (LY-8).

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** The caretaker who cannot trust their eyes falls back on the oldest check there is: touch the wall. Layout makes that check always available and always honest, and stops there — it does not model any lie.
- *Given by construction:* collision is the sweep of Body's foot box against Layout's rectangles; light is stopped by the same rectangles. Neither reads anything perceptual, and no Layout query has a per-player or perceived variant. A phantom doorway in a solid wall is falsified by walking into it; a phantom wall across an open doorway is falsified by walking through it — each costs a few steps and the risk of where you are standing (Pillar 4: "checking costs time, risk or trust"). A crewmate's beam (inviolable, concept Pillar 1) is stopped by the same rectangles, so its true shape traces the true walls *(2D Lighting LO-11 builds its occluders from `rects_in`; designed)*. Trails stop at the sill of their own room, so the consequence channel (the Graph's closed list) never contradicts the walls.
- *An ask, now GRANTED (routed to the Perception Filter; written as "Layout requests"). **Granted 2026-10-27: Perception Filter PF-5 row 11, grade TRUTH_ST, OQ-13 resolved; Layout accepts the grant, and the fallback below was recorded there and not adopted** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier text left the grant pending)*.* Layout requests that wall faces, door leaves and socket/seam geometry be tagged truth-channel in the Filter's tag contract, so a hallucination may *add* a phantom visual (a door painted on a wall, a figure in a doorway) but never *move or remove* a wall, a leaf or a seam. The concept's never-shifting anchor list (character sprites, hand tools, the HUD frame, the light's physical radius) does **not** name walls, so the grant makes it a Filter rule, and the concept's list needs a one-line wording refresh (routed to the concept owner, never a silent edit). *If the Filter declines:* Pillar 4 still holds because the rectangle falsifies the sprite on contact; the fantasy degrades from "the wall is always where you see it" to "the wall is always where you *touch* it" — honest, more expensive. An acceptable fallback only, not the request: walls and seams stay truth-channel and the leaves of ordinary interior doors fall under the Filter's discretion as a cheap-to-falsify "false system state" that costs a bump, not a death. That cheapness holds only for an ordinary door. It does not hold for a hatch or seam door between a pressurised room and a breached or vented compartment, where a door that looks closed but is open sits on a lethal decision. So the fallback is narrowed and travels in the same Layout-requests ASK (OQ-13), not as a Filter rule: **even if the Filter takes discretion over interior doors, every hatch or door leaf on a seam or socket, and every door whose two sides the Graph reports at a pressure differential (the Graph's per-compartment pressure, read through its `read_view`; the threshold is for the Filter and the Graph to name, not Layout), stays truth-channel.**
- *Guard:* Layout carries no "phantom", "fake", "perceived" or per-player field and no false-door record. A phantom door is entirely the Filter's presentation.

**Reference techniques, taken precisely.**

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | The base as accumulated history — the lived-in mess you can point to and name. | Build mode: placing walls, floors, doors anywhere. |
| Barotrauma | One hull moved through at body scale; hatches and bulkheads as decisions; a breach *where it is*. | The submarine editor and the Status Monitor ship overview. |
| FTL | A ship made of rooms whose doors are causality (what you open changes what flows). | The schematic overview and per-room status shading. |
| Satisfactory | Legible machinery placed where it physically sits. | Freeform placement, snapping grids, the builder's rotate key. |
| Space Engineers / Starbound-style block building | — | Rejected outright: block-by-block construction is the anti-pillar. |
| Oxygen Not Included | Geometry as the constraint every gas and heat flow obeys. | Digging and building the space; the overlays. |
| Pandorum | Waking inside a ship whose layout is the only reliable narrator. | — |
| Dead Space (USG Ishimura) | The diegetic principle: the ship is read from its own surfaces, not a HUD. | The in-world holographic map and locator line (a map by any other name). |
| Real ship-breaking / shipwrighting | Seams and cap plates as honest records of joins and cuts; patches on-grid and off-rhythm (art bible 6.1). | — |

**What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails).**
1. No freeform construction: no runtime "place a wall / draw a room / add a door" capability exists in the schema. Sections are authored whole and attach only at pre-declared sockets.
2. No blueprint: no ship-design export, import or "save ship design"; the composite ship is instances, welds and scars only (SL:120).
3. No rotation, no mirror, no free transform: sections translate on the tile grid; variety comes from authored socket facings.
4. No ship map, minimap, schematic, overlay or outline of the ship. The only player-facing surfaces Layout implies are the weld/cut cursor outline data and socket highlight data, shaped by `/ux-design` under art bible 7.x grammar.
5. No cut through a room: cuts happen only along seams.
6. No erased history: a cut socket does not return to its pre-weld state in MVP; the bead ages by time, not by a cleanup action.
7. No exploration meter: no "explored" flag, fog-of-war reveal or percent-explored in Layout (concept Visual principle 1: rooms are not fogged, they are unlit).
8. No expression by shape: the player chooses which section and whether to cut; never where rooms go inside a section, nor any cosmetic arrangement.
9. No geometry that differs per player or per sanity: one rectangle set, every player, every frame.
10. The body metaphor never becomes flesh.

**Experience targets (playtest-checkable, not mechanics).** X-1 After one weld and one session of play a playtester can walk to the seam and say which side was the original ship without prompting (the seam reads as history) — *(manual; depends on the art-director's donor-wear commitment, OQ-25: the symmetric seam bead shows where the join is, not which side is original; without OQ-25 this target is Vertical Slice)*. X-2 No playtester reports "I could see through it but couldn't walk through" or the reverse, for any wall or door, across a full MVP session — *(manual)*, mirrored by the same-tick door criterion in Acceptance Criteria. X-3 Asked "can you rearrange your ship?", playtesters answer "no, but we can choose what to weld on and what to cut off" and do not ask for a build mode — *(manual; **downgraded to Vertical Slice** in the design review: with one home-ship socket and one mate there is no choice of *what* to weld, only whether, so the target is unfalsifiable at MVP — OQ-1; the MVP form is only "do not ask for a build mode")*. X-4 A playtester who cut the graft can point to where it was in a later session **of the same saved world** (a death-restore to an autosave taken before the cut returns the socket to its pre-cut state, LY-35) — *(manual)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Playtest protocol for X-1 to X-5** (defaults, tunable by the playtest owner): at least 5 first-time playtesters per target, a scripted session (one weld, one cut, one later session), answers transcribed and coded by two reviewers who agree before the count is taken. Pass lines: X-1 at least 4 of 5 name the original side unprompted; X-2 **0 of 5** report see-through-but-solid or the reverse; X-3 (MVP form) at least 4 of 5 do not ask for a build mode; X-4 at least 4 of 5 point to the cut's location; X-5 at least 4 of 5 falsify the phantom doorway in one attempt. X-5 Under a staged spatial hallucination (VS-A, once the Filter exists) a playtester falsifies a phantom doorway by walking into the wall within one attempt — *(manual)*.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Rule ids are `LY-n`; formulas are `D.n` (Formulas section); acceptance criteria are `AC-n`. Every table below carries an **Owner** column or states its owner in the lead-in: Layout's own tables hold only identity, tiles, rectangles, sockets, hardpoints, slots, anchors, membership, offsets, welds and counters. Locked constants and formulas from the kernel, the Graph, the Controller and the Pipeline are used by name with their registry values and are never restated differently (a needed different value would be a CONFLICT line in Open Questions, never a new number). Provisional contracts with undesigned or in-flight systems are marked **(provisional — <system> undesigned)**. Conflicts, clarifications and asks are marked **CONFLICT**, **CLARIFY** and **ASK**; they are routed in Cross-References and Open Questions, never silently resolved in a neighbour's text.

#### 1.1 Block, ownership and data kinds

**LY-1 — One block, one ownership clause.** Layout registers exactly one state block, `layout.state` (registry: `layout_state_block`), in the kernel's block registry (CR-8):

| Field | Value | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| `block_id` | `layout.state` | Layout |
| `owner` | Layout | Layout |
| `class` | `SIM` | Layout |
| `persist` / `persist_scope` | true / `WORLD` | Layout |
| `scope` | `SHARED` | Layout |
| `repl_hint` | `SHARED_SUMMARY` — the summary IS the instance table plus the `geometry_hash` and `visual_hash` values *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the single `template_hash` is split, LY-26 e)*; geometry never travels, because templates are identical on every client by catalogue version | Layout |
| `schema_version` | `LAYOUT_BLOCK_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 | Layout |
| `restore_after` | `[]` — Layout is the root of the restore order; it reads no other block in `restore()` | Layout |
| Hooks | exactly one: `layout.doors` (LY-10) | Layout |
| Callbacks | `snapshot()`, `restore(data, stored_version)`, `default_state()`, `post_restore()`, `read_view()` (kernel CR-8 descriptor) | Layout |

The ownership clause routed from the Graph (LS-6, LSG:74; LSG:1158 Open Question 9 requires it "repeated verbatim in the Layout and Interaction GDDs") is, word for word: "**Layout owns** room identity, polygon, `V` (tiles) and `exposed_tiles`; the Graph caches `V` and `exposed_tiles` as derived fields (not hashed; rebuilt in `post_restore`, which is why `restore_after: [layout.state]`). **The Graph owns** the atmosphere — `n_o2`, `n_inert`, `T`, `t_rem`, `fuel_puddle_ml` — and every opening/hatch `area` and `door_state`, every VALVE `open` and every breaker `closed`; Interaction issues the commands, the Graph publishes the mutators (R11; one writer per fact, kernel H3)." Layout's side of the same clause: **sections own compartment/room GEOMETRY and the volume `V` in tiles; the Graph owns atmosphere and node/edge state; Layout only PROJECTS `area` into a rectangle (LY-9).** The Graph's block `graph.state` is registered with `restore_after: [layout.state]` (LSG:64, LSG:1023) and the Emitter's `emitter.state` with `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` (EM:104); this rule is the other end of both edges. Entity ids are allocated by the owning block from a counter stored in it (CR-8): Layout owns the section, body and room counters (LY-6).

**LY-2 — Templates are static; instances are state.** A **template** is authored, immutable, integer-only data: rooms, wall runs, kit-piece placements, doors, sockets, hardpoints, plate positions, loose and story slots, anchors and fixture anchors (LY-4). It lives under `assets/data/sections/`, is loaded and range-checked in BOOTING (every JSON number is converted with an explicit integer cast and bound check, because the engine's JSON parser returns floats [UNV] (NOT SOURCEABLE: `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` has no scripting or core file) — the same defensive pattern the Graph and the Item DB use), and is **never in a snapshot**. An **instance** is a row in `layout.state` that names a template. The block therefore stores instances *by reference*: full geometry in the block would bloat it (rectangles × sections), duplicate authored data, defeat the Vertical Slice "seed plus deltas" model, and let an exporter bug corrupt the world permanently; a second `layout.templates` block would add a second restore root for no gain. Both are rejected. (→ ADR-LAY-01.) A template edit between builds is handled like an Item DB `def_id` change: by `geometry_hash` / `visual_hash` and the drift policy of LY-26 e. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Catalogue loading in an export build** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: in a Godot export, non-resource files such as `assets/data/**/*.json` are dropped unless the export filters list them, and a `DirAccess` scan returns entries in a platform-dependent order, so a directory-scanned catalogue can pass in the editor and fail (or reorder) in a release build. The catalogue is therefore built from a **committed manifest** (`assets/data/sections/manifest.json`, one `template_id` and path per line) **loaded in sorted `template_id` order**, never from a directory scan; the four MVP template paths (`home_ship`, `derelict_a`, `derelict_b`, `pod`) and `assets/data/world/mvp_world.json` are added to the export filter (`include_filter` for `*.json`); and an **exported-build smoke check** loads all four MVP templates from the exported package and compares the resulting `geometry_hash` / `visual_hash` with the editor run (AC-178).

| Data kind | Lives in | Written by | Persisted | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Template (rooms, tile sets, rect table, doors, sockets, hardpoints, plates, slots, anchors) | `assets/data/sections/*.json`, loaded at BOOTING | authors and the offline tool; never at runtime | no | Layout (schema, structural lint); Derelict Authoring (authored files, content lint) |
| Bodies table: `{body_id, origin_section_id, state LIVE\|DISSOLVED}` | `layout.state` | LM1-LM4 | yes | Layout |
| Section-instance table: `{section_id, template_id, geometry_hash, visual_hash, body_id, offset_px (two ints), rot_q (0), room_id_base, graph_ref_base {node_base, edge_base, comp_base}, discarded}` (13 ints per row, D.21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `template_hash` split into the two u32 hashes of LY-26 e)* | `layout.state` | LM1, LM4 | yes | Layout |
| Socket-state table (sparse, non-`FREE` only): `{section_id, socket_index, state, seam_id}` | `layout.state` | LM2, LM3 | yes | Layout |
| Weld history `welds[]`: `{seam_id, section_a, socket_a, section_b, socket_b, welded_at_T, state ACTIVE\|CUT_SEALED\|CUT_RAGGED, cut_at_T}` | `layout.state` | LM2, LM3 | yes | Layout (the `seam_id` is the Graph's; Layout allocates none) |
| Counters `next_section_id`, `next_body_id`, `next_room_id` | `layout.state` | LM1 | yes | Layout |
| Derived caches: broadphase grid, door-leaf rects, variant-slot activity, `version` / `static_version` / `leaf_version(section_id)`, `graph_ref` lookup maps, `opening_leaves` (edge id → leaf slots) | RAM | LM1-LM4, `layout.doors`, `restore` | **never** | Layout (PROJECTOR objects: the PROJECTOR) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**LY-3 — Units, grid, frames and transforms (stated once).**
(a) *Integers only.* The unit is the base pixel; `TILE_PX` 48 is the floor tile; the half-tile grid is 24 px (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 is the wall depth). No float exists anywhere in the module. Every footprint coordinate lies on the 24-px grid, with the single exception of the moving edge of a DOOR_LEAF rectangle (LY-7).
(b) *Frames.* A **section-local** frame has its origin at the section's grid origin; local coordinates are `>= 0` by authoring. The **body frame** is the section-local frame plus `offset_px(section_id)`. Layout has no world frame (Body's pose and the Pipeline's camera are not Layout's).
(c) *One rule for which frame a read answers in.* Every Layout geometry read returns SECTION-LOCAL base px (fixture anchors EM:331; `anchor_geometry.base_px_polyline[]` and `room_surface` FT:306; hardpoint, slot and socket anchors); the caller composes `section_offset(section_id)` (FT:306 "the section's body-frame offset"). The reads that answer for one body answer in the BODY frame: `OccluderSource.rects_in` and `solid_in`, because the sweep (PCI:103, "`pos` base px, body frame") and the occlusion test (EM-30, EM:253) run there, and the body-frame helpers built on them or on the offsets — `clip_to_footprints`, `leaf_would_overlap`, `section_at`, `weld_offset` and `detach_preview` (LY-28).
(d) *Transforms are integer translation only.* A section's `offset_px` lies on the 24-px grid (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH`) — a DERIVED property of D.6 and D.12 over `GRID_24`-linted inputs, never an input constraint; a reserved `rot_q` field is stored and **must be 0** (the template lint and `restore` reject any other value with `LAYOUT_ROT_UNSUPPORTED`); there is no mirror. Variety comes from authored socket FACINGS (LY-13), not from rotating a section. Rejected: four-way rotation (the kit's wall faces are authored for the up-screen convention only, PIPE:794; the art bible's "× 2 orientations" is pipes only) and free transform (the anti-pillar). A detached section "translates without rotation in the interior view" (PIPE:694).
(e) *Zoom invariance.* No `k`, `s`, `field_h` or zoom value appears anywhere in Layout (FT-34): every length, clearance and rectangle is base px and identical at every ladder step, so there is nothing to re-check when the zoom ladder changes (the reflexion-log pattern "an invariant written against 'the default' must be re-checked whenever a later decision adds reachable states" is satisfied by construction).
(f) *Floor division.* Body-frame coordinates and `offset_px` may be negative after a detach re-base, so every px-to-tile conversion is FLOOR division (D.1); truncating integer division is forbidden.

**LY-4 — Template schema (field by field, mapped to the art-bible kit).** `SECTION_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1. The art bible's room kit (6.3 "The room kit: what one kit must contain") is the authoring input: Derelict Authoring and the Vertical Slice procedural generator consume this schema (LSG, IDX:28).

| Record | Fields | Kit piece / source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Header | `template_id` (snake_case, unique), `schema`, `dialect` (`foundry`/`lattice`/`civil`; every MVP template is `foundry`, IDB), `kind_default`, `size_tiles {w, h}`, `entry_room_key` (the room the pod arrives in, one meaning across all templates: the pod-dock room `airlock` for the home ship, `a_dock` for derelict A, `b_dock` for derelict B; registry `mvp_template_roster`) | dialect set, art bible 3.3 | `w <= SECTION_MAX_TILES_W`, `h <= SECTION_MAX_TILES_H`; `kind_default` is a convenience, the build call passes `kind` explicitly |
| Room | `room_key` (snake_case, unique in the template), `room_type` (lint hint), `outer` (bool), `frame_exception` (bool), `origin_px {x, y}` (on the 24 grid), `tile_runs[] {ty, tx0, tx1}` (inclusive row spans in 48-px tile units from `origin_px`) | floor (base, variant, threshold) | expanded to the sorted tile list (LY-5) |
| Wall run | `run_id` (template-local int), `orientation EW\|NS`, `start_px {x, y}` (24 grid), `len_tiles`, `kind HULL\|PARTITION`, `room_key_a`, `room_key_b` (or `EXTERIOR`), `face_h_px` (default 72, art bible 8.2 front face), `face_variant FACE\|CAP_ONLY` (default `FACE`; a HULL run on a room's south edge must be `CAP_ONLY`, D.14) | wall straight face, inner/outer corner, end cap, top cap | footprint = the 24-px strip (LY-8); HULL runs feed `exposed_tiles` (counted on the authored run, before the socket-span split of LY-15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Kit placement | `kit_piece_id`, `px` or tile, `layer`, `footprint {x0, y0, x1, y1}` (copied from the kit piece), `flags` | every placed piece | the authored footprint rect travels with the kit piece; Layout lints alignment |
| Rect | `idx` (template-local, dense from 0), `x0, y0, x1, y1` (half-open), `flags` (LY-7), `owner_kind`, `owner_ref` | derived from wall runs and kit placements at template load | the stored table is the compiled result; `idx` is the stable rect id (D.9). The compiled `idx` space has three parts: static rects (sorted), then one reserved leaf slot per door, then two reserved variant slots (PLATE, JAMB) per hatched socket — the HULL run containing a socket span is split into before / span / after and the span is owned by the socket's variant slots (LY-15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Door | `door_key`, `wall_run_id`, `doorway_start_px` (for a socket hatch derived: it equals the socket's `start_px`, low-aligned, LY-14 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), `width_px` (96, art bible 8.2 door cell), `hinge LOW\|HIGH` (interior doors only; a socket hatch's hinge is fixed by LY-9), `sill_room_key`, `edge_ref` (the Graph template OPENING) or `hatch_socket_key`, `area_max`, `door_motor_node_ref` (IDB Open Question 2), `leaf_rect_idx` | door frame + leaf (closed / open / cut / welded, art bible 6.5) | the leaf rect index is reserved even while the leaf is absent (D.9) |
| Socket | `socket_key`, `facing N\|E\|S\|W`, `line_px` (outer face of the hull wall), `start_px` (along the run), `width_tiles` (1-12), `class_id`, `lanes[] {loop, lane_index, node_ref, port_local_index}`, `hatch {door_key}` or none | port shape inherits the donor dialect (art bible 3.3) | LY-13 |
| Hardpoint | `hp_key`, `node_ref`, `room_key`, `accepted_kinds[]`, `ports[]` (ordered port-type ids, `<= MAX_PORTS_PER_DEF` 8), `accepted_subtypes[]`, `cell`, `anchor_px`, `mount WALL_FACE\|FLOOR`, `host_face` or floor tile, `footprint_rect_idx`, `socket_tag` (advisory) | machinery node (96×96) incl. the hull-plate module position | LY-20 |
| Plate position | `segment_key`, `node_ref`, `wall_run_id`, `start_tile`, `len_tiles`, `bound_room_key` | hull plate module | LY-21 |
| Loose slot | `slot_id`, `room_key`, `anchor_px`, `surface FLOOR\|SHELF\|CRATE`, `allowed_class[]`, `capacity` 1 | props 8-10 | LY-22 |
| Story slot | `slot_id`, `role CAUSE\|VERB\|ABSENCE`, `room_key`, `anchor_px` | storytelling set (Vertical Slice; art bible 6.5 ("one cause, one verb, one absence per wreck", placed as three tagged slots)) | list reserved, empty in MVP |
| Anchor | `anchor_key`, `ref {node_ref, port_local_index} \| {edge_ref}`, `kind WALL_FACE\|FLOOR`, `host_face`, `polyline[] {x, y}`, `max_sev_class` (input from the Graph descriptor data; Layout never reads severity) | pipe mount, light fixture | LY-23 |
| Fixture anchor | `local_id`, `kind`, `anchor_px`, `shape_id`, `base_pm`, `node_ref`, `hue_class` (the EM:328 placement record) | light fixture | `shape_id`, `base_pm`, `hue_class` come from the installed def's `emits` (IDB); Layout carries the placement |
| Instrument placement *(provisional — routed from In-World Instruments IW-31)* | `instrument_id` (a key into `assets/data/instruments/`), `node_ref` or `room_key` plus `quantity` (the Graph binding, carried opaquely: Layout never reads a quantity), `mount WALL_FACE\|MACHINE_PANEL`, `host_face`, `anchor_px` | a `Kit placement` of category `mach` or `prop` (gauge, dial, sight-glass) | exposed through `subject_anchors(section_id)` with `kind_hint` `INSTRUMENT` (LY-23) so Interaction registers the subject; `INSTRUMENT_NO_BINDING`, the at-least-3-eligible-surfaces floor and the DIAL_L placement constraint (46 of 48 rows: never a console-face detail inside a cap-plus-face prop) are Derelict Authoring's CONTENT lint, not Layout's |

Schema ownership: **Layout owns the schema and the structural lint (LY-30); Derelict Authoring owns the authored files, the content and visual-rule lint, and the kit pieces** *(provisional — Derelict Authoring undesigned)*.
*The `pod` template (answers Derelict Authoring OQ-DA-17 and Web Map ASK-L1 for Layout's part).* `pod_cabin` (one room, 2 × 2 tiles, `V` 4 — exactly `ROOM_TILES_MIN`, inclusive, so `V_RANGE` passes) is an ordinary template under schema v1: Layout adds no pod record type and persists no section kind; the `kind` argument forwarded to M1 comes from the Graph's LS-17 enum, and which value the pod receives is the Graph's and Web Map's call (OQ-26). A template with exactly one room is exempt from `ROOM_NO_EXIT` and `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED` (its one room is its `entry_room_key`, and the pod is entered and left by Web Map's pod hop, never by walking through a door); `ROOM_UNREACHABLE` passes trivially. Layout's structural lint has no fixture minimum — `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM` is Authoring's CONTENT lint (LY-30) — so the `fixtures_min` exemption is Authoring's to write and Layout grants nothing. A pod room that hosts no HULL_SEGMENT is not tagged `outer`, so `OUTER_ROOM_HULL_RUNS` does not apply.

**LY-5 — A room is a rectilinear, 4-connected set of floor tiles on the 24-px lattice.** `room.tiles[]` is the list of floor tiles of one room, each a 48×48 square whose section-local origin is `origin_px + 48 × (tx, ty)`, sorted row-major (`ty` ascending, then `tx` ascending). A tile's **tile id** is its index in that list. `V = |tiles|` (D.3). A room is 4-connected (neighbours differ by exactly one in `tx` or `ty`), has no interior holes and no diagonal pinch vertex (lint `ROOM_SHAPE`), has `V` within the Graph's compartment bounds (`ROOM_TILES_MIN`..`ROOM_TILES_MAX`, 4-200, LSG:299 — cited, not redefined) and belongs to exactly one section (LSG:172 "a compartment belongs to one section"). L-shapes are allowed; two rooms of a section never share a tile. The "polygon" the Graph names (LSG:74, LSG:849) is DERIVED: `room_boundary(room_id)` returns the closed boundary polyline (unit edges traced clockwise from the north-west corner of the minimum-id tile), never stored. `room_surface(compartment_id).floor_tiles[]` is literally the sorted tile list in section-local px (tile origin), so the Failure & Trail ordering "(distance from the anchor, tile id)" (FT:383) has a defined tie-break.
*(orchestrator ruling: the floor-tile lattice.)* A wall between two rooms is ONE 24-px footprint strip (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24; Pipeline D.12's own example — wall rows `[240, 264)`, first legal row 264 — presumes exactly this), so the floor on its far side begins 24 px later than a 48-px lattice would put it: **tile parity flips across every shared wall**. Each room therefore carries its own `origin_px` on the 24-px grid and its tiles are counted in 48-px units from that origin. This is a storage-unit refinement of "tiles on the 48-px grid", not a new tile size: `TILE_PX` stays 48, `V` is unchanged, and the alternatives are rejected — a double wall between rooms (a 48-px-thick shared wall needs two door frames in line and two Graph openings per passage, or a 48-deep frame the art bible does not define) and a full-48 wall row with a 24-px walkable skirt owned by no tile. The **sill** — the door-width × 24-px floor strip inside a doorway — is not a tile and does not count in `V`: each door declares a `sill_room` (one of its two flanking rooms) and `room_at(section_id, px)` answers `sill_room` for a px on the sill, so there is no walkable point in a section where `room_at` answers `NONE`.

**LY-6 — Identity, allocation and the migration stance.**
(a) *Ids.* Layout allocates `section_id` from `next_section_id`, `body_id` from `next_body_id` and `room_id` (a u32) from `next_room_id` **in template room order at `build_section`**, contiguous per instance (`room_id = room_id_base + room_index`). Ids are never reused and never renumbered (the Graph's append-only pattern, LS-15). A discarded section keeps its row as a tombstone (LY-19), so its ids stay consumed.
(b) *Keys.* Template-local keys (`room_key`, `socket_key`, `hp_key`, `door_key`, `node_ref`, `edge_ref`) are strings used for lint, diagnostics, migration and restore verification. The block persists only numeric ids, socket ordinals and the `template_id` string.
(c) *Graph ids.* The Graph allocates node, edge, compartment and seam ids from its own `next_*` counters in template order (LSG:170, LSG:180). Layout persists, per instance, `graph_ref_base {node_base, edge_base, comp_base}` so a template-local `node_ref` / `edge_ref` index maps to a numeric Graph id by `base + index` and a compartment id maps to its room by `comp_base`, deterministically. The seam id in `welds[]` is the Graph's `seam_id` (LSG:72 SeamRecord); Layout allocates no seam id.
(d) **ANSWERED by the Graph (LS-15, LS-19, M1 row; *Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass*); the ask is closed (OQ-4).** M1 `instantiate_section(section_id, template_id, kind)` takes a caller section id and now returns `{node_base, edge_base, comp_base}`, which Layout stores as `graph_ref_base`; M1 advances the Graph's persisted `next_section_id` to `max(section_id) + 1`, so Layout allocates the section id and the Graph's counter follows it. `weld_join` returns `{seam_id, opening_ids[]}` and `seam_view(seam_id)` is a pure read of `{state, sub_edges[], leak_bp, opening_ids[]}`; Layout uses `opening_ids[]` for the mated-hatch leaf map (LY-11, LY-16). The only mismatch case left is the restore-time guard of Edge Cases ("Save, restore and drift"). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
(e) *Migration stance, stated here and again in Open Questions:* "A renamed `room_key` or `template_id` needs a migration step in the owner once the first build has been given outside the project (SL-14, SL:219); before that, saves are disposable (SL-14)." This closes Save/Load Open Question 12 (SL:1300) and the Graph's Open Question 9 (LSG:1158) from the Layout side. Because the persisted ids are numeric and assigned at build, a template that merely *reorders* rooms changes ids only for NEW instances; existing saves keep theirs.

#### 1.2 Rectangles, walls, frames

**LY-7 — The footprint rectangle record and flags** (registry: `layout_rect_flags`)**.** A rect is `(x0, y0, x1, y1)` in section-local base px, stored as packed integer arrays plus one flags byte, **half-open** `[x0, x1) × [y0, y1)` over pixel CELLS (the tile grid, the foot box and sprite rows are all cells). Flags: `SOLID` (blocks `LocomotionStep`), `OCCLUDES_LIGHT` (the Emitter's `occludes_light`, EM-20), `DOOR_LEAF`, `PROP`, `MACHINE`, `HULL`. Rects are SIMULATION DATA (kernel H10): never read back from the scene tree, never produced by an engine physics shape, and the avatar has no physics body. Layout never calls its own rects "collision shapes" (wording refresh routed in Cross-References). The only legal flag combinations (anything else is the lint error `RECT_FLAGS_ILLEGAL`):

| Class | SOLID | OCCLUDES_LIGHT | DOOR_LEAF | PROP | MACHINE | HULL | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wall run | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0/1 | HULL on exterior-facing runs |
| Door jamb | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | fixed, either side of the gap |
| Door leaf | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | always solid and occluding (no glass in MVP, EM-20); dynamic (LY-9) |
| Socket hatch plate (variant slot PLATE) while `FREE`, `CAPPED`, `CUT_OPEN` or `WELDED` one-sided | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | the plate covers the whole socket span (`width_tiles × 48` × 24); a reserved variant slot, not a static rect (LY-15). While `CUT_OPEN` it is solid AND occluding: nothing lies beyond a cut, and no avatar can leave the hull through it. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Socket jamb (variant slot JAMB), `WELDED` two-sided, `width_tiles >= 3` only | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | the hull bulkhead beside the 96-px hatch doorway, `(width_tiles × 48 − 96)` × 24; exterior hull wall, hence HULL; zero-width (omitted) at `width_tiles` 2. Same precedent as the hull-port row below: the HULL run compiles in parts with no SOLID overlap. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Tall prop / machinery node | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0/1 | 0 | `occludes_light` authored |
| Low prop | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | blocks feet, not light |
| Hull port (`wall_port` piece in a HULL run; routed from Derelict Authoring OQ-DA-19 / OQ-V2) | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | blocks feet, passes light, so a residual lamp's light truthfully leaves the hull; its footprint replaces the HULL run's own over its span (the run compiles in two parts, no SOLID overlap) and `CONTAINMENT_LEAK` is unaffected because it is SOLID; it is absent from `rects_in`, not a translucent occluder, so EM-20 (f) is untouched |

Two query filters, never merged: `solid_in(body_id, aabb)` returns SOLID rects (Body's sweep); `OccluderSource.rects_in(aabb)` returns OCCLUDES_LIGHT rects (Emitter, Lighting). A rect with `x1 <= x0` or `y1 <= y0` is **omitted from every result** but keeps its reserved `idx`, so a fully open leaf renumbers nothing; the omission itself bumps `leaf_version` (and so `version`, LY-12 d). The same omission rule governs the socket variant slots (PLATE, JAMB; LY-15): a slot that is inactive in the socket's current state, or whose computed length is zero, is omitted from every result and keeps its reserved `idx`, so a weld, a cut or a restore never adds or removes a key from the reserved set `(section_id, idx)` — only the active subset changes. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(CLARIFY, orchestrator ruling: the DOOR_LEAF exemption.)* The 24-grid rule has one exception: a leaf's fixed axes lie on the 24 grid but its moving edge takes any integer in `[hinge, hinge + width_px]` (LY-9). The broadphase (LY-12) therefore stores static rects only; leaves (and the socket variant slots, which change only inside LM2 and LM3 and are rebuilt into the grid there) live in a small per-section dynamic list tested with a bounding-box prefilter, because re-inserting a moving rect into a uniform grid every tick costs more than testing a handful of boxes.

**LY-8 — Walls, corners, canonical decomposition, corridors, doorways, floor runs.**
(a) *Depth.* A wall footprint is `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 thick normal to its run in BOTH orientations (an E-W wall: 24 rows deep, the run wide; a N-S wall: 24 columns wide, the run tall). Corner cells belong to the E-W run; N-S runs are trimmed to exclude the 24×24 corner, so the compiled table has no overlapping duplicates ("corners are the union").
(b) *Which half-tile.* Which half-tile of a 48×96 kit piece the footprint occupies is the kit piece's authored rect, not a Layout formula (PIPE:794 routes the projection convention to the art-director); Layout lints alignment (every coordinate on the 24 grid) and derives `sort_origin_y = y_0 + 24` per Pipeline D.12 (cited, not restated). The first legal row south of a wall with rows `[y_0, y_0 + 24)` is `y_0 + 24` (the rect is occupied through `y_0 + 23`, LY-12).
(c) *Canonical order.* Within a template, `idx` is assigned by sorting all rects by `(y0, x0, y1, x1, flags)` at load; no two SOLID rects of one template may overlap (lint `RECT_OVERLAP`; abutting is legal). Door-leaf rects are indexed by their door's declaration order and keep that index while their coordinates change. Consequence: the key `(section_id, idx)` is stable across attach, detach and animation.
(d) *Corridor lint.* An anchor whose descriptor can reach severity 4 needs 144 px of clearance at its head (FT-37, ruling F-3, FT:211); narrower is a Layout CONTENT ERROR reported by the structural lint (`CORRIDOR_HEAD_NARROW`), never a runtime fallback and never a shortened trail. The measure is the free interval, normal to the final run segment, through each sample point of the floor run (D.8). *(CLARIFY, orchestrator ruling: interval reading.)* FT-37 does not say whether the 144-px square is centred on the run line or merely contained in a free interval; Layout adopts the **interval** reading, because FT's own worked example (the cryo-bay leak, FT:377, whose run lies 24 px from the north wall) would fail the centred reading. One-line confirmation routed to the Failure & Trail owner. A corridor hosting no sev-4-capable anchor may be one tile wide (the art bible's rule 8 needs only a clear path ≥ 1 tile).
(e) *Doorway rule (answers FT:705).* A wall fitting's floor run is authored as `{start_px, dir}` and clipped at authoring to its own room's tile set plus, if the room is the door's `sill_room`, that door's sill; a run reaching a doorway or room boundary STOPS AT THE SILL in MVP. A run that would cross is the lint error `FLOOR_RUN_CROSSES_BOUNDARY`, never a runtime clip; `anchor_geometry` never returns a polyline that crosses a wall line. Layout confirms Failure & Trail's provisional reading as the rule. *Cross-room edges (answers Derelict Authoring OQ-DA-5):* an anchor polyline never penetrates a wall — a PIPE or CABLE edge that crosses a partition (`e_trunk`, the coolant trunk) keeps exactly one anchor, authored in one of the rooms it serves (Authoring picks the leak site), and its polyline stays inside that room and ends at the wall face; the run through the partition is kit-piece presentation with no polyline and no rect beyond the wall's own.
(f) *Door assembly.* A door's assembly is its frame (`width_px`) plus its pocket (`width_px`, on the hinge side) along the run, and must be straight wall: no corner, no other assembly, no socket frame, no MACHINE footprint, no wall-mounted prop (lint `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR`; the non-hinge side needs `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX` of straight wall before a corner). Each face of a door needs a free 48×48 approach square inside its owning room so that both face-subject anchors are reachable (lint `DOOR_APPROACH_CLEAR`). The pipe band (art bible 6.3 rule 7) applies to PROP rects only; doors, machinery nodes, hardpoints and seam beads sit in or on the wall line and are exempt.
(g) *No rotated kit.* Wall faces project up-screen only (PIPE:794); there is no rotated or mirrored kit (LY-3 d).

**LY-9 — The door leaf is ONE sliding rectangle derived from the Graph's `area`.** For a door or a mated hatch with doorway width `width_px` (96 in every dialect — the 96×96 door cell, art bible 8.2) and the Graph opening's `area` and the template's `area_max`: `leaf_w = width_px − floor(width_px × area / area_max)` (D.5). The leaf is a rect of depth `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 along the doorway, `leaf_w` long, anchored to the **hinge side**; it retracts into the wall pocket on that side (for an interior door the pocket is part of the adjacent wall run, so no extra rect; for a socket hatch the pocket is presentation only and exempt from `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR`, LY-15 e *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*). `area = 0` → `leaf_w = width_px` (the leaf covers the doorway); `area = area_max` → `leaf_w = 0` (no rect is returned). Flags `SOLID | OCCLUDES_LIGHT | DOOR_LEAF`. The same rect is the collision input (PCI:103), the occluder rect (EM-20 d) and the drawn leaf's extent (the PROJECTOR sizes the sprite from it), so **collision and light cannot disagree** (Pillar 1). In the MVP the Graph's `area` jumps 0 ↔ `area_max` in one tick (IA-24), so the leaf is binary by consequence and no text may promise a sliding leaf (LY-11, Option A); the formula has no threshold, so `DOOR_LIGHT_OPEN_PM` (EM:788-789, "only if the leaf is binary") is **RETIRED by this GDD** — a registry supersede (`status: deprecated`, superseded-by D.5), never a silent delete (R23 handling in Cross-References). The gap a body passes through is `width_px − leaf_w = floor(width_px × area / area_max)`; a foot box passes iff the gap is at least the box's extent across the doorway — Body supplies the box as an argument (LY-11), and for the MVP foot box of 16×8 (PCI:103) that is 16 px across a doorway in an E-W wall and 8 px across one in a N-S wall (D.5, `area_pass_min`).
*Hinge rule for mated hatches (CLARIFY, adopted).* Two Layout leaves (one per section) project one mated hatch's single `area` (LY-15). If their pockets lay on different sides the one `area` would produce two different gaps and one leaf would cover the other's opening — two truths from one value, a Pillar 1 break. A socket hatch's pocket side is therefore **fixed by the socket's axis, not authored**: the LOW-coordinate side (north for E/W-facing sockets, west for N/S-facing sockets); a hatch that declares a hinge is the lint error `HATCH_HINGE_AUTHORED`. The rule is **sound only because welds are pure translation (LY-3 d)**: both sections then share one axis orientation, so "LOW side" is the same side in both frames. Interior doors keep an authored `hinge LOW|HIGH`.
*Unit note (C-6, not resolved here).* The Graph states `area` in 1/64 tile with `A_HOLE_MAX` 64 "one door's worth" (LSG:72, LSG:323), while a door is two tiles wide. D.5 is unit-free (a ratio `area / area_max`); each door and hatch template declares its own `area_max`, a lint checks it against the door width, and the Graph owner is asked to state what 1/64 tile means for a door two tiles wide. **ASK (Graph owner).**

**LY-10 — `layout.doors`: Layout's only hook, and the P2/P3 correctness condition.** Registration (CR-3): `hook_id layout.doors`, owner Layout, divisor 1, offset 0, `after: []`. It runs in P3 and, for every loaded door and hatch, re-derives the leaf rect from the opening's `area` — read through the Graph's published `opening(edge_id) -> {area, area_max, door_state}` (LS-19 additions, *Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass*; the ask this GDD made is **answered and closed**, OQ-3) — updates the dynamic leaf list and bumps `leaf_version(section_id)` (and so `version`, LY-12 d; never `static_version`) once per section whose leaf set changed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (changed = any `leaf_w` differs from the last projected value; the last-projected cache is derived, not persisted). Cost: a loop over at most `MAX_DOORS_LOADED` doors, an integer multiply and divide each (≈ 20 in MVP). **Correctness condition, stated as a rule and tested: the `area` and `door_state` of every OPENING Layout PROJECTS — template doors and mated hatches — change only in P2** — M13 `set_opening_area` is a command-backed Class A mutator (LSG:192) and commands apply in P2 (CR-2) — so a P3 hook sees same-tick state (the mirror of Controller PC-18 test 7: a closed leaf blocks and an open one does not in the SAME tick its state changes). **Virtual openings have no leaf:** the Graph derives breach holes and seam weeps as VIRTUAL OPENINGs on every run of its failure hook in P3 (LS-5, LSG:72), so "area changes only in P2" is false for them, and Layout neither projects nor reads them (their gas effect is the Graph's; their trail is Failure & Trail's). Stated consequences: (i) the `CLOSING_BLOCKED` retry (PCI:650, "retries on each of its hook runs") must be a SYSTEM-origin command processed at T+1 (CR-9 b), never M13 called from a P3 hook — adopted by Interaction (IA-24, registry `door_attempt`); (ii) **confirmed by the Graph owner (scoped identically; the Graph's Interactions row for Layout states "no Graph phase writes `area` or `door_state`", *Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass*)** that no Graph phase writes `area` or `door_state` of a template door or a mated hatch; (iii) **resolved (Ship Body BDY-8, registry `body_state_block`):** `body.integrate` is registered `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]` (Controller PC-14 and PC-15 read the same) — because a registration-order tie-break would work by accident and CR-3 forbids relying on it.
**RESOLVED (Ship Body BDY-8, registry `body_state_block`):** `body.integrate` is registered by its owner, Ship Body, as `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]`, and Controller PC-14 / PC-15 read the same. The Emitter needs no edit for correctness: EM-1 registers `emitter.place` as `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]` (EM:104, EM:321), and CR-3 resolves a topological order over `after` transitively, so once `body.integrate` is `after: layout.doors`, `emitter.place` is ordered after `layout.doors` already. An explicit `layout.doors` entry in EM-1's list is **optional, explicit** (EM-23 at EM:227 may add it for readability).

**RESOLVED (Interaction IA-24, registry `door_attempt`): the attempt completion and the `CLOSING_BLOCKED` retry are SYSTEM-origin commands applied in P2 of T+1 with `leaf_would_overlap` called from validate (LY-10 i adopted); no M13 call from a P3 hook.** `interaction.step` never calls M13 (IA-24 c/d, IA D.12): at `T == Tb + DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` it submits a SYSTEM `set_opening {target_ref, area}` command, and a door in `CLOSING_BLOCKED` has its command re-submitted each tick by Interaction's `attempts[]`, so every `area` write of a template door or mated hatch lands in P2 and the same-tick guarantee of this rule holds (`layout.doors after: [interaction.step]` would be a cycle and is not needed). OQ-14 (a) is closed. *Historical fallback, no longer needed (the amendment landed via Ship Body BDY-8; recorded in Open Questions OQ-6):* lazy derive on read, stamped by the Graph's write state rather than by tick, with no hook and no amendment — its defects are that a read mutates a derived cache and that a refresh in P2 can precede a later same-P2 change to `area`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**LY-11 — Door-close safety.** The invariant "a footprint rect may never be enabled while it overlaps an actor's foot box" (PCI:650) is Layout rule text, enforced as follows. Layout publishes the PURE query `leaf_would_overlap(opening_ref, area, foot_boxes[]) -> bool` (D.5), whose first argument is the **opening**, not one section's door *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a mated hatch is two leaf rects (one per section) driven by one `area` (LY-15), and a 16-px foot box straddling the seam can overlap only the far section's leaf, so a check validated against one section's `door_ref` would pass while the other leaf grows over the body (the PCI:650 invariant broken on the MVP's only hatch). Layout therefore evaluates **every leaf projected from that opening, on both sections, against every foot box, and returns true if any overlaps**. **Foot boxes are arguments, supplied by the caller from Body's read view as opaque half-open rectangles**; Layout never reads `body.state` (acyclic restore) and does not define the foot box — Body fixes it (BDY-14, registry `foot_box`: the foot origin is the box's TOP row, box `[pos.x − 8, pos.x + 8) × [pos.y, pos.y + 8)`), and Layout's guarantee stays stated on rectangles and caller boxes. The check runs for the NEW `area` of **every write that raises `leaf_w`** (a close): under the MVP's binary door model (below) that is the one jump to `area` 0 and each T+1 retry of `CLOSING_BLOCKED`; were a stepped slide ever introduced (Option B, OQ-14 c) it would run on every step, because the leaf grows each step. A body standing in the doorway therefore halts the closure at the first overlapping write and nothing is displaced. Layout returns the bool only; the reason code is the verb owner's, Interaction's: a PLAYER-origin close is rejected `OBSTRUCTED` (IA-24; PCI:650 left the code to Layout and Interaction to confirm, and its `BLOCKED` wording is a refresh routed to the Controller owner, Cross-References B). **Retry owner:** Interaction's `attempts[]` holds the `CLOSING_BLOCKED` retry for both origins (seat 0 for a SYSTEM-origin close). **Travel — the MVP door model is BINARY (Option A, decided in the design review).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Layout owns no door clock: D.5 is evaluated on whatever `area` the Graph holds each tick. IA-24 and the registry `door_attempt` fix the MVP behaviour: a `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` (30) attempt window in which the leaf does not move, then one M13 jump of `area` 0 ↔ `area_max`. The leaf is therefore closed in one tick and open in the next, and **no player-facing text promises a leaf that slides**. Layout adds no latency of its own (the leaf is projected in the same tick's P3, LY-10), so the leaf's travel time is exactly one tick and the cross-system invariant `TAP_REARM_TICKS` (12) `>=` that travel time (Interaction I-8) holds trivially. **The sprite equals the rect (binding on the PROJECTOR):** the drawn leaf is sized from the Layout leaf rect of the same tick and **no tween, ease or interpolation may give the leaf sprite an extent different from the rect** — a "slide" added later for polish would show a leaf where the rect, the foot and the lamp beam say there is none, a Pillar 1 break. Motion that does not change the leaf's extent (the motor whine and its sound during the attempt window, an emissive frame sprite with **no `Light2D`**, which AC-122 forbids; *amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was 'a light on the frame'*) is Adaptive Audio's and the PROJECTOR's to add freely. A stepped slide (Option B) is a Vertical Slice option only: it would name its owner (Graph: a motor rate on M13; or Interaction: stepped commands), bind that owner to the `TAP_REARM_TICKS` invariant above, and re-open the per-step close check (OQ-14 c); until then Layout states the invariant and owns no value in it. On SYSTEM origin Interaction enters `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and retries at T+1 (LY-10 i), `area` stays at its last non-overlapping value, and the body is never displaced. Opening (raising `area`) is never blocked by Layout. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The obstructed cue (rect-honest, geometric):** a blocked close and an unpowered door are otherwise both just a motor whine, which would break the Game Feel promise that the "why" is always a geometric fact. Layout therefore also publishes the pure read `leaf_obstruction(opening_ref, area, foot_boxes[]) -> {box_index, leaf_slot}` (the first overlapping box in caller order and the leaf it overlaps, or `NONE`), so the verb owner and the PROJECTOR can point the cue at the obstruction itself — a stalled leaf edge and the blocking body — which an unpowered door can never produce, because an unpowered door never reaches the overlap test (IA-24: it resolves `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` at the end of the window). The cue's form and the decision whether it may surface during `CLOSING_BLOCKED` without becoming a power oracle (IA-24's uniform attempt window exists to prevent one) are Interaction's and the art-director's (cross-GDD note, OQ-31); Layout supplies the geometry and asserts nothing about the cue. The debug assert that PCI:650 attributes to "a Layout projection" can run only where the rect and the foot box are both visible — Body's sweep (`PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP`); this GDD says so and routes a one-line wording note to the Controller owner. **`opening_ref` is the Graph OPENING `edge_id`** — the same `target_ref` that Interaction's `set_opening {target_ref, area}` carries (IA-24) — so one name serves an interior door and a mated hatch. Layout resolves it through a derived map `opening_leaves(edge_id) -> [(section_id, slot)]`: an interior door's opening is `edge_base + edge_ref index` (LY-6 c) and yields one leaf; a mated hatch's opening ids come from the weld (`seam_view(seam_id).opening_ids[]`, LY-16) and yield **two** leaves, one per section, each at its own `section_offset`. The map is derived, never persisted, and rebuilt in `post_restore`. An `edge_id` that is not a projected opening returns false and raises the diagnostic `opening_unknown` (a validate-time defect of the caller, never a player-reachable state). Items are not actors: a loose item inside a door's footprint is not a foot box; **ASK (Interaction, designed; consistent with IA-28, which treats a cell as valid only where `floor_tile_at` returns a tile)**: door cells are `NO_LOOSE` (`floor_tile_at` returns `NONE` for them), a drop snaps to the nearest legal cell, and an authored item on a door cell is the lint error `LOOSE_ON_DOOR`. Interaction's earlier `foot_box_overlaps_leaf` is gone (IA-37); Layout uses only its own name `leaf_would_overlap`, which Interaction calls from `validate` (IA-24) *(Interaction is designed; its call site currently passes `door_ref = (section_id, door_index)` and reads `door_ref` in IA-24, its D.12 and the registry `door_attempt` variables — a refresh routed in Cross-References B so that it passes the opening's `target_ref`)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**LY-12 — The OccluderSource, the boundary convention, the broadphase and `version`.**
(a) *Seam.* `occluder_source(body_id) -> OccluderSource` exposes `rects_in(aabb)` and `version(section_id)` with EM-20's names verbatim (names unchanged; the out parameter of LY-12 e and the sibling `static_version` / `leaf_version` are additive). EM-20 names no `body_id` while EM-30 compares geometry only within one body, so each instance's `rects_in` answers in THAT body's frame, in ascending key `(section_id, idx)` (D.9) — the **reserved key set** is stable across a weld, a cut and a restore because an attach renumbers nothing: every piece that can ever exist at a socket (PLATE, JAMB, LEAF; LY-15) owns an `idx` from the template's compile, and a state change only switches slots between active and inactive (an inactive slot is omitted from results exactly like a `leaf_w` 0 rect). What changes on a weld is therefore the *active subset* of the sockets' slots, never a key. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
(b) *Boundary convention — stated once.* Integer coordinates in Layout name pixel CELLS. A stored rect `[x0, x1) × [y0, y1)` therefore *occupies* cells `x0..x1−1`, `y0..y1−1`. **One convention per function, no exceptions** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: every box a caller passes IN (`rects_in`, `solid_in`, `clip_to_footprints`, `leaf_would_overlap`'s foot boxes, D.2, D.9, D.22) is **half-open** `[ax0, ax1) × [ay0, ay1)`; every rect handed OUT by `rects_in` **and by `solid_in`** is the **occupied span** `(x0, y0, x1−1, y1−1)`, so the Emitter's D.6 slab arithmetic is unchanged in code and Body's cell sweep reads the same numbers. (The earlier D.9 "closed query box" is retired; a half-open box of width `w` starting at `ax0` touches cells `floor_div(ax0, c)` to `floor_div(ax0 + w − 1, c)`.) Rule text: "the conservative closed test (EM-20 a, a graze blocks) is applied to the occupied span `[x0, x1−1] × [y0, y1−1]`; `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` is Chebyshev distance from that span" (D.2). A cell outside every occupied span — the first legal row south of a wall, Pipeline D.12 — is therefore not blocked at its origin, which is what EM-6's "outside every footprint by construction" (EM:157) assumes. The foot box's vertical placement is Body's; Layout's guarantee is stated on rectangles and on whatever box the caller passes: a box whose cells lie outside every occupied span is legal.
**CONFLICT C-1 (one-pixel example, not a rule change; routed to the Emitter owner):** EM:467 (D.6 example), EM:1013 (AC-22) and EM:1014 (AC-23) describe "wall x 408..432" as 25 closed points and reject an anchor at 432; under this rule the wall occupies 408..431, so (432, 200) passes with clearance 1 and (431, 200) fails; AC-22's three probes (540 blocked, 407 clear, 408 blocked) are unchanged. `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` is confirmed at 1 (range 1-2) and re-sourced to this GDD (Cross-References).
(c) *Broadphase.* A per-body uniform grid, cell `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` 48, derived, never persisted, rebuilt at build, attach, detach and restore (D.9). One structure serves Body's `solid_in`, the Emitter's segment test and Lighting; the Emitter's ADR candidate 4 ("the broadphase … and whether the spatial index lives in Layout or here", EM:32) is answered: **Layout**. `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` is sized to the Controller's performance criterion (PCI:1078: 2,000 rects, ≤ 0.05 ms per actor per tick at P99; spike, GDScript cost [UNV]). Callers issue ONE `solid_in` per actor per tick with the swept bounding box and sweep analytically against the result, never one query per 1-px step. Rejected: an Emitter-owned index (drift, double memory) and engine physics queries (EM-20: nondeterministic).
(d) *`version(section_id)`* is a monotone u32, NOT persisted, reset to 1 on `restore` (the Emitter rebuilds in its own `post_restore`; consumers must treat a restore as an unconditional rebuild signal, because a post-restore `version` can numerically equal a pre-restore cached value). It is bumped by `build_section`, `attach_section`, `detach_section`, `discard_section_geometry`, `layout.doors` and Layout's own `post_restore` (LY-26 c, 1 → 2 for a section with an open leaf). MVP has no prop mutator (props are static); the bump list is closed so a Vertical Slice movable prop adds one entry deliberately. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Which sections are bumped (the rule, stated once):** every section whose rect set can change is bumped, *moved or not*. That is (i) every section whose **socket state changes** — both sections of the seam on a weld and on a cut, including the host section that does not move, because on a weld its PLATE becomes JAMB plus LEAF and on a cut its LEAF (possibly open, `area` > 0) becomes a solid PLATE, so a consumer keyed on `version` that missed the unmoved mate would keep passing light through a solid plate (a see-through-but-solid wall, the failure the secondary design test forbids); and (ii) every section whose body-frame offset changes (every moved section). Each such section also gets the dirty key `SEC/<id>`, and both bodies of the seam get `BODY/<id>` (LY-16, LY-17, LY-27). **Three counters, one contract** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `static_version(s)` is bumped by every cause above except a leaf change; `leaf_version(s)` is bumped only by `layout.doors` (and by `post_restore`'s self-heal for an open leaf); `version(s)` is the EM-20 name and is bumped whenever either is, so a consumer that rebuilds everything keys on `version` unchanged, while a consumer that can patch (the Emitter's static layer, the weld-preview memo) keys the static layer on `static_version` and the dynamic leaves on `leaf_version`.
