Animating a door therefore bumps `static_version` 0 times; a stepped slide (a Vertical Slice option, OQ-14 c) costs a leaf patch per tick, never a full static rebuild. All three are not persisted and reset to 1 on `restore`. **`changed_region(section_id)`** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: a pure ordered read `changed_region(section_id) -> ALL | REGION(box) | FRAME`, valid for bumps applied in the current tick (cleared at the end of the tick; the Emitter reads it in W0 of the same tick, because `emitter.place` runs every tick after the P2 apply; NONE when no bump was applied to the section this tick); derived, never persisted (after a restore every section reports ALL once; `version` already resets to 1). Values: build, discard and `post_restore` = ALL (unchanged); a `layout.doors` leaf change = REGION(union box of the old and new leaf rectangles; Emitter ask E-N, unchanged); `attach_section` (LY-16) and `detach_section` (LY-17): for each of the two socket-host sections REGION(union box of that socket's PLATE, JAMB and LEAF slot rectangles, active and inactive, in the section's body frame); for every other moved section (the absorbed body's remaining sections, or the cut-off component's remaining sections) FRAME = a pure translation of the whole body (`rot_q` 0), geometry and slot sets unchanged. A socket-host section that also moves reports REGION; a reader detects its translation by comparing `section_offset(section_id)` with its cached value. The bumps of `version` and `static_version` are unchanged. The read tells the Emitter what kind of change a `static_version` bump was, so it can re-derive its `body_id` and keep its pair rows across a weld or cut without a full cold rebuild (Emitter S8 against S8b); a reader that does not read it treats every bump as ALL.
(e) *Allocation and the result contract.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The hot-path API is an **out-parameter** API: `rects_in(aabb, out: QueryBuffer)` and `solid_in(body_id, aabb, out: QueryBuffer)` fill a **caller-owned** `QueryBuffer` and return the record count; they return no array. A function that returned one shared reused buffer would alias between consumers (Body's sweep, the Emitter, Lighting and Interaction's throw all call within one tick) and cannot be both allocation-free and safe under copy-on-write, so each consumer owns one buffer and `clear()` resets its length without releasing capacity. **Record layout (structure of arrays, one index per record):** `keys: PackedInt64Array` — `rect_key = section_id × RECT_KEY_STRIDE + idx` reaches `2^32 − 1`, which overflows a 32-bit element for `section_id >= 2^19`, so the key column is 64-bit; `spans: PackedInt32Array` — four ints per record `(x0, y0, x1−1, y1−1)`, the occupied span in the BODY frame (LY-12 b); `flags: PackedByteArray` — one LY-7 flags byte. Records are in ascending `rect_key`; a rect appears once per call (the broadphase de-duplicates a rect spanning several cells). A value-returning wrapper (`rects_in_copy`) may exist for tools and tests only and is not on a hot path. The steady-state call allocates nothing once each buffer has reached its working capacity. (→ ADR: packed-array buffer layout and the engine cost of `resize`, spike SP-ENG-1.)

#### 1.3 Sockets, seams, welds, bodies

**LY-13 — A socket is a pre-declared attachment site.** SOCKET = `{socket_key, facing N|E|S|W, line_px, start_px, width_tiles, class_id, lanes[] {loop, lane_index, node_ref, port_local_index}, hatch {door_key} | none}`. `line_px` is the OUTER face of the hull wall (the wall footprint lies inboard of it: for facing E the footprint is `[line_px − 24, line_px)`); `start_px` is the low end of the span along the run; `width_tiles` equals the seam length `L_seam` (1-12, LSG:328); the socket's reference point `pos_px` is `(line_px, start_px)` for E/W facings and `(start_px, line_px)` for N/S facings, in section-local px. A **lane** is one loop feed-through across the seam and is the Layout record that PRODUCES a Graph weld-plan pair `(node_a, port_a, node_b, port_b, loop)`; "pair" stays the Graph's noun and "lane" is Layout's (C-10). The number of authored sockets per section is at most `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION`, which is itself at most `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION` (8, LSG:916 — cited, not restated; content lint). **Socket states** (registry: `socket_state`)**:** `FREE → WELDED(seam_id)`, then `CAPPED` (after a SEALED cut) or `CUT_OPEN` (after a RAGGED cut, LSG:172). `CAPPED` and `CUT_OPEN` **never return to `FREE` in MVP**: the scar is history (Pillar 5), and no mutator exists that could do it (LY-27). MVP: exactly one socket on the home-ship (LSG:228) and its mate on derelict B.

*The four-facing table (stated once; the N/S rule used to appear only in a fixture)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `W` is `width_tiles`; the span along the run is `[start_px, start_px + W × 48)`; every footprint is 24 px deep; "LOW" is the coordinate-low end of the span, where the hatch is aligned and its leaf retracts (LY-9, LY-14, LY-15).

| Facing | Wall run orientation | Hull footprint (normal axis × span axis) | `pos_px` | LOW end of the span | Mate facing | PLATE rect | JAMB rect (`W` >= 3) | LEAF rect at `leaf_w` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E | N-S | `[line_px − 24, line_px) × [start_px, start_px + W·48)` | `(line_px, start_px)` | north (low y) | W | the whole footprint | `[line_px − 24, line_px) × [start_px + 96, start_px + W·48)` | `[line_px − 24, line_px) × [start_px, start_px + leaf_w)` |
| W | N-S | `[line_px, line_px + 24) × [start_px, start_px + W·48)` | `(line_px, start_px)` | north (low y) | E | the whole footprint | `[line_px, line_px + 24) × [start_px + 96, start_px + W·48)` | `[line_px, line_px + 24) × [start_px, start_px + leaf_w)` |
| S | E-W | `[start_px, start_px + W·48) × [line_px − 24, line_px)` | `(start_px, line_px)` | west (low x) | N | the whole footprint | `[start_px + 96, start_px + W·48) × [line_px − 24, line_px)` | `[start_px, start_px + leaf_w) × [line_px − 24, line_px)` |
| N | E-W | `[start_px, start_px + W·48) × [line_px, line_px + 24)` | `(start_px, line_px)` | west (low x) | S | the whole footprint | `[start_px + 96, start_px + W·48) × [line_px, line_px + 24)` | `[start_px, start_px + leaf_w) × [line_px, line_px + 24)` |

Checks against the fixtures: `fixture_airlock` (E, `line_px` 696, `start_px` 240, `W` 2) gives footprint `[672, 696) × [240, 336)` with no JAMB; `fixture_derelict_b_socket` (W, `line_px` 120, `start_px` 144) gives `[120, 144) × [144, 240)`; `fixture_ns_pair` host (S, `line_px` 480) gives `[456, 480)` and donor (N, `line_px` 96) gives `[96, 120)`, which land abutting at body `y` 480 (AC-68). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

*Socket economy — flagged, not solved.* With one home-ship socket and a terminal `CAPPED`/`CUT_OPEN`, the first weld-then-cut leaves the ship with no free socket for the rest of the MVP slice. This is acceptable for the MVP slice (one weld scenario, concept MVP definition) and recorded as a Vertical Slice question to Modular Ship Assembly and the game-designer: either more sockets per home-ship, or a verb that re-cuts a `CAPPED` socket open again while leaving its history visible (old bead plus cut ring). The three options and the socket-economy formula are in Open Questions. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Consequence for the fantasy and the experience targets:** with one socket and one mate there is no *choice* of what to weld (only whether), so the claim that the crew chooses *what* to graft (Player Fantasy, X-3) is a Vertical Slice claim; and if OQ-1 is resolved before then, the design review's preferred answer is **option A** (a re-cut verb that reopens a `CAPPED` socket while leaving its history visible), because it is the only option that does not delete the scar the fantasy is built on.

**LY-14 — The mating predicate, `socket_plan` and `can_attach`.** Two sockets MATE iff all of the following hold; the verdict is the FIRST failing reason in this order (cheap before expensive; closed set, distinct from the Graph's): both exist (`SOCKET_UNKNOWN`); both are `FREE` (`SOCKET_NOT_FREE`); they lie on different bodies (`SAME_BODY`); facings are opposite (`FACING_MISMATCH`); `width_tiles` equal (`WIDTH_MISMATCH`); classes compatible (`CLASS_MISMATCH`; MVP: equal `class_id`); lane multisets equal by `(loop, lane_index)` (`LANE_MISMATCH`); and the translated footprints of the two bodies do not overlap (`GEOM_OVERLAP`, D.2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The `GEOM_OVERLAP` test set is the STATIC footprint, not `rects_in`:** `rects_in` returns occluders in their current leaf state, so a low prop (SOLID, not occluding) would escape it, the verdict would flip as a door animates, and floor laid on floor would pass. The test therefore runs over (i) every static rect of both bodies with **all** flags (SOLID or not, occluding or not), each socket's PLATE slot counted at its full footprint (the maximal, FREE-state geometry) and no leaf or JAMB slot, (ii) the tile cells of every room of both bodies (rect against tile, and tile against tile, so a wall over the other body's floor and two floors on one cell are both `GEOM_OVERLAP`), after the D.6 translation; abutting is legal. It uses the broadphase grid of each body and is **memoised on `(socket_a, socket_b, static_version_a, static_version_b)`** (D.22), so a door animating on either body never invalidates or flips it. The mated PLATE footprints of the two sockets themselves abut at the seam line and do not overlap. **Order of the two verdicts (C-4):** Layout's `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` answers socket-level occupancy FIRST; the Graph's `SOCKET_OCCUPIED` (LSG:170) remains the port- and edge-level reason. Because `SAME_BODY` is a hard reject and every weld merges two bodies, the weld relation over sections is always a forest, so a cut always splits a body into exactly two components (no ring, no "cut that does not split").
*Geometry.* Mating is ABUT: the two 24-px hull walls sit side by side, the shared boundary is the seam line and the door frames are in line. The weld offset is `offset_b = offset_a + pos_a − pos_b` (D.6). Every input of that sum lies on the 24-px grid (lint `GRID_24`, LY-30) and `offset_a` does too, so `offset_b` lies on the 24-px grid by construction (D.6): the closure needs no catalogue-wide socket phase, because rooms sit on per-room 24-px origins (LY-5). Any off-grid `offset_b` from a validated plan is a defect, not a reason code. The N/S double bulkhead's up-screen face arrangement is routed to the art-director (PIPE:794), not a Layout rule.
*`socket_plan(socket_a, socket_b) -> {pairs[], openings[], L_seam, reason}`* is a PURE query returning the Graph's weld-plan DATA verbatim (LSG:170): `pairs[]` one per lane, `openings[]` one per mated hatch pair `(comp_a, comp_b, area_max)`, `L_seam = width_tiles`. **Assembly adds `q` and issues the command; the Graph's `can_join(plan)` validates; both verdicts must pass before `apply`** *(Modular Ship Assembly is designed; MSA repeats this)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* This is the whole answer to the index's "three GDDs would each define a socket" (IDX:28): one record type, one mating rule, one plan builder. Lane matching is by `(loop, lane_index)` (positional within the socket), so a pipe never visibly kinks across the seam. A socket with no `hatch` is bulkhead-only (the weld joins lanes only; `openings[]` is empty). *(CLARIFY, orchestrator ruling: one-sided hatches.)* If exactly one of two mating sockets declares a hatch, the pair is legal and behaves as bulkhead-only: `openings[]` is empty, the hatched socket's hatch stays HULL (its PLATE slot stays active, as in `FREE`, LY-15) while the socket is `WELDED`, `socket_plan` raises the diagnostic `hatch_one_sided`, and no reason code is added (the closed set of LY-29 stands). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The player must be able to read it before the weld spends the socket:** `hatch_one_sided` is part of the `can_attach` / `socket_plan` **preview payload** `{reason, hatch_one_sided, openings_count}`, and the weld cursor outline (UI Requirements) shows the one-sided side diegetically as a closed bulkhead outline with no hatch ring on the bare side — "no passage here" — so welding onto a hatchless mate is never a surprise (routed to Modular Ship Assembly and the art-director). Rejected: a mismatch reason (it would forbid welding a hatched home-ship dock to a derelict whose wreck lost its hatch, which is the common case) and an automatic hatch on the bare side (Layout never invents geometry). A hatch requires `width_tiles >= 2` and is **low-aligned**: its `doorway_start_px` equals the socket's `start_px` (lint `HATCH_WIDTH` checks the width and lint `HATCH_ALIGN` checks the alignment; the field is derived for a hatch door and any other authored value is the lint error `HATCH_ALIGN`). A 3-tile (144-px) socket holds one 96-px hatch plus 48 px of bulkhead on the HIGH side. Low alignment is what keeps the two hatches of a mate in line: spans coincide after the D.6 translation and "LOW" is the same side in both frames (LY-9), so no per-hatch offset can differ and no `HATCH_OFFSET_MISMATCH` lint is needed. Both MVP fixtures satisfy it (`fixture_airlock`: `doorway_start_px` 240 = `start_px` 240; `fixture_derelict_b_socket`: 144 = 144). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**LY-15 — Hatches: HULL before, ONE opening after.** Before mating, a socket's hatch is HULL: the socket's PLATE, a `SOLID | OCCLUDES_LIGHT | HULL` rect covering the **whole socket span** (`width_tiles × 48` along the run × 24 deep — for the MVP's 2-tile socket that span is exactly the 96-px doorway) and no Graph opening. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: one plate footprint, used by the table, the prose and the compile rule below.)* The weld creates ONE Graph OPENING per mated hatch pair with `area_max = min(a.area_max, b.area_max)`; both sections' hatch leaves are DOOR_LEAF rects driven by that one `area` (two rects, one truth, hinges fixed by LY-9). A `CAPPED` hatch is HULL again (the same PLATE) plus the cap-plate presentation piece. A `CUT_OPEN` hatch is HULL — **solid and occluding** — plus the ragged-edge presentation piece: nothing lies beyond a cut (art bible 6.1: "a seam with a cap plate and nothing beyond it"), no avatar can leave the hull through it (the avatar has no physics body and no walkable mask, so a hull opening with no solid rect would be a walk-out into space), and a rect that blocked the body but not the light would be exactly the "wall the player cannot see but collides" the secondary design test forbids. The leak itself belongs to the Graph (`A_CUT` 24 per former opening, hull stub `hp` 2500, LSG:172 — the Graph's hole, not a Layout rect).

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this table is the socket-span variant matrix, replacing an earlier per-state table whose variant pieces had no reserved `idx`.)* It applies to a **hatched** socket of width `W` tiles (span `W × 48` px). The three variant slots are PLATE (reserved variant slot), JAMB (reserved variant slot) and LEAF (the hatch door's own slot in the door list, D.9); "on" means the slot's rect is returned by the queries, "off" means it is omitted and its `idx` stays reserved. A change of socket state never adds or removes an `idx`.

| Socket state (this socket) | PLATE | JAMB | LEAF | `W` = 2 (span = the 96-px doorway) | `W` >= 3 (span `W × 48`) | Light | Gas (Graph's) | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FREE` | on | off | off | PLATE 96 × 24 | PLATE `W·48` × 24 | blocked | sealed, no opening | Layout (rects); Graph (no opening) |
| `WELDED(seam)`, **two-sided** (the mate's socket also has a hatch) | off | on iff `W` >= 3 | on iff `leaf_w` > 0 (area from the shared OPENING) | LEAF only; JAMB length 0, omitted | JAMB `(W·48 − 96)` × 24 plus LEAF | by leaf | by OPENING `area` | Layout (rects); Graph (`area`) |
| `WELDED(seam)`, **one-sided** (this socket hatched, the mate bare; `hatch_one_sided`) | on | off | off | PLATE 96 × 24 | PLATE `W·48` × 24 | blocked | sealed, no opening | Layout |
| `WELDED(seam)`, this socket **hatchless** | n/a (no variant slots; the bulkhead stays its ordinary static wall rect) | n/a | n/a | static rect unchanged | static rect unchanged | blocked | no opening | Layout |
| `CAPPED` | on (the cap-plate presentation piece sits on it) | off | off | PLATE 96 × 24 | PLATE `W·48` × 24 | blocked | sealed | Layout |
| `CUT_OPEN` | on (the ragged piece is presentation) | off | off | PLATE 96 × 24 | PLATE `W·48` × 24 | blocked | Graph hull stub (`A_CUT`, hp 2500) | Layout (rect); Graph (stub) |

**The socket-span compile (stated once; used by LY-4, LY-7, LY-30 and D.9).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (a) The socket span is `[start_px, start_px + W × 48)` along its HULL run and must lie inside that one authored run (lint `SOCKET_ON_EXTERIOR`). (b) At template load the run is **split into three**: `[run start, start_px)` before the span, the span, and `[span end, run end)` after it. Before and after are ordinary static wall rects carrying the run's flags and each is omitted if its length is 0 (a 2-tile airlock socket that fills its whole run has neither); the span is **not** a static rect, so the plate never overlaps the wall (`RECT_OVERLAP` cannot fire) — the same "run compiles in parts, no SOLID overlap" precedent as the hull-port row of LY-7. (c) A **hatched** socket reserves two variant slots, PLATE and JAMB, plus its hatch door's LEAF slot; a **hatchless** socket reserves none and its span is simply part of the ordinary run (a bulkhead never opens, so its rect never changes). (d) The hatch is **low-aligned** (LY-14): the doorway is `[start_px, start_px + 96)`; the JAMB is `[start_px + 96, span end)`, length `W × 48 − 96`, so it is **0 px (omitted) at `W` = 2 and one 48-px piece at `W` = 3**; there is never a low jamb. (e) The pocket into which the leaf retracts is **presentation only** for a hatch: the leaf rect shrinks toward the LOW end and the retracted part is never a rect, so a hatch is **exempt from `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR`** (the LOW-side pocket of D.18 would lie outside a 2-tile run, and no rect or clearance test depends on it); interior doors keep the pocket rule. (f) `exposed_tiles` is unchanged: D.4 counts the **authored** run's `len_tiles`, and the split is a compile step. (g) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: split, because the LEAF's extent depends on `area`, which is not a function of the socket.)* **Slot activation** (which of PLATE, JAMB and LEAF are *present*) is a pure function of `(this socket's state, this socket's hatch, the mate's hatch, W)` — the table above — evaluated by LM1-LM4 and `post_restore`; the mate's hatch is known from the two templates, so the function needs no persisted field. **Leaf extent** (`leaf_w`, hence whether the present LEAF returns a rect) is a function of the opening's `area` (D.5), set by `layout.doors` in P3 — except that a LEAF newly activated by a weld (LM2) or by `post_restore` is initialised **closed** (`leaf_w = width_px`) and its last-projected cache seeded, so it is never absent before its first P3 derivation (LY-16, LY-26 d).

**LY-16 — Recording a weld.** `attach_section(socket_a, socket_b, seam_id, welded_at_T)` (LM2, Class A) runs after the Graph's `weld_join` inside Assembly's single `apply` (KRN:197: single commands "whose `apply` calls published mutators on Graph, Layout and Body"). It appends a `welds[]` row `{seam_id, section_a, socket_a, section_b, socket_b, welded_at_T, state ACTIVE}`; sets both sockets `WELDED(seam_id)`; re-bases every section of the absorbed body by D.6 and moves them onto the host body; marks the absorbed body `DISSOLVED`; switches the variant slots of both sockets (LY-15), **initialises each newly active LEAF closed (`leaf_w = width_px`, consistent with `weld_join`'s new opening starting at `area` 0, Assembly MSA-15) and seeds the last-projected `leaf_w` cache with that value in the same `apply`, so the hatch span is solid from the instant the PLATE switches off; `layout.doors` re-derives `leaf_w` from the opening's `area` in P3 and changes nothing while `area` is still 0** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: BLOCKING, the LEAF slot had no initial state at weld time, so a P2 reader between the weld and the P3 hook saw an open gap)*, rebuilds the grid; bumps `version` (and `static_version`, LY-12 d) of **every section whose socket state changed — the host section of `socket_a`/`socket_b` whether or not it moves — and of every moved section**, and marks the dirty keys of LY-32: `S/<seam_id>`, `BODY/<survivor>`, `BODY/<absorbed>`, and `SEC/<id>` for each of those sections. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text bumped moved sections only, so the unmoved host's PLATE → JAMB + LEAF change was invisible to a version-keyed consumer.)* The bump reports REGION for the two socket-host sections and FRAME for the other moved sections (LY-12 d); `body_of` of every moved section changes in this apply (absorbed -> survivor) and is readable by any ordered reader in the same tick; Emitter State derives its `body_id` from it *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The mated hatch's opening ids are read from the Graph (`seam_view(seam_id).opening_ids[]`, ordinal = the index in `socket_plan.openings[]`) into a derived cache `opening_leaves` (edge id → the leaf slots it drives, LY-11); the cache is not persisted and is rebuilt in `post_restore` (LY-26 d). **Host rule (deterministic, argument-order independent):** the surviving body is the body with the **lower `body_id`** (the older body; the home-ship, built first, in the MVP). `socket_a` and `socket_b` are re-labelled internally by survivor, not by argument order, so D.6's `offset_b = offset_a + pos_a − pos_b` always places the *absorbed* frame in the *survivor's* frame and `frame_shift_px` has one sign convention; a call with reversed arguments therefore produces the identical result, and the dissolved body is never the home-ship's by an argument mix-up. `welds[]` is **canonicalised**: it records the survivor-side section and socket as `section_a`/`socket_a` and the absorbed side as `section_b`/`socket_b` whatever the call order, so a reversed call produces a byte-identical block and `layout_hash`. Layout returns `{surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` (`frame_shift_px` as defined in D.6: the absorbed frame origin in the host frame, added to every moved section's offset) so Body can emit `BODY_TRANSFER` (PIPE:694) and keep the avatar's screen position where the layout allows — Body's concern. A weld **moves nothing already built** (the Graph's LS-15): only the absorbed body's frame is re-based, a translation of whole tiles. Layout records no `q` (Assembly's) and no cut mode until a cut (LY-17).

**LY-17 — Recording a cut; detach and body ids.** `detach_section(seam_id, boundary_mode)` (LM3, Class A) runs after the Graph's `cut_detach` (LSG:172). It marks the weld row `CUT_SEALED` or `CUT_RAGGED` with `cut_at_T`; sets both sockets `CAPPED` (SEALED) or `CUT_OPEN` (RAGGED); then runs connected components over the ACTIVE `welds[]` in ascending `section_id`. The component containing the body's `origin_section_id` keeps its `body_id`; the other component receives a NEW `body_id` from `next_body_id` (KRN:197: "Detach allocates a new body ID from the Layout block") and its `origin_section_id` is **`m`, the minimum `section_id` of that component** (D.12 — the same section whose origin becomes the new frame origin); its offsets are re-based so its frame origin is its minimum-id section's origin (D.12). **Dirty keys and versions (the rule of LY-12 d):** `S/<seam_id>`, `BODY/<kept>`, `BODY/<new>`, and `SEC/<id>` plus a `version` and `static_version` bump for **every section whose socket state changed (both sections of the seam, the kept-side section even though it does not move) and every moved section**. If the hatch was open when it was cut (`area` > 0), the kept section's LEAF slot goes inactive and its PLATE goes active in the same apply, so the bump is what tells a version-keyed consumer that a light gap has become a solid plate. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The bump reports REGION for the two socket-host sections and FRAME for the other moved sections (LY-12 d); `body_of` of every moved section changes in this apply (kept/new split) and is readable by any ordered reader in the same tick; Emitter State derives its `body_id` from it *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* **Entombment: who owns the guarantee that no actor stands where the PLATE materialises** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; the severity is RECOMMENDED because the crisis cut and lethal friendly fire make the state reachable and AC-28 treats such an overlap as a Body defect)*: **Layout performs no actor check** (it reads nothing from `body.state`, LY-18) and `apply` places the PLATE unconditionally. On the **CHANNEL cut path** the guarantee is Assembly's: the completion `validate` blocks the cut outright (`MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`, MSA-21) while any crewmate's foot box overlaps the open hatch passage, using `detach_preview`'s `rects[]` (the PLATE footprint) as the test region. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* There is no cut path around Assembly's completion `validate`: CHANNEL rejects with `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` and CASCADE (the "crisis" cut) defers and is abandoned on expiry, never forced (MSA-21, MSA-22). As a net behind that, Body REFUSES (it does not eject; BDY-24 iii, ruled 2026-10-28, delegated ruling G6): `rebase_on_detach` takes this record's `rects[]` and returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`, writing nothing, if a foot box overlaps them. Layout still performs no actor check. `detach_section` also checks `CAPACITY` in its validate (LY-29). Returns `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}`, where `rects[]` is exactly `detach_preview(seam_id).rects[]` (the seam's hatch rectangles, one per side, pre-cut body frame, computed before the sockets change) so prediction and apply cannot drift *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **A cut through a compartment is not representable:** a cut is requested only by `seam_id` (LSG:849 "Cuts happen only along seams, never through a compartment"), so Layout has no "cut here" geometry input. A cut never splits gas by a volume ratio because a compartment belongs to one section (LSG:172).

**LY-18 — Bodies, membership and the Body boundary.** Layout owns `bodies[]`, each `section_instances[].body_id`, offsets, and `welds[]`. Ship Body owns pose, velocity, actor positions, kinematics and any engine-side shapes built from Layout data. Body repeats *(Ship Body & Interior Physics is designed; BDY repeats this)*: its ownership of `body.state` (pose, velocity, actor positions); `restore_after: [layout.state]`; and the physics PROJECTOR (KRN:197, KRN:198). Layout reads nothing from `body.state` (acyclic). *Correction surfaced to the 2D Lighting stage:* its brief says "Ship Body owns frames and `body_id`"; the kernel's "Detach allocates a new body ID from the Layout block" (KRN:197) decides it — Layout allocates the id and owns membership; Body owns the frame's POSE. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**LY-19 — Discard.** `discard_section_geometry(section_id)` (LM4, Class A) runs after the Graph's `discard_section` M4, which requires no ACTIVE seam (LSG:172). Precondition: no `WELDED` socket, else `SECTION_WELDED`. It marks the instance row `discarded` (a **tombstone**: ids are retained so they are never reused — the Graph's LS-21 form), removes its rects from the grid, dissolves its sole body and bumps `version`. Weld-history rows that name the section stay (Pillar 5), and the surviving mate's socket keeps its `CAPPED`/`CUT_OPEN` state. Items that lay in the section belong to Interaction and are carried or destroyed by the orchestrating apply (CR-9 atomicity), not by Layout.

#### 1.4 Slots, anchors, derived promises

**LY-20 — Hardpoints are Layout declarations; the Item DB decides what fits.** HARDPOINT fields per LY-4. The Graph's ABSENT or installed node is the runtime state; `compatible_defs(h)` (IDB F-6, IDB:577-594) is the Item DB's pure function, called by Layout's STRUCTURAL lint: every hardpoint has at least one compatible def; a LOAD (kind 13) hardpoint without `accepted_subtypes` is a lint ERROR (heater and lamp share a port signature) — this unblocks IDB AC-18 (IDB:1180). `socket_tag` is an advisory lint hint, never authoritative; port types are (IDB:169). A hardpoint's footprint rect (`MACHINE` flag, static) stays whether or not a module is installed: a pedestal or mount remains after salvage and Layout has no dependency on node state. The nouns stay distinct: **HARDPOINT** (module slot, node-backed) versus **LOOSE SLOT** (LY-22); the index's "salvage/module slots" means both.

**LY-21 — A HULL_SEGMENT has no hardpoint.** The hull plate is a PART, not a module (HULL_SEGMENT has no `def_id`, IDB-5, LSG:185). `plate_positions[]` per HULL_SEGMENT = the host wall-face tile set (the Graph's `tiles` field, LSG:68), `bound_room_key`, and `exposed` (true iff the run is HULL); `segment_at(face_tile) -> segment_key | NONE` is a read, and `plate_positions(section_id)` lists the records in ascending HULL_SEGMENT node id (template order). It is the input of Adaptive Audio's `hull_anchor` derivation (AA-17, registry `hull_anchor`: the floor-division centroid of the lowest-id HULL_SEGMENT's plate set composed with `section_offset`), which Layout confirms as the contract (CX-A4); Layout publishes no `hull_anchor` read, because Layout's geometry reads answer section-local by default (LY-3 c). The DESTROYED-segment replacement path (LSG:755 against M5's ABSENT/SALVAGED precondition, IDB Open Questions 1 and 11) stays routed to the Graph and Item DB owners (C-9); Layout states only the geometry. Precondition for the MVP slice's seven HULL_SEGMENTs (LSG:228): every room tagged `outer` has at least `HULL_RUNS_MIN` disjoint exterior wall runs **that bear a FACE (a `CAP_ONLY` run, including every HULL run on a room's south edge, D.14, has no face to carry a plate and is not counted)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, each long enough for a segment's face tiles, separated by `SEGMENT_GAP_MIN_PX` so two breach trails do not overlap at the wall base; plate positions of different segments never share a wall tile (lints `OUTER_ROOM_HULL_RUNS`, `SEGMENT_TILE_SHARED`).

**LY-22 — Loose slots and story slots; no item table.** LOOSE SLOT = `{slot_id, room_key, anchor_px, surface FLOOR|SHELF|CRATE, allowed_class[], capacity 1}`. The authored start placement `{def_id, count, condition_bp}` is Derelict Authoring's CONTENT, expanded to an ItemInstance by the Item DB's `fresh_instance` and placed by Interaction's `place_loose(inst, section_id, pos_px)` at world build *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **Layout's schema reserves NO item table**: the loose-item holder is Interaction's `interaction.state` *(orchestrator ruling; Interaction is designed and IA-36 adds it)*, which adds `layout.state` to its own `restore_after`. One writer per fact (kernel H3): Interaction performs every instance move (IDB:426-452), and atomicity with a discard or detach is given by CR-9 whichever owner's mutator holds the rows. Layout supplies `loose_slots(section_id)`, `floor_tile_at(section_id, px) -> tile | NONE` (placement validity) and `body_of(section_id)`; an item position is `(section_id, pos_px section-local)`, so a weld or a detach moves nothing. STORY SLOT (Vertical Slice; art bible 6.5 ("one cause, one verb, one absence per wreck", placed as three tagged slots)) = `{slot_id, role CAUSE|VERB|ABSENCE, room_key, anchor_px}`; the list is reserved in schema v1, empty and lint-checked in MVP, so the Vertical Slice addition is not a `schema_version` bump. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**LY-23 — Anchors.** `anchor_geometry(anchor) -> {kind WALL_FACE|FLOOR, host_face_id, base_px_polyline[], sort_origin_y, section_id}` (FT:306 names verbatim). The Graph descriptor anchor `(node_id, port_local_index)` or `edge_id` (LSG:152-153, "Layout supplies the polyline") maps to a template anchor through `section_of(id)` and `graph_ref_base` (id minus base = template-local index). The polyline is axis-aligned, integer, wall part first then floor part (FT-13); `edge_len_px = Σ|Δ|` (D.8) and must not exceed `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` (1152, 24 tiles — the Failure & Trail knob's content bound, owned here as a lint: Layout REJECTS a longer polyline and never shortens it, FT:642). It returns nothing, without error, for an id whose section is DISCARDED (FT:703-704: the draw must not crash). `sort_origin_y` is per Pipeline D.12 for WALL_FACE and `NONE` for pure FLOOR anchors (floor marks are not Y-sorted). `subject_anchors(section_id) -> [{local_key, kind_hint DOOR_FACE|HARDPOINT|SOCKET|SLOT|CONSOLE|INSTRUMENT, anchor_px, room_id, console_role}]` where `console_role` is `POD` or `DOCK` and is present iff `kind_hint` is `CONSOLE` (a structural lint `CONSOLE_ROLE_UNSET`, ERROR, rejects a CONSOLE anchor without a role and a role on any other kind); Layout asserts no placement: Derelict Authoring authors them (a POD anchor in every dock room, a DOCK anchor in the home airlock only; its lints `POD_CONSOLE_MISSING` and `DOCK_CONSOLE_MISSING`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: two DOOR_FACE anchors per **interior** door, one per face, each `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` outside the leaf's occupied span (EM:208 e); **a socket hatch gets ONE inner-face DOOR_FACE per hatch per section, plus its WELD_SOCKET anchor** (the outer face lies in void or in the mate's wall, which would conflict with `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE` and `DOOR_APPROACH_CLEAR`, so no outer-face anchor is placed; the mate's own section carries its own inner-face anchor) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; "Layout places, Interaction names the kind and registers". Presentation rebuilds anchors statelessly from Layout on Layout's `SEC/<id>` and the Graph's `T`/`SEC/<id>` (FT:265, FT AC-107). A wall fitting with no floor-run record is a lint error (`WALL_FITTING_NO_RUN`, FT:703), not a hidden trail. Layout must not grow a "trail" field: Failure & Trail reads Graph descriptors only and has no block (FT:26).

**LY-24 — `V` and `exposed_tiles` never change while a room exists.** The MVP promise to the Graph, as rule text: "`V` and `exposed_tiles` of an instantiated room never change while the room exists" (LSG:840 is the Vertical Slice re-base rule, not an MVP path). `V` is D.3. `exposed_tiles(room)` is the sum of the lengths of the room's HULL wall-run segments (D.4), static at build; socket frames and plate positions lie inside HULL runs (lint) so they are counted once, and a weld does not change it. The **conservative error** this accepts: a welded, closed hatch still radiates as hull. It is quantified in Edge Cases (the single MVP socket is 2 of the fixture airlock's 6 exposed tiles, D.4; a larger outer room would carry 20-40) and the Vertical Slice re-base is routed to the Graph owner in Open Questions. The Graph's lint ranges (`V` 4-200, `exposed_tiles` 0-80, LSG:299, LSG:452) are the Graph's variable ranges (not reason codes); Layout's structural lint reports a room outside them under its own lint ids `V_RANGE` and `EXPOSED_RANGE` and restates no bound. *Which value governs (answers Derelict Authoring OQ-DA-13):* D.4 evaluated on the loaded template's HULL runs governs everywhere, including across a 24-px jog (each run's `len_tiles` counts once); an authoring design table is documentation, and Authoring's `EXPOSED_DRIFT` WARN (tolerance 1 tile) reports where the two differ.

#### 1.5 Construction, restore, mutators, reads

**LY-25 — World construction order.** `build_section(template_id, kind) -> {section_id, body_id}` (LM1, Class A) = allocate ids → record the instance on its own NEW body at `offset_px` (0, 0) (the body frame's origin is its minimum-id section's origin, D.12) → Graph M1 `instantiate_section(section_id, template_id, kind)` (the Graph's own table names the caller "Layout / derelict build, home-ship setup", LSG:180; `kind` is an argument forwarded to M1 and is **not persisted by Layout** — the Graph's section record owns it, LS-17, LSG:174) → store `graph_ref_base` → build the grid → bump `version`. **Every built section starts on its own body, and every multi-section body is produced by welds only** (authored welds in Vertical Slice procgen use `attach_section` with `welded_at_T` 0): there is no "place on an existing body" path, so the weld relation stays a forest (LY-14) and the component split of LY-17 stays exact. **Layout calls NO Emitter or Interaction mutator.** The orchestrating atomic apply — Web Map & Transit's `init_world(world_seed)` per Save/Load SL-18 (SL:255, run in `LOADING` in `restore_after` order under a WriteScope), the only caller of LM1 (the pod is built there once, as one persistent body, and a pod hop builds nothing — Web Map CF-LY; Assembly never discards (MSA-24) and is not an LM1 caller (C11)) — calls, inside one WriteScope and in this fixed order: Layout `build_section` → Emitter E1 `instantiate_section_emitters(section_id)` (its stated caller stays "Assembly / Web Map atomic apply", EM:147) → Interaction `sync_section(section_id)` → PROJECTOR notification *(Web Map & Transit and Interaction are designed; the call order is theirs to repeat)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Layout supplies their data through reads (`subject_anchors`, `loose_slots`, LY-22, LY-23). This keeps `restore_after: []` honest: Layout depends on nothing at restore. The MVP world (home-ship + derelict A + derelict B + the always-dormant pod: four sections on four bodies, registry `mvp_template_roster`) is a data file `assets/data/world/mvp_world.json` consumed by `init_world`; the floor plans in it are Derelict Authoring's. The build is incremental and the PROJECTOR reports ready when its scene content exists; Transit's `ARRIVING` is the fixed-length state `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` 45 ticks; the Pipeline, not the sim, holds the black plate for any PROJECTOR lag (a large section may exceed one tick — `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold, not a cut-off).

**LY-26 — Restore, the two validity classes, and template drift.**
(a) `restore(data, stored_version)` replaces the instance tables whole into scratch and swaps only on success; an unsupported `stored_version` is a reported failure, never a silent default. The snapshot obeys SL-2 (SL:176): no `Vector2i`, `Rect2`, `Color` or `Resource`; integer vectors travel as two ints or a packed integer array; dictionary keys are String or int; 64-bit values (`welded_at_T`, `cut_at_T`) never travel as JSON numbers. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Concrete encoding rules** (JSON text turns int keys into strings and numbers into floats; the payload itself is `var_to_bytes` binary, SL-2, but the dev `dump-save` JSON and the generic K-3 round-trip test must not change a table's meaning): (1) **no integer-keyed Dictionary** — every table (instances, sockets, welds, bodies) is a dense set of parallel packed arrays in ascending id order, so a stringified key can never be misread; the few Dictionaries are String-keyed and hold only scalars and packed arrays; (2) **64-bit values** (`welded_at_T`, `cut_at_T`; and `layout_hash` wherever Save/Load's header carries it, which is not `layout.state`, D.10 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) travel as **two 32-bit int halves** `(hi, lo)`, each normalised with `& 0xFFFFFFFF` before any compare (D.10) — the project convention (Interaction `last_salvage_T`, Assembly `last_shape_change_T`, and D.21's two-int count) — and are rendered as exact-width lowercase hex strings only by Save/Load's header and the dev dump (SL-2 S-1), never as a JSON number; (3) **`snapshot()` deep-copies**: it returns fresh packed arrays, never a reference into the live block, so a mutation after `snapshot()` cannot reach a captured snapshot (and a captured snapshot cannot be mutated into the live block).
(b) *Class 1 — static queries, valid immediately after `restore()` returns.* Every query that depends only on templates and the instance table — `room_at`, `V`, `exposed_tiles`, `room_surface`, `socket_plan`, `anchor_geometry`, static `rects_in`, `section_offset`, `body_of` — is valid at once, because templates were loaded in BOOTING and the instance table is the only restored state. This makes the Graph's loud failure (`GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_ROOM` on mis-order, LSG:831) impossible by construction whatever the order of `post_restore` calls.
(c) *Class 2 — leaf-dependent results, valid only after Layout's own `post_restore()`.* DOOR_LEAF rects depend on the Graph's persisted `area`, which Layout cannot read inside its own `restore()` (the Graph restores after Layout) and which the Graph now publishes as `opening(edge_id) -> {area, area_max, door_state}` (LY-10; the ask is closed, OQ-3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **(orchestrator ruling; routed amendment to the Layout-side promise "every query is valid immediately after restore":)** leaves are derived by one `derive_doors` pass in Layout's `post_restore` (the kernel guarantees every `restore()` has completed before any `post_restore()` begins, KRN:843, so the Graph's persisted `area` is in memory whatever the order of the `post_restore` calls) and thereafter by `layout.doors`. Between `restore()` and `post_restore()` leaf-dependent queries (`rects_in`, `solid_in`, `clip_to_footprints`, `leaf_would_overlap`, `door_passage` (reports `open_extent_px` 0) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*) report the TEMPLATE default (every leaf closed) and raise a `stale_leaf` diagnostic flag if read. **Self-heal:** Layout's `post_restore` bumps `version(section_id)` (1 → 2) for every section whose derived leaf set differs from the all-closed template default; LY-12(d)'s "a restore is an unconditional rebuild signal" and the Emitter's W0 `version` read (EM:227) therefore rebuild the Emitter's static layer on tick 1 with no ordering between the two `post_restore` calls, and `body.integrate after: layout.doors` (LY-10) covers every P3 reader. Whether `post_restore` order follows `restore_after` is **informational only** (CR-8 and KRN:843 say "after every `restore()`"); no Layout rule depends on the answer. The `stale_leaf` diagnostic and this restore path are **live behaviour**, not a stand-in for an unanswered ask: they stay as specified now that the Graph read exists, because the window between `restore()` and `post_restore()` is real whatever the Graph publishes. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `post_restore` also bumps `leaf_version` (never `static_version`) for each such section, and rebuilds the derived `opening_leaves` map from the restored `welds[]` and `seam_view(seam_id).opening_ids[]`.
(d) `post_restore()` does cross-block VERIFICATION only: every `graph_ref_base` resolves (node, edge and compartment counts match the template), every installed `def_id` at a hardpoint resolves in the Item DB catalogue (a diagnostic, not a hard fail), and every `welds[]` `seam_id` resolves to a Graph SeamRecord. Failures use the restore reason set (LY-29).
(e) *Template drift.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The single `template_hash` is **split in two**, both persisted per instance (D.10): `geometry_hash` (u32; the canonical bytes of everything that decides where a rect, tile, socket, door or anchor is — room `origin_px` and tile runs, wall runs and the compiled rect table, socket `facing`/`line_px`/`start_px`/`width_tiles`/`hatch`, door positions and `area_max`, hardpoint cells, anchor polylines) and `visual_hash` (u32; kit-piece ids, art references, dialect, labels, hinted flags that no query reads). `restore` refuses an unknown `template_id` (`LAYOUT_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_TEMPLATE`) and a STRUCTURAL change (`LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED`: the sets of `room_key`, `socket_key`, `hp_key`, `node_ref`, or any room's tile set differ) **and a geometry change on an instance that carries history** — `geometry_hash` differs and the instance has any non-`FREE` socket, any `welds[]` row naming it, or shares its body with another section — because a moved socket, wall edge or doorway under a welded or cut instance restores with broken abutment while every key set still matches (a socket position or wall rect edit used to classify as "visual-only"). A geometry change on a never-welded `FREE` instance alone is accepted with one diagnostic `geometry_changed_fresh` (nothing depends on it yet and its rects are re-derived from the new template); a `visual_hash` difference alone is accepted with one diagnostic. Pre-release saves are disposable (SL-14, adopted verbatim); after the first build given outside the project the migration obligation of LY-6(e) applies. `rot_q` must be 0 (LY-3 d).

**LY-27 — Mutators.** Layout has four mutators, all Class A (command-backed, validate-then-apply, CR-9) and **no Class B** (clamp-never-reject): it holds no quantity that can sensibly be clamped. Each has a pure `can_*` validate query; `apply` is infallible once validated (a failure inside `apply` is a defect and sends the kernel to `FAULTED`).

| # | Mutator | Takes | Validate (pure) | Caller | Marks (dirty keys) | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LM1 | `build_section` | `template_id, kind` (`kind` forwarded to M1 only, never persisted); returns `{section_id, body_id}` | `can_build`: template known, Layout capacity (`MAX_SECTIONS`, `MAX_BODIES`, rooms, rects, **`MAX_DOORS_LOADED`** (loaded doors plus the template's doors; *added 2026-10-27 — re-review: the edge case 'refused earlier by `can_build` with `CAPACITY`' had no validate clause behind it*), **`next_section_id <= 2^20 − 1`**, the bound D.9's `rect_key` needs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), Graph `CAPACITY` pre-check | Web Map `init_world` (the only caller; a pod hop builds nothing) | `SEC/<id>`, `BODY/<id>` | Layout |
| LM2 | `attach_section` | `socket_a, socket_b, seam_id, welded_at_T` | `can_attach` (LY-14 reason set) | Assembly `weld`, after the Graph's `weld_join` | `S/<seam_id>`, `BODY/<survivor>`, `BODY/<absorbed>`, and `SEC/<id>` for **every section whose socket state changed (both sockets' sections, the unmoved host included) and every moved section** (5 keys for the MVP weld of two single-section bodies: `S` 1 + `BODY` 2 + `SEC` 2, AC-73) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Layout |
| LM3 | `detach_section` | `seam_id, boundary_mode`; returns `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}` (`rects[]` = `detach_preview(seam_id).rects[]`, pre-cut frame, computed before the sockets change; BDY-24 iii) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `can_detach`: seam exists and is ACTIVE, and a body can be allocated (`CAPACITY`, LY-29) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Assembly `seam_cut`, after the Graph's `cut_detach` | `S/<seam_id>`, `BODY/<kept>`, `BODY/<new>`, and `SEC/<id>` for **every section whose socket state changed (both sockets' sections, the kept-side section included) and every moved section** (6 keys in D.12's example) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Layout |
| LM4 | `discard_section_geometry` | `section_id` | `can_discard`: exists, no WELDED socket | Web Map only (Assembly never discards, MSA-24), after the Graph's M4 | `SEC/<id>`, `BODY/<id>` | Layout |

*(CLARIFY, adopted — deviation from the brief's provisional list.)* There is NO `install_cap_plate` and NO `set_socket_state` mutator: a socket's state changes only inside LM2/LM3, atomic with the Graph's weld and cut, so a cap plate or a scar cannot exist without its cut record and no caller can desync them. **Dirty tracking is explicit inside each mutator** — the Godot 4.7 packed-array element-write change (setting one element of a packed array no longer calls the setter of the whole property, breaking-changes 4.6→4.7; kernel CR-8 "4.7 gotcha") means a setter-based dirty flag would silently miss element writes, and `layout.state` is mostly packed arrays.

**LY-28 — Read views (the one true channel).** All reads are pure, side-effect free, deterministic, callable from `validate`, P3 and presentation, and **none has a "perceived" variant** (the mirror of LSG:199). Frame and ordering are part of the contract.

| Query | Returns | Frame | Order | Valid after | Owner of the fact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `room_at(section_id, px) -> room_id \| NONE` (sill px → `sill_room`) | id | section-local | n/a | `restore`; hook-callable (Suit SE-3) | Layout |
| `compartment_of_room(room_id)`, `room_of_compartment(cid)` | id mapping via `comp_base` | n/a | n/a | `restore` | Layout (mapping); Graph (compartment) |
| `V(room_id)`, `exposed_tiles(room_id)` | ints | n/a | n/a | `restore` | Layout |
| `room_surface(compartment_id) -> {floor_tiles[], wall_faces[]}` | FT:306 shape | section-local | tile id ascending | `restore` | Layout |
| `room_boundary(room_id)` | polyline | section-local | clockwise from min tile | `restore` | Layout |
| `floor_tile_at(section_id, px)` | tile or `NONE` | section-local | n/a | `restore` | Layout |
| `anchor_geometry(anchor)`, `subject_anchors(section_id)` | LY-23 | section-local | `local_key` ascending | `restore` | Layout |
| `occluder_source(body_id)` → `rects_in(aabb, out)`, `version(section_id)`, `static_version(section_id)`, `leaf_version(section_id)` | records into the caller's `QueryBuffer`: `keys` (int64), occupied spans `(x0, y0, x1−1, y1−1)`, flags byte (LY-12 e); input `aabb` half-open | BODY | ascending key `(section_id, idx)` | `post_restore` (door leaves) | Layout *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: out-parameter, 64-bit keys, one convention)* |
| `solid_in(body_id, aabb, out)` | SOLID records into the caller's `QueryBuffer`, **same layout and the same occupied-span output as `rects_in`**; input `aabb` half-open | BODY | ascending key | `post_restore` | Layout *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `clip_to_footprints(body_id, from, to) -> point` *(Interaction throw clipping, IA-29; names confirmed by IA-28/IA-36/IA-37)* | the first cell on the segment `from → to` (inclusive of `to`) that lies inside a SOLID occupied span, or `to` if none; the caller's landing cell is the cell before it (Interaction D.4 derives its `s − 1`) | BODY | n/a | `post_restore` | Layout |
| `leaf_would_overlap(opening_ref, area, foot_boxes[])` — `opening_ref` is the Graph OPENING `edge_id`; evaluates **every** leaf projected from it, on both sections of a mated hatch, and is true if any overlaps any foot box (LY-11); foot boxes half-open, body frame | bool | BODY | n/a | `post_restore` | Layout (foot boxes: caller) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `leaf_obstruction(opening_ref, area, foot_boxes[])` — the geometric "why" of a refused close (LY-11) | `{box_index, leaf_slot}` or `NONE` | BODY | first box in caller order | `post_restore` | Layout *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `socket_plan`, `can_attach`, `can_build`, `can_detach`, `can_discard` | plan / reason; the `can_attach` preview payload is `{reason, hatch_one_sided, openings_count}` (LY-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | n/a | lane order | `restore` | Layout |
| `section_offset(section_id)`, `body_of(section_id)`, `sections_of(body_id)` | ints | body | ascending | `restore` | Layout |
| `section_at(body_id, px) -> section_id \| NONE` *(Ship Body BDY-29)* | id | BODY | n/a | `restore` | Layout — containment in each section's own half-open cell set `cells(s)` (its rooms plus its owned rectangles, incl. the 24-px hull footprint and hatch rectangles, **plus every door's and hatch's sill strip (door-width × 24 px, LY-5) in every leaf state** — a sill is not a tile and a leaf rect disappears when the door opens, so without the strip an open doorway's walkable px would answer `NONE`; `cells(s)` is therefore the same set as the walkable Floor of D.17) translated by `section_offset` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the sets of one body are disjoint (LY-14 abut mating, LY-8 c), so no tie rule exists; FLOOR division (LY-3 f) |
| `weld_offset(socket_a, socket_b) -> {offset_b, frame_shift_px}` *(Assembly ASK L3)* | ints | body | n/a | `restore` | Layout — D.6 evaluated without mutation; LM2 applies the same values |
| `detach_preview(seam_id) -> {keep_sections[], leave_sections[], moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}` *(Assembly ASK L1)* | records | body (pre-cut frame) | ascending `section_id` | `post_restore` | Layout — the same components and re-base as LM3 / D.12 computed without mutation, so prediction and apply cannot drift (Assembly `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`); `rects[]` = the cut seam's hatch rectangles, one per side, the input Assembly passes to Body's `actors_overlapping` (MSA-21) |
| `hull_segments_near(section_id, px, r) -> [HULL_SEGMENT node id]` *(Horror C-HE-11)* | ids | section-local | node id ascending | `restore` | Layout — every HULL_SEGMENT whose plate-position face span (the HULL wall footprint over its `start_tile .. start_tile + len_tiles − 1`) lies within Chebyshev distance `r` of `px` (D.2's occupied-span distance); node ids through `graph_ref_base` |
| `loose_slots(section_id)`, `hardpoints_of(section_id)`, `segment_at(face_tile)`, `plate_positions(section_id)` (LY-21) | records | section-local | key ascending | `restore` | Layout |
| `room_adjacency(section_id) -> [{room_a, room_b_or_EXTERIOR, via DOOR\|HATCH, via_id, sill_room}]` | records | n/a | `(room_a, via_id)` ascending | `restore` | Layout (serves Authoring lints, Boarding entry, Attention/Horror pathing — provisional); `via_id` = the door's template-local door index, the `door_index` of Interaction's `door_ref = (section_id, door_index)` (IA-24); a HATCH record is a Door record with `hatch_socket_key` (LY-4) in the same index space; `(section_id, via_id)` names exactly one leaf *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `door_passage(section_id, via_id) -> {orientation EW\|NS, sill_rect, gap_rect, open_extent_px}` | one record, or NONE with no error for an unknown or discarded section or an unknown `via_id` | section-local base px | n/a | valid after `post_restore` (Class 2, same as the door leaves) | Layout — D.5 *(Added 2026-10-28, cross-GDD fix, Horror HE-19, GAP)*: Layout's door GEOMETRY, derived from the leaf state `layout.doors` already projects from the Graph's `opening(edge_id) = {area, area_max, door_state}`; Layout does not store, own or write `area` or `door_state` (LY-35 unchanged) |
| `changed_region(section_id) -> ALL \| REGION(box) \| FRAME` (NONE when no bump was applied to the section this tick) | variant | `REGION` box in the section's body frame | n/a | derived, never persisted; valid only for bumps applied in the current tick, cleared at the end of the tick | Layout — LY-12 (d) *(Added 2026-10-28, cross-GDD fix, Emitter body_id/weld GAP)* |
| `route_length_px(anchor_a, anchor_b)` (diagnostic; shortest 4-connected route over free lattice cells incl. sills) | int | section-local | n/a | `restore` | Layout (the escape-route LINT is Authoring's — Pillar 2 content; this is never a runtime pathing service) |
| `south_band(room_id) -> [{x0, x1, y0, y1}]`, `behind_wall_footprint(section_id, px) -> bool` (true iff `px` lies in a room's `south_band`, D.14 — floor hidden behind the face of a `FACE`-variant east-west partition on its south side; empty for `CAP_ONLY` runs and for cap-only HULL south walls; Horror R-4's presenter flag; *amended 2026-10-27 — design review*), `head_clearance_tiles(anchor)`, `wall_face_rows(body_id)` (diagnostics; the last serves the Failure & Trail bound `WALL_ROWS_MAX` 16, FT:591) | records / ints | section-local | n/a | `restore` | Layout |
| `layout_hash()`, `template_hash(section_id) -> {geometry_hash, visual_hash}` | u64 as two int32 halves / two u32 | n/a | n/a | `restore` | Layout; the `layout_hash` cadence is D.10 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**LY-29 — Closed reason-code sets.** Domain rejects surface as `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)` in the kernel's existing channel.
- `can_attach` (closed, evaluated in this order, distinct from the Graph's `can_join` set): `SOCKET_UNKNOWN, SOCKET_NOT_FREE, SAME_BODY, FACING_MISMATCH, WIDTH_MISMATCH, CLASS_MISMATCH, LANE_MISMATCH, GEOM_OVERLAP`.
- `can_build`: `TEMPLATE_UNKNOWN, CAPACITY` (Layout's caps, including the section-id bound `next_section_id <= 2^20 − 1`; the Graph's `CAPACITY` stays the Graph's). A built section always starts on its own new body at offset (0, 0) (LY-25), so there is no body, offset or overlap reason. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- `can_detach` (closed, evaluated in this order): `SEAM_UNKNOWN, SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE, CAPACITY`. `CAPACITY` means the cut would allocate a body past `MAX_BODIES` live bodies; it is checked in **validate**, so `apply` stays infallible and a cut at the Layout ceiling is a clean reject, never a `FAULTED` event. `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` (Lighting's, 4) is **not** a Layout reject: beyond it Lighting only degrades the surplus bodies' lights to stand-ins and the simulation is unaffected, so refusing a crisis cut for a lighting budget would be the worse failure; the zero MVP margin (4 against 4) is reported by the world lint `BODIES_LIVE_MARGIN` (WARN) and asserted in AC-88. `can_discard`: `SECTION_UNKNOWN, SECTION_WELDED`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- Restore (loud, by design): `LAYOUT_RESTORE_SCHEMA, LAYOUT_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_TEMPLATE, LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED, LAYOUT_ROT_UNSUPPORTED, LAYOUT_RESTORE_COUNTER` (a counter is not greater than its largest id), `LAYOUT_RESTORE_DANGLING` (a weld, socket or body reference is missing).
- Player-origin door close (not Layout's code, not Layout's token): `OBSTRUCTED` (Interaction IA-24; PCI:650's `BLOCKED` wording is a routed refresh); system-origin: `CLOSING_BLOCKED`, retried by Interaction's `attempts[]` (LY-11).
Two further reasons were proposed by the systems-designer — `BODY_FULL` (a merged body would exceed a per-body section cap) and `OFFSET_OFF_GRID` on `can_attach` — and are **not** added to the closed set: `OFFSET_OFF_GRID` is unreachable by construction (24-grid arithmetic over `GRID_24`-linted inputs, LY-3 d, D.6), and `BODY_FULL` is raised as a question in Open Questions pending a `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` cap, so the set stays the one the rulings fixed.

**LY-30 — STRUCTURAL lint (Layout) versus CONTENT lint (Authoring).** Layout owns the schema and the structural lint, run by the template loader in BOOTING and by the offline build tool. Each rule is an ERROR unless marked WARN; a template that fails an ERROR is rejected whole in BOOTING, never partially loaded.

| Area | Lint ids (reason names) | Severity | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Schema | `SCHEMA_TYPE_RANGE`, `RECT_FLAGS_ILLEGAL`, `RECT_OVERLAP`, `RECT_INDEX_DENSE`, `RECT_STRIDE_OVERFLOW`, `LAYOUT_ROT_UNSUPPORTED` | ERROR | every field type and range; flag legality (LY-7); no overlapping SOLID rects (after the socket-span compile of LY-15, so a plate never overlaps its run); dense unique `idx` over **static + leaf + socket-variant** slots; `N_static + N_doors + 2 × N_hatched_sockets <= RECT_KEY_STRIDE` (the section-id bound is in `can_build`, D.9); `rot_q == 0` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Grid | `GRID_24` | ERROR | every px on the 24 grid (the leaf's moving edge exempt) |
| Rooms | `ROOM_SHAPE`, `ROOM_NOT_CONNECTED`, `TILE_IN_TWO_ROOMS`, `ROOM_RUN_OVERLAP`, `V_RANGE`, `EXPOSED_RANGE` (ERROR); `ROOM_LONG_AXIS_OVER_TRAIL_BOUND` (WARN) | ERROR / WARN | LY-5; `ROOM_RUN_OVERLAP`: two `tile_runs` of one room share a tile (D.3 counts each tile once, so a naive run-length sum would count it twice; the overlap is an authoring error, not a tolerated redundancy) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `V` and `exposed_tiles` ranges cited from the Graph (LY-24). The WARN reports a room whose long axis in tiles exceeds `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX / TILE_PX` (derived, 24): it is advisory only, because the real bound is on a polyline and is already the ERROR `TRAIL_EDGE_OVER_BOUND` (Anchors row) — a long room with short runs is legal |
| Frame fit | `ROOM_EXCEEDS_FRAME` | WARN | a room's bounding box exceeds the soft frame limits `ROOM_DEPTH_SOFT_MAX` / `ROOM_WIDTH_SOFT_MAX`; silent with `frame_exception` (the machine room is the one type expected to exceed it, art bible 8.2); never rejects |
| Walls and doors | `WALL_RUN_DEPTH`, `DOOR_WIDTH`, `DOOR_AREA_MAX`, `DOOR_SILL_UNOWNED`, `DOOR_SILL_ROOM_INVALID`, `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR` (`DOOR_AT_CORNER`, `DOOR_ASSEMBLY_OVERLAP`, `DOOR_NEAR_SOCKET`), `DOOR_APPROACH_CLEAR`, `HATCH_HINGE_AUTHORED`, `LOOSE_ON_DOOR` | ERROR | LY-8, LY-9, LY-11; `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR` applies to **interior doors only** — a socket hatch's pocket is presentation (LY-15 e) and is exempt *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Connectivity | `ROOM_NO_EXIT`, `ROOM_UNREACHABLE`, `ENTRY_MISSING`, `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED`, `CONTAINMENT_LEAK` | ERROR | every room has a door or hatch (a one-room template is exempt from `ROOM_NO_EXIT` and `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED`, LY-4 pod note) and is reachable from `entry_room_key` treating doors as traversable regardless of state; a 48×48 square can travel between every pair of door/hatch approach squares inside the floor touching no SOLID rect (the art bible's rule 8 made exact on the 24 lattice); a flood-fill of the floor through non-SOLID cells never reaches a cell outside rooms, door gaps and FREE-hatch interiors (a hull opening with no solid rect is a walk-out into space) |
| Sockets | `SOCKET_ON_EXTERIOR`, `SOCKET_SPACING`, `SOCKET_APPROACH_FREE`, `SOCKET_COUNT`, `LANE_UNRESOLVED`, `HATCH_WIDTH`, `HATCH_ALIGN`, `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` | ERROR | LY-13, LY-14: hatch in a HULL run and the whole span inside one authored run; `HATCH_WIDTH`: a hatch has `width_tiles >= 2`; `HATCH_ALIGN`: a hatch's `doorway_start_px` equals the socket's `start_px` (low-aligned, LY-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; clear straight hull between socket spans (`SOCKET_SPACING_MIN_TILES`); the outward half-tile in front of a socket holds no rect of its own section; sockets per section within `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION`; every lane resolves to a Graph-template node and port; `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` (routed from Assembly ASK L2, MSA-21): the socket's `WELD_SOCKET` subject anchor leaves at least one legal stance — a 16 × 8 foot box (registry `foot_box`) touching no SOLID occupied span, within `REACH_PX` 72 of the anchor and at least `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` 8 (Assembly's, cited) outside both the socket's hatch rectangle and its mirror across the seam line (the mate's hatch after an abut weld) |
| Hardpoints and plates | `HARDPOINT_NODE_MISSING`, `HARDPOINT_NO_COMPATIBLE`, `HARDPOINT_LOAD_UNFILTERED`, `HARDPOINT_CELL_NO_FIT`, `PLATE_OUTSIDE_HULL`, `OUTER_ROOM_HULL_RUNS`, `SEGMENT_TILE_SHARED` | ERROR | LY-20, LY-21; `compatible_defs ≥ 1`; a LOAD hardpoint has `accepted_subtypes`; the cell fits its mount |
| Anchors and trails | `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE`, `POLY_DIAGONAL`, `TRAIL_EDGE_OVER_BOUND`, `WALL_FITTING_NO_RUN`, `FLOOR_RUN_CROSSES_BOUNDARY`, `CORRIDOR_HEAD_NARROW`, `ANCHOR_HOST_CAP_ONLY`, `CONSOLE_ROLE_UNSET` | ERROR | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `ANCHOR_HOST_CAP_ONLY`: a `WALL_FACE` anchor or a wall fitting hosted on a `CAP_ONLY` run (a HULL run on a room's south edge, or `face_variant CAP_ONLY`) is rejected because the run has no face to draw it on; a HULL_SEGMENT plate, an S-facing hatch or a seam bead on such a run raises the WARN `PLATE_HOST_CAP_ONLY` (their rendering on a cap-only run is routed to the art-director, OQ-15); anchors at least `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` from every occluding occupied span (D.2); axis-aligned polylines; `edge_len_px ≤ TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` (rejected, never shortened); the 144-px head clearance at sev-4-capable anchors (D.8) |
| Placement | `HULL_SOUTH_NOT_CAP_ONLY`, `PARTITION_FACE_HIDES_FLOOR`, `SOUTH_BAND_OCCUPIED` (ERROR); `SOUTH_BAND_DOOR_FACE` (info) | ERROR | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the south-band rule is arithmetic, not a spike unknown; Pipeline PR-5 states that a wall hides 1.5 tiles = 72 rows of the floor north of its footprint.)* D.14: every HULL run on a room's south edge is built **cap-only** (PR-5 kit consequence), so it has no band; an east-west PARTITION run with room floor on its north side hides that floor by draw order unless it declares `face_variant CAP_ONLY` or its band is declared non-walkable (every band cell covered by a SOLID rect); no interactable anchor, loose slot or floor fixture may lie in a band (the columns of the run's own doorways excepted, where the door cell replaces the wall face, reported as info `SOUTH_BAND_DOOR_FACE`) |
| Capacity | `CAPACITY_NODES`, `CAPACITY_EDGES`, `SUBJECT_ID_RANGE`, `WORLD_TILES_NEAR_CEILING`, `WORLD_TILES_OVER_CEILING`, `BODIES_LIVE_MARGIN` (WARN: the world's worst-case live-body count reaches `min(MAX_BODIES, BODIES_LIVE_MAX)`; the MVP world reports margin 0, 4 against 4) | ERROR / WARN | `nodes_in_template` against `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 / edges against 8192 (ERROR above the cap, WARN within 10 %, D.7); `subject_id < 2^20`; `Σ V ≤ TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600 over the loaded set (WARN at 90 %). The Graph's 800-node MVP template bound (LSG:915) is Authoring's CONTENT lint, WARN at most, cited here and not enforced by Layout *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Slots | `STORY_SLOT_LIST` | ERROR | the story-slot list is present (may be empty) |

**Which state each lint evaluates (variant slots PLATE, JAMB and LEAF overlap geometrically, so a literal "no overlapping SOLID" test over every slot would fire on every hatched socket).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (1) `RECT_OVERLAP` runs over the **static rects only** (walls after the socket-span compile, props, machinery); variant slots are excluded and their mutual overlap (PLATE against JAMB against LEAF) is by design. (2) `GEOM_OVERLAP` (D.6) uses static rects with each PLATE at its full footprint, as D.6 already states, and never reads leaf state. (3) The Connectivity lints `ROOM_UNREACHABLE`, `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED` (D.17) and `CONTAINMENT_LEAK` (D.20) evaluate **leaves OPEN and hatched sockets `FREE`** (PLATE on, JAMB off, LEAF absent). (4) `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE` (D.2) measures against every occluding occupied span in the **closed-leaf, `FREE`-socket** state (the worst case for an anchor beside a door). Each lint states its state in its own rule text; none evaluates a socket in `WELDED`, `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN` (those are runtime states, AC-63).

**CONTENT** rules are Derelict Authoring's, not Layout's: the art bible's visual rules 1-8 (continuity, one trail per source, hero cap, one residual light per wreck, seams only at joins, intrusion only in breached rooms, pipe band clear, clear path), the dead-module convention (IDB-24), the part ledger, the lamp counts, the escape-route budget (supplied the read `route_length_px`), the connectivity cycle count, and the volume guidance by room type *(Derelict Authoring is designed)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Fixture counts are CONTENT, Authoring's** (EM:328 "Authoring owns placements and the lint"; consumer: Emitter AC-58, EM:1091): lint ids `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM` and `FIXTURE_COUNT_WORLD`, per-room bound minimum 1 and maximum `min(6, 1 + floor(V/8))` (D.16), the Emitter's world total of 28 binding first. Layout supplies only `V`; the formula is provisional until the Emitter owner records it in AC-58. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Dock-room glow placement is CONTENT, Authoring's** (X2): every pod-dock room (`entry_room_key`) places the docked-podcraft prop's self-powered status glow, linted by Authoring; Layout supplies only `entry_room_key`, `V` and the prop's anchor read, and the glow counts in the fixture bound (D.16).
