A new lint id is added here first. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

#### 1.6 PROJECTOR, determinism, pillars, scope

**LY-31 — The PROJECTOR contract.** Layout's data module is scene-tree free (kernel H10): a headless test asserts it references no `Node`. A **separate PROJECTOR** (subscriber class `PROJECTOR`, CR-10: "write engine-side *derived* objects owned by its block owner … never writes blocks"; kernel example "Layout → physics projection; derelict content build") builds engine-side derived objects from `layout.state` and the templates; they are never persisted and are rebuilt from the block, also after restore. The object shape (the [UNV] default and the weld spike that measures it) is in the engine feasibility note and ADR-LAY-03, not in this contract. It builds the door, prop and machinery sprites from the rect table and the seam bead, cap plate and scar pieces from `welds[]` and socket state. It fills the Pipeline's PR-5 layer stack (floor z 0 never Y-sorted, floor marks, ONE Y-sort `Node2D` holding in tree order wall-face, wall-mounted, doors, props, machinery, actors; wall caps) and gives every placed SPRITE object (door leaf, prop, machinery, bead or plate piece) the Pipeline's **`entity_id`** (PIPE D.12, PIPE:553: persistent, owned by the state block, stable across save and reload; the third key of `(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)`, PIPE:1139). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `creation_id` is retired; the Pipeline has no such field.)* **Layout's `entity_id` (publisher-defined, G1; the namespace tag is registered with the Pipeline, Open Questions OQ-LY-ID):** a block-tagged int64, `entity_id = (LAYOUT_ID_NS << 56) | (section_id << 24) | (kind_ordinal << 16) | template_local_idx`, with `LAYOUT_ID_NS` a Layout namespace byte in `1 … 127` (GDScript `int` is signed 64-bit, so a byte of 128 or more would make every Layout `entity_id` negative; the Pipeline compares ids as plain ints and only relative order inside one namespace matters, but the range is fixed so the sign is never in question) that Body, Interaction and any other id publisher must not use in its own high bits (so a Layout id can never equal a Body or Interaction id in the same `class_rank`), `section_id` 32 bits at most (`next_section_id` is monotonic and tombstones keep their ids), `kind_ordinal` 8 bits, `template_local_idx` 16 bits. It is a pure function of the block (`section_id`) and the template, so it is identical in a live build and a restored one, and it is never persisted by the PROJECTOR. **Tiles carry no `entity_id`:** `TileMapLayer` cells are not nodes; wall-face and cap tiles are `class_rank` 0 and tie-break among themselves by cell position `(y, x)` (the engine's own order for a `TileMapLayer`, Y-sort mechanism per SP-YSORT-1 below). Within `class_rank` 2 the doors, props and machinery of one `sort_y` are ordered by `entity_id` alone (they share one rank in the Pipeline; Layout assumes no sub-rank). The *mechanism* that delivers the key (`TileMapLayer` participation, who owns child insertion order) is not defined here and stays gated on SP-YSORT-1. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* It writes no block and creates **no engine tile collision and no navigation mesh in MVP**. It never creates `Light2D` or `LightOccluder2D` (Lighting's). Its section nodes are parented under Body's per-body root `Node2D` (Body's `body.projector` owns the root and its translation, BDY-33) — the default shape, which the Y-sort spike below may supersede with option B (no reparent; BDY-33 would then own the pose-driven section positions rather than a root). (→ ADR-LAY-03.) The PROJECTOR must satisfy "never hidden" (art bible 6.1) for seam beads. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Prerequisite story — the Y-sort / PROJECTOR scene-shape spike (SP-YSORT-1), done before the PROJECTOR story starts.** The default shape above reparents section nodes under a per-body root on every weld, which sits badly with the Pipeline's single Y-sort scope (PR-5: ONE Y-sort `Node2D` holding wall-face, wall-mounted, doors, props, machinery and actors in tree order) and is [UNV]. The spike tests **option B first**: *no reparent* — every section's nodes keep one parent under the single Y-sort node and each section's position is driven from the body's pose plus `section_offset` every tick (so a weld changes an offset, never a parent) — and falls back to option A (per-body roots, reparent on weld) only if B fails. **Exit criteria:** (1) the PR-5 draw order of AC-123 holds with two bodies in one scene and across a weld (an actor south of a wall over its face, north of it behind it, the cap above both); (2) no sprite changes sort rank across the weld frame; (3) draw calls for the MVP world are recorded for both options; (4) the weld frame costs at most one frame over the P99 ceiling (16.6 ms on RM-DESKTOP, 33.3 ms on RM-DECK; pending spike measurement) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*; (5) the result is written to ADR-LAY-03. Until the spike closes, AC-140 has no pass line.

**LY-32 — Dirty keys and notification.** Layout's batch `(layout.state, tick, change_keys)` carries `SEC/<section_id>`, `BODY/<body_id>`, `S/<seam_id>`, or `ALL` past `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256. **Encoding** (registry: `layout_dirty_key_encoding`)**:** Layout's keys are ints `(KIND << 32) | id` with KIND in {SEC 1, BODY 2, SEAM 3} (the Graph's LS-20 encoding, LSG:201), written in this document by family name; the family name `S/<seam_id>` is the Graph's (LS-20) and Failure & Trail's (FT:264, FT AC-107), so `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` accounting is comparable across blocks. Dirty keys are namespaced by block (CR-10; KRN:465 bounds them per block), so Layout's `SEC/<id>` and the Graph's `SEC/<id>` (LSG:170) are different keys in different blocks; every subscriber names the block, and subscribers that need both (Failure & Trail anchors, FT AC-107) subscribe to both.

**LY-33 — Determinism, hashing, seam age.** `layout_hash` is the kernel's `block_hash` of `layout.state` (D.10, kernel D.12); the kernel's `state_hash` folds it unchanged and derived caches (`version`, grids, leaves) are excluded *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)*. **`layout_hash()` does not cover door state** (Horror C-HE-13): `area` and `door_state` are the Graph's and hash in `graph.state`, and the leaf rects are excluded derived caches; a reader caching on door geometry keys on `version(section_id)`, which `layout.doors` bumps whenever a leaf changes (LY-12 d). All iteration is over dense arrays in ascending id order, never a Dictionary. Layout draws no RNG in MVP (`rng_derive` unused). `welded_at_T` is the only time value stored. **Seam bead age is DERIVED, never a flag:** `age_class = OLD` iff `T − welded_at_T ≥ SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` (D.11); the 4-px Hull Grey bead (art bible 8.2), its straight/corner/T and fresh/old variants, and the cap plate are PRESENTATION pieces on the wall-mounted layer.

**LY-34 — Pillars as tests; no lie in geometry.** *P1 (primary):* one rectangle set for collision, light and drawing; no "perceived" variant of any query; a phantom door is the Perception Filter's presentation and is falsified by the rect. Layout carries no phantom-geometry field, no "false door" record, no sanity input and no per-player variant of any table; a flag named hallucinated, fake or perceived is struck on sight. **Granted (Perception Filter PF-5 row 11, grade TRUTH_ST; OQ-13 resolved):** wall faces, door leaves (ordinary doors and hatches alike) and socket/seam geometry are truth-channel — the Filter's rule, which Layout accepts; Layout's narrower fallback was recorded there, not adopted (OQ-PF-14). *P5:* seams and scars persist as geometry (LY-16, LY-17). *P3, P4:* indirect only. *P2:* no Layout mechanic. *Anti-pillars:* no runtime "place a wall / draw a room" capability exists; sections attach only at pre-declared sockets; no blueprint export or import (SL:120).

**LY-35 — Not owned (scope guard).** Layout does not own: `door_state`, opening `area`, VALVE `open`, breaker `closed`, atmosphere, node/edge state, failure (Graph); pose, velocity, `LocomotionStep`, the foot box (Body / Controller); the occlusion test, the cone test, `Light2D` / `LightOccluder2D`, shadow slots (Emitter / Lighting); weld/cut/install commands, `q`, the preview cursor (Assembly) and `can_join` (Graph); trail stamps, extents, dullness (Failure & Trail); the loose-item table (Interaction, LY-22); a `visited` or "explored" bit — `layout.state` is WORLD-scoped and rolls back on `request_restore` (CR-8, KRN:855), so a bit here would reset too; campaign-level "first entry" knowledge belongs to a PROFILE-scoped owner or Web Map & Transit's world model (Save/Load SL:825 names the alternative source), Layout supplies only the pure `room_at` and routes the owner question to Open Questions; the def vocabulary and the `compatible_defs` body (Item DB); the world list, seeds, deltas, loaded set (Web Map & Transit); authored floor plans and the room-kit art (Derelict Authoring, art-director); the camera and zoom (Pipeline); procgen grammar and ART 6.3 rules 1-8 (Procedural Derelict Generation, Authoring); sanity, hallucination and perceived values (none exist in Layout); UI (see UI Requirements). `door_passage` is derived door geometry (D.5), not door state: Layout still owns neither `area` nor `door_state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

**Engine feasibility (Godot 4.7.2, GDScript).** *Default PROJECTOR shape [UNV] (→ ADR-LAY-03; the spike SP-YSORT-1 of LY-31 tests the no-reparent option B first, so read this default as the fallback, option A):* per-section `TileMapLayer` nodes parented under Body's per-body root `Node2D` (owned by Body's PROJECTOR `body.projector`, BDY-33; Layout's PROJECTOR creates the section nodes); one map per body is what the weld spike measures (batching and Y-sort under reparent are [UNV]). `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` holds animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui, and has **no `scripting.md`, `core.md`, `tilemap.md` or `2d.md`**: `TileMapLayer` behaviour (batching, Y-sort under reparent, rotation, collision), `var_to_bytes` determinism and GDScript packed-array cost are **NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference** and are tagged [UNV] where used. Knowledge gaps: **HIGH** — merged-versus-per-section `TileMapLayer` batching cost and Y-sort behaviour when sections are reparented under a moving body node (the 50-300-draw figure is NOT SOURCEABLE until the weld spike); **HIGH** — the Y-sort tie rule (tree order) [UNV], which is why the Pipeline's `(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)` key exists and Layout supplies a stable `entity_id` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was `creation_id`)*; **MEDIUM** — `var_to_bytes` determinism and typed-container encoding of the snapshot dictionary [UNV] (SL-2); **MEDIUM** — `TileMapLayer` rotation, collision and navigation behaviour in 4.6/4.7 (Layout deliberately uses no engine tile collision and no navigation mesh, so this blocks only a later PROJECTOR-to-physics decision, which is Body's); **MEDIUM** — GDScript cost of `rects_in` over about 2,000 rects with a uniform grid against the Controller's 0.05 ms per actor per tick budget (spike). Verified from the engine reference: the 4.7 packed-array element-write change (LY-27) and the 4.7 typed-return override change (an override of a method with a typed return now inherits that type and needs an explicit `return`, which matters to any abstract `OccluderSource` base). `@abstract` (4.5) exists and may serve the `OccluderSource` seam; verify before use. `TileMapLayer` replaced `TileMap` in 4.3 (one node per layer). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### States and Transitions

Four state machines: the kernel-visible **block** (2.1), the **section instance** lifecycle (2.2), the **socket** (2.3), and the **body and weld rows** with the two *derived* states (2.4). Only the first three are persisted; derived states are recomputed and never stored. "Who may cause" names the only legal caller; a transition with no listed cause does not exist.

**2.1 Block states (kernel-visible).**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UNREGISTERED` | process start | composition-root registration of `layout.state` | → `BOOTING` | the composition root (kernel CR-8) |
| `BOOTING` | registration | templates loaded and structural-linted (LY-30) | → `EMPTY` (new game) or `RESTORING` (load); a template that fails an ERROR lint aborts boot with the lint id | the kernel's boot sequence |
| `EMPTY` | `default_state()`: no bodies, sections, welds; counters at 1 | the first successful `build_section` | → `LIVE` | Web Map & Transit's `init_world(world_seed)` (SL-18; provisional) |
| `RESTORING` | a load request | swap succeeds or fails | → `LIVE` (ok; the instance table replaces the live one whole); → `LIVE` with the PRE-restore state untouched and a LY-29 restore reason (fail) | Save/Load via the kernel (`restore`); never Layout itself |
| `LIVE` | `init_world` or a successful restore | a `FAULTED` event | queries answer, mutators accept; leaf-dependent queries are valid only after `post_restore` (LY-26 c) | LM1-LM4 through a kernel WriteScope only |
| `FAULTED` | an `apply` defect (CR-9: validate-then-apply, no rollback) | the kernel owns the exit | mutators stop; reads still answer | the kernel |

**2.2 Section-instance lifecycle (derived from socket states; persisted: the row and the `discarded` flag).**

| State | Definition | Entry | Exit | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UNBUILT` | template only; no row | initial | `build_section` (LM1) | the orchestrating apply (LY-25) |
| `BUILT` | row exists, every socket `FREE` | LM1 | LM2 on one of its sockets → `ATTACHED`; LM4 → `DISCARDED` | Assembly (LM2); Web Map only (LM4; Assembly never discards, MSA-24) |
| `ATTACHED` | at least one socket `WELDED` | LM2 | LM3 on its last `WELDED` seam → `DETACHED` | Assembly |
| `DETACHED` | no socket `WELDED`, at least one `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN` (history exists) | LM3 | LM2 on a still-`FREE` socket → `ATTACHED`; LM4 → `DISCARDED` | Assembly |
| `DISCARDED` | tombstone: ids retained, rects gone | LM4 from `BUILT` or `DETACHED` | terminal | Web Map only, after the Graph's M4 (Assembly never discards, MSA-24) |

A section in `ATTACHED` cannot be discarded (`SECTION_WELDED`). A dormant section is the Graph's flag (LS-17); Layout never mirrors it, and a dormant section is simply not built into the scene (Web Map decides what is loaded).

**2.3 Socket state (persisted, sparse: only non-`FREE` sockets have a row).**

| From | To | Trigger | Condition | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FREE` | `WELDED(seam_id)` | LM2 | `can_attach` passed (LY-14) and the Graph's `can_join` passed | Assembly `weld` |
| `WELDED` | `CAPPED` | LM3, `boundary_mode` SEALED | the seam is ACTIVE | Assembly `seam_cut` |
| `WELDED` | `CUT_OPEN` | LM3, `boundary_mode` RAGGED | the seam is ACTIVE | Assembly `seam_cut` |
| `CAPPED`, `CUT_OPEN` | (none) | n/a | never returns to `FREE` in MVP (LY-13) | nobody — no mutator exists |

**2.4 Body, weld rows, and the derived states.**

| Object | States | Notes | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body (persisted) | `LIVE` → `DISSOLVED` | dissolved by LM2 (the absorbed body) or LM4 (a discarded section's sole body); the id is never reused | LM2, LM4 |
| Body creation | a new `LIVE` row | by LM1 (every built section starts on its own new body at offset (0, 0); a multi-section body exists only through LM2 welds) or LM3 (the split-off component) | LM1, LM3 |
| Weld row (persisted) | `ACTIVE` → `CUT_SEALED` / `CUT_RAGGED` | rows are history and persist (Pillar 5) | LM2, LM3 |
| Door leaf (derived, not persisted) | `ABSENT` (`leaf_w` 0) · `PARTIAL` (**Vertical Slice only, unreachable in the MVP**: the MVP door is binary, LY-11, so `0 < leaf_w < width_px` never occurs; *amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*) · `COVERING` (`leaf_w` = `width_px`) | follows the Graph's `area` each tick (LY-9, LY-10) | the Graph (via `area`); Layout only projects |
| Hatch (derived) | `HULL` (socket `FREE`, `CAPPED`, `CUT_OPEN`, or `WELDED` one-sided: the PLATE slot active) → `LEAF` (socket `WELDED` two-sided: the JAMB and LEAF slots active, the PLATE inactive) | LY-15 variant matrix; a state change switches slots and never adds or removes an `idx` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | LM2, LM3 |
| Seam bead age (derived) | `FRESH` → `OLD` | `T − welded_at_T ≥ SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` (D.11); a restore rolls `T` and the rows back together, so age never contradicts itself | time (`kernel.clock`) |

### Interactions with Other Systems

Disk state (Glob of `design/gdd/`, re-checked at advisor review 2026-10-26; Interaction System and Suit & Equipment relabelled at the final advisor review 2026-10-27): **designed** — Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Item & Module Database, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Failure & Trail System, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Interaction System, Suit & Equipment (the last two now carry `Status: Designed`; any stale Layout wording in their rows is a refresh routed in Cross-References B). *At authoring time no file existed* for Ship Body & Interior Physics, Modular Ship Assembly, Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits), Boarding & Salvage, Web Map & Transit, Attention, Horror Entities, Sanity, Perception Filter, Procedural Derelict Generation. *Re-checked 2026-10-27 (consistency pass) and again in the design review: every system in that list except Procedural Derelict Generation has a designed GDD on disk, as do Adaptive Audio, In-World Instruments & Inspect View, HUD & Suit Layer and Debug Overlay; the rows below read as designed, the word "provisional" survives only where a contract is genuinely still open, and **Procedural Derelict Generation is the one system still undesigned**.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Each contract is stated as what Layout SUPPLIES, TAKES and OWNS so the neighbour can repeat it (the Graph's LSG:1140 and the Item DB's AC-63 pattern: each provisional row "appears in the dependent GDD when it is written, or a conflict is raised to the producer"). Each designed neighbour's own row naming Layout is **quoted** below; this GDD's row is its bidirectional counterpart.

**3.3 Atomic world operations — the call order Layout takes part in.** Each is one command whose `apply` runs under one kernel WriteScope (CR-9, validate-then-apply); every row's orchestrating system is now designed (Web Map & Transit, Modular Ship Assembly, Interaction). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

| Operation | Validate (pure; every verdict must pass) | Apply order | Layout's part |
|---|---|---|---|
| World build (`init_world`; a pod hop builds nothing) | `can_build` (`TEMPLATE_UNKNOWN`, `CAPACITY`) + the Graph's M1 `CAPACITY` check | Layout `build_section` → Graph M1 (inside LM1) → Emitter E1 → Interaction `sync_section` → PROJECTOR notification | LM1 |
| Weld | Layout `can_attach` and Graph `can_join` | Graph `weld_join` → Layout `attach_section` → Body reprojection (`BODY_TRANSFER`, PIPE:694) | LM2 |
| Cut | Layout `can_detach` and the Graph's cut precondition | Graph `cut_detach` → Layout `detach_section` → Body reprojection | LM3 |
| Discard | Layout `can_discard` and the Graph's M4 precondition | Graph `discard_section` → Layout `discard_section_geometry` | LM4 |
| Door open / close | Interaction `validate` (the owner's reject token `OBSTRUCTED` via `leaf_would_overlap`) | Graph M13 `set_opening_area` (P2; Interaction submits it as a SYSTEM command applied at T+1, LY-10 i) → `layout.doors` re-derives the leaf in the same tick's P3 | LY-10, LY-11 |

## Formulas

Conventions. Everything in this section is integer arithmetic: nothing here is hashed or replicated as a float, because nothing here is a float (LY-3 a, kernel determinism rule). `floor_div(a, b)` for `b > 0` is mathematical floor, `floor_div(a, b) = (a − posmod(a, b)) / b` with `posmod(a, b)` in `[0, b)`; truncating division is forbidden (LY-3 f; that GDScript integer `/` truncates toward zero is [UNV] — NOT SOURCEABLE in `docs/engine-reference/godot/`, which has no scripting module — so the posmod spelling is the safe one). `ceil_div(a, b) = floor_div(a + b − 1, b)`. Locked constants are cited by name with their registry value and never restated differently: `TILE_PX` 48, `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24, `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` 1, `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` 1152, `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 (edges 8192), `TICK_RATE` 60, `V_WALK` 2 and `V_RUN` 3 px per tick, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s. All lengths are base px, section-local unless a formula says "body frame", and none contains `k`, `s` or `field_h` (LY-3 e). The rule or lint that cites each formula is named in its lead-in. Fixtures are schema examples, not authored content: **`fixture_cryo_excerpt`** is the cryo bay of the technical-director's fixture (`origin_px` (48, 96), `tile_runs` `ty` 0..5 × `tx` 0..6, floor `x` in `[48, 384)` × `y` in `[96, 384)`, walls N `[24, 408) × [72, 96)` HULL, S `[24, 408) × [384, 408)` PARTITION, W `[24, 48) × [96, 384)` HULL, E `[384, 408) × [96, 384)` PARTITION, door `door_cryo_s` in the S wall, heater hardpoint `hp_cryo_heater`, coolant anchor `anc_cryo_coolant`, lamp anchor `fx_cryo_lamp`) and the airlock with socket `sock_airlock_e` (airlock `origin_px` (576, 240), `tile_runs` `ty` 0..1 × `tx` 0..1, E wall `[672, 696) × [240, 336)`, socket `line_px` 696, `start_px` 240), mated to a derelict-B socket (orchestrator ruling: this fixture, re-expressed per the lattice ruling, is canonical; the level-designer's fixture with its north wall at `[0, 24)` is not used because it drives the wall-face rows negative against LY-3 b).

### D.1 Tile–pixel conversion

The tile–pixel conversion formula is defined as:

`tile(x) = floor_div(x, TILE_PX)` · `px(t) = t × TILE_PX` · `half(x) = floor_div(x, WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x` | int (px) | −2^20 … 2^20 (section-local `≥ 0` by authoring; body frame may be negative after a detach re-base, LY-3 b) | caller | a coordinate on one axis |
| `t` | int (tile) | −21,845 … 21,845 | derived | a tile index on one axis |
| `TILE_PX` | int const | 48 | registry (REG:1269) | floor tile edge |
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | int const | 24 | registry (REG:1516) | half-tile / wall depth |

**Output Range:** `tile` and `half` are total over the integer range (no clamp); `px(tile(x)) ≤ x < px(tile(x) + 1)` always holds.

**Example:** `tile(407) = 8`, `tile(432) = 9`, `px(9) = 432`, `half(432) = 18`. At a negative body-frame coordinate: `tile(−1) = −1`, `tile(−48) = −1`, `tile(−49) = −2` — truncating division would give 0 for `−1`, a defect at any detached section whose offset is negative (LY-3 f).

### D.2 Rectangle algebra: occupied span, overlap, adjacency, clearance

The rectangle algebra formula (registry: `rect_algebra_closed_span`) is defined as:

`S(R) = [x0, x1−1] × [y0, y1−1]` · `overlap(A, B) = A.x0 < B.x1 ∧ B.x0 < A.x1 ∧ A.y0 < B.y1 ∧ B.y0 < A.y1` · `d(p, R) = max( max(S.x0 − p.x, p.x − S.x1, 0), max(S.y0 − p.y, p.y − S.y1, 0) )` · anchor legal iff `d(p, R) ≥ ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` for every `OCCLUDES_LIGHT` rect `R` · a caller box `F` is legal against a `SOLID` rect `R` iff `¬overlap(F, R)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R` = `(x0, y0, x1, y1)` | 4 × int (px) | `x1 > x0`, `y1 > y0` for a returned rect; all on the 24 grid except a leaf's moving edge (LY-7) | template / derived leaf | a stored half-open rect `[x0, x1) × [y0, y1)` over pixel cells (LY-12 b) |
| `S(R)` | 4 × int (px) | derived | derived | the occupied closed span; what `rects_in` returns (LY-12 b) |
| `p` | int pair (px) | frame of the caller | caller | an anchor or a ray origin |
| `F` | 4 × int (px) | half-open | caller (Body's read view) | an opaque foot box; Layout never defines its placement (LY-11; Open Questions) |
| `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` | int const | 1 (range 1–2) | registry (REG:2454), re-sourced to this GDD | minimum Chebyshev distance of an anchor from every occluding span |

**Output Range:** `overlap` is boolean (abutting rects, `A.x1 == B.x0`, do NOT overlap); `d ≥ 0`, and `d = 0` means on or inside the closed span.

**Example (C-1 wall):** `R = [408, 432) × [96, 288)` has `S(R)` columns 408..431. Anchor `(407, 200)` has `d = 1` (legal); `(408, 200)` and `(431, 200)` have `d = 0` (fail `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE`); `(432, 200)` has `d = 1` (legal). **Example (boundary fixture):** a wall `R = [0, 480) × [240, 264)` occupies rows 240..263. A caller box `[264, 272)` in rows (and `[100, 116)` in columns) does not overlap it — `264 < 264` is false — and its origin row 264 has `d = 1`, so the beam from it is not blocked at origin and the box is legal; a caller box `[260, 268)` in rows overlaps (`260 < 264`) and is a Body collision defect, never a sorting case (Pipeline D.12). **Example (overlap):** `A = [408, 432) × [96, 288)`, `B = [432, 456) × [96, 288)`: `432 < 432` false, no overlap; `B′ = [431, 455) × [96, 288)`: overlaps.

### D.3 Room volume and tile id

The room volume formula (registry: `room_volume`, which also covers D.4 `exposed_tiles`) is defined as:

`V(room) = |room.tiles|` = the **de-duplicated** count of distinct `(tx, ty)` in the union of the room's `tile_runs`, which equals `Σ over tile_runs (tx1 − tx0 + 1)` iff no two runs overlap (lint `ROOM_RUN_OVERLAP`; a naive run-length sum would otherwise count a shared tile twice and inflate `V`, the Graph's volume and `TILES_WORLD_MVP`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* · `tile_id(tx, ty) = index of (tx, ty) in the row-major sorted tile list` · `tile_origin_px(tx, ty) = origin_px + TILE_PX × (tx, ty)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tile_runs[]` `{ty, tx0, tx1}` | list of int triples | `tx0 ≤ tx1` (inclusive spans, 48-px tile units from `origin_px`) | template | the authored floor of one room (LY-4) |
| `origin_px` | int pair (px) | on the 24 grid (LY-5) | template | the room's own lattice origin |
| `V` | int | 4 … 200 (`ROOM_TILES_MIN`…`ROOM_TILES_MAX`, LSG:299 — cited, not redefined) | derived | tiles in the room; the Graph caches it |

**Output Range:** `V` in `[4, 200]` by lint (`V_RANGE`: a template outside it is rejected, never clamped); `tile_id` in `[0, V−1]`, dense, and equal to the Failure & Trail tie-break order (FT:383).

**Example:** `cryo_bay`: `origin_px` (48, 96), runs `ty` 0..5 each `tx` 0..6, so `V = 6 × 7 = 42` (≥ 36, the Failure & Trail 18-patch fixture, FT:383). `tile_id(0, 0) = 0`, `tile_id(1, 0) = 1`, `tile_id(0, 1) = 7`; `tile_origin_px(0, 1) = (48, 144)`. The airlock: `origin_px` (576, 240), runs `ty` 0..1 each `tx` 0..1, `V = 4` (the Graph's minimum). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Overlap example:** a row with runs `tx` 0..3 and `tx` 2..5 holds 6 distinct tiles, so `V` contributes 6, not the naive 8; the template raises `ROOM_RUN_OVERLAP` and is rejected.

### D.4 Exposed tiles

The exposed-tiles formula is defined as:

`exposed_tiles(room) = Σ len_tiles(w)` over wall runs `w` with `w.kind == HULL` and (`w.room_key_a == room` or `w.room_key_b == room`) and the other side `EXTERIOR`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `w.kind` | enum | `HULL`, `PARTITION` | template | whether the run faces the exterior |
| `len_tiles(w)` | int | 1 … `SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` | template | floor-facing tiles of the run, excluding the 24-px corner cells (LY-8 a) — the N wall footprint `[24, 408)` is 384 px but counts 7; counted on the **authored** run, never on the compiled rects, so the LY-15 socket-span split (before / span / after) and a weld, cut or hatch state never change it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `exposed_tiles` | int | 0 … 80 (lint `EXPOSED_RANGE`, LSG:452 — cited) | derived at build, static | the count the Graph's thermal loss uses |

**Output Range:** `[0, 80]` by lint; static per room instance, so a weld never changes it (LY-24). Socket frames and plate positions lie inside HULL runs (lint `OUTER_ROOM_HULL_RUNS`, `PLATE_OUTSIDE_HULL`), so each exterior tile is counted once.

**Example:** `cryo_bay`: N run 7 tiles HULL, W run 6 tiles HULL, S run 7 tiles PARTITION (5 wall + the 2-tile doorway), E run 6 tiles PARTITION: `exposed_tiles = 7 + 6 = 13`. The airlock (2 × 2, hull on N, S and E, open on its west side to the interior): `exposed_tiles = 2 + 2 + 2 = 6`, of which the socket frame is 2 tiles (the conservative error of Edge Cases).

### D.5 Door leaf and passage

The door leaf formula (registry: `door_leaf_gap`) is defined as:

`gap(area) = floor_div(width_px × area, area_max)` · `leaf_w(area) = width_px − gap(area)` · `passes(F) ⇔ gap(area) ≥ F` · `area_pass_min(F) = ceil_div(F × area_max, width_px)` · `leaf_would_overlap(opening, area′, boxes) = ∃ L ∈ leaves(opening), ∃ f ∈ boxes : overlap(leaf_rect(L, area′), f)` (*Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* `leaves(opening)` is one leaf for an interior door and **both** leaves of a mated hatch, each at its section's body-frame offset, with the opening's single `area_max`)

where `leaf_rect` for a doorway starting at `s` in an E-W wall whose footprint starts at `w` is: hinge `LOW`: `[s, s + leaf_w) × [w, w + WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH)`; hinge `HIGH`: `[s + width_px − leaf_w, s + width_px) × [w, w + WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH)`; a N-S wall swaps the axes. The leaf slides INTO the pocket on the hinge side, so the visible leaf stays anchored to the hinge end of the doorway and the gap opens from the other end. `leaf_w = 0` returns no rect (its `idx` stays reserved, D.9).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `width_px` | int (px) | 96 (the 96 × 96 door cell, art bible 8.2); lint `DOOR_WIDTH` | template | doorway width |
| `area` | int | `0 … area_max` | the Graph's opening state, read through `opening(edge_id)` (LY-10; published, the ask is closed); for a mated hatch, the shared OPENING's | the opening's current `area` (the Graph states 1/64 tile, LSG:72) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `area_max` | int | `> 0`, `≤ 128`; default 64 (`A_HOLE_MAX` 64, range 32–128, LSG:323 — cited as the scale reference) | template; for a mated hatch `min(a.area_max, b.area_max)` (LY-15) | full-open value; lint `DOOR_AREA_MAX` |
| `F` | int (px) | 16 across a doorway in an E-W wall, 8 across one in a N-S wall | the foot box 16 × 8 (PCI:103), supplied by Body | foot-box extent across the doorway |
| `hinge` | enum | `LOW`, `HIGH` | template (interior door); fixed `LOW` for a socket hatch (LY-9) | pocket side |
| `leaf_w`, `gap` | int (px) | `0 … width_px`; `gap` monotone non-decreasing in `area` | derived | leaf length and open length |

**Output Range:** `leaf_w`, `gap` in `[0, 96]`; the product `width_px × area` is at most `96 × 128 = 12,288`. At `area = 0` the leaf covers the doorway; at `area = area_max` there is no rect. Because the formula has no threshold, a binary jump `0 ↔ area_max` is binary by consequence and `DOOR_LIGHT_OPEN_PM` is retired (LY-9).

**Example (`area_max` 64):** `area` 0 → `leaf_w` 96; 32 → 48; 64 → 0; 21 → `96 − floor_div(2016, 64) = 96 − 31 = 65`, `gap` 31; 10 → `gap` 15, 11 → `gap` 16. `area_pass_min(16) = ceil_div(1024, 96) = 11` (check: `gap(10) = 15 < 16`, `gap(11) = 16`) and `area_pass_min(8) = ceil_div(512, 96) = 6` (`gap(5) = 7`, `gap(6) = 9`). With `area_max` 128: `area_pass_min(16) = 22` (`gap(21) = 15`, `gap(22) = 16`) and `area_pass_min(8) = 11`. Leaf rect of `door_cryo_s` (E-W wall footprint `y` in `[384, 408)`, doorway `x` in `[240, 336)`, hinge `LOW`/west, `area_max` 64) at `area` 32: `[240, 288) × [384, 408)`, gap `[288, 336)`. **Close-safety:** closing from `area` 32 to 20 gives `leaf_w = 96 − floor_div(1920, 64) = 66`, leaf `[240, 306) × [384, 408)`; a foot box `[296, 312) × [384, 392)` overlaps it (`240 < 312`, `296 < 306`, `384 < 392`, `384 < 408`), so `leaf_would_overlap` is true and a PLAYER-origin close returns `OBSTRUCTED` (LY-11). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Mated-hatch example (the seam-straddling case):** the `fixture_airlock` weld of D.6 with `area_max` 64 (the minimum of 64 and 128): the opening's leaves are A's `[672, 696) × [240, 240 + leaf_w)` and B's `[696, 720) × [240, 240 + leaf_w)`. Closing from `area` 32 to 20 gives `leaf_w` 66, so both leaves reach `y` 306. The foot box `[696, 712) × [296, 304)` overlaps **only B's leaf** (`696 < 720`, `296 < 306`; against A's leaf `696 < 696` is false), so `leaf_would_overlap(opening, 20, [box])` is true; a check made against A's leaf alone would have returned false. (A box `[688, 704) × [296, 304)` straddling the seam overlaps both.) The (MVP) binary close tests the jump to `area` 0 the same way.

**Passage record (`door_passage`)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: `orientation` = `EW` for a door in an E-W wall, `NS` in a N-S wall. For a doorway starting at `s` in an E-W wall whose footprint starts at `w`: `sill_rect = [s, s + 96) × [w, w + 24)`; `open_extent_px = gap(area)` (0 when closed, 96 when `area = area_max`); `gap_rect`, hinge `LOW` = `[s + leaf_w, s + 96) × [w, w + 24)`, hinge `HIGH` = `[s, s + gap) × [w, w + 24)`; a N-S wall swaps the axes (`sill_rect = [w, w + 24) × [s, s + 96)`). `gap_rect` is NONE iff `open_extent_px = 0`, and equals `sill_rect` when `area = area_max`. The OUT convention is LY-12 (b)'s: every returned rect is the occupied span `(x0, y0, x1 − 1, y1 − 1)` (the half-open figures above are the stored form). The read returns NO passable flag: the caller compares `open_extent_px` with its foot extent `F` (`passes(F) ⇔ gap ≥ F`; `F` supplied by Body/Controller, PCI:103: 16 across an E-W doorway, 8 across a N-S one). **Mated hatch:** the opening's single `area_max = min(a, b)` and the fixed LOW hinge (LY-9, LY-15) apply; each section answers for its OWN leaf in its OWN section-local frame, and both answer the same `open_extent_px`. The read re-uses the leaf value `layout.doors` last projected (the same `leaf_w` that sizes the leaf rect), so collision, light and passage cannot disagree and `opening()` is not read a second time; an unknown opening reads as closed (the existing `opening_unknown` edge case), so `open_extent_px = 0`. It changes exactly when `leaf_version(section_id)` bumps (LY-12 d), so a reader's cache keyed on `version(section_id)` is correct (Horror HE-25 keys so). Cost: O(1) integer arithmetic, no `QueryBuffer`, safe in a P3 hook after `layout.doors` (`body.integrate` is `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]` and `horror.step` follows `body.integrate`, so a Horror run sees same-tick state).

**Worked values** (`door_cryo_s`, E-W, `s` 240, `w` 384, hinge LOW, `area_max` 64; `sill_rect` span (240, 384, 335, 407) in every row): `area` 0 → `open_extent_px` 0, `gap_rect` NONE; `area` 4 → `gap` floor_div(384, 64) = 6, LOW (330, 384, 335, 407), HIGH (240, 384, 245, 407); `area` 8 → 12, LOW (324, 384, 335, 407), HIGH (240, 384, 251, 407); `area` 11 → floor_div(1056, 64) = 16, LOW (320, 384, 335, 407), HIGH (240, 384, 255, 407); `area` 32 → 48, LOW (288, 384, 335, 407), HIGH (240, 384, 287, 407); `area` 64 → 96, `gap_rect` (240, 384, 335, 407). **N-S variant** (a harness-added door, as AC-52 adds `door_b`; `w` 384, `s` 240, footprint `x` in [384, 408), doorway `y` in [240, 336)): `sill_rect` span (384, 240, 407, 335); `area` 8 (12 px): LOW (384, 324, 407, 335), HIGH (384, 240, 407, 251); `area` 4 (6 px): LOW (384, 330, 407, 335), HIGH (384, 240, 407, 245). Against `F`: 6 px is passable across neither orientation, 12 px across N-S only (12 ≥ 8, 12 < 16), 16 px across both. `area_max` 128 with `area` 1 gives `open_extent_px = floor_div(96, 128) = 0`.

### D.6 Weld offset and the 24-grid closure

The weld offset formula (registry: `weld_offset`) is defined as:

`pos(socket) = (line_px, start_px)` for facing E or W, `(start_px, line_px)` for facing N or S · `offset_b = offset_a + pos_a − pos_b`, where **`a` is the survivor-side socket and `b` the absorbed-side socket, assigned by body (the body with the lower `body_id` survives, LY-16), never by argument order** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* · mate holds iff facings opposite ∧ `width_tiles` equal ∧ classes compatible ∧ lane multisets equal ∧ `¬overlap(rect_a, rect_b + offset_b − offset_a)` for every static-footprint pair (all static rects with all flags, each PLATE at its full footprint, plus room tile cells; LY-14) (`GEOM_OVERLAP`)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `offset_a`, `offset_b` | int pair (px) | on the 24 grid; `(0, 0)` at build (LY-25); may be negative after a detach re-base | the block | body-frame section origins |
| `pos_a`, `pos_b` | int pair (px) | on the 24 grid (lint `GRID_24`); local `≥ 0` | template sockets | the socket reference point (LY-13) |
| `width_tiles` | int | 1 … 12 (`L_seam`, LSG:328 — cited) | template | equal for both sockets of a mate |

**Output Range:** `offset_b` on the 24-px grid. **`frame_shift_px` (attach)** `= offset_b − offset_b_old`, the position of the absorbed body's frame origin expressed in the host body's frame (`offset_b_old` is section b's offset inside its own body before the weld, `(0, 0)` for a freshly built single-section body, LY-25); every section of the absorbed body GAINS `frame_shift_px` (`new = old + frame_shift_px`). D.12's detach `frame_shift` has the same meaning mirrored (the position of the split-off body's new frame origin in the old frame; moved sections LOSE it), so one sentence serves Body for both. **24-grid closure (one line):** every input of the sum lies on the 24 grid (`GRID_24`-linted) and `offset_a` does by induction from `(0, 0)`, so `offset_b` does — a derived property, not an input constraint, and the reason `OFFSET_OFF_GRID` is unreachable and no socket phase is needed. After translation the two hull footprints abut: A facing E occupies `[line_a − 24, line_a)` in its normal axis and B facing W occupies `[line_b, line_b + 24)`, which lands at `[offset_a + line_a, offset_a + line_a + 24)`.

**Example:** home-ship airlock socket E-facing, `pos_a = (696, 240)`, `width_tiles` 2, `offset_a = (0, 0)`; derelict B socket W-facing, `pos_b = (120, 144)`, `width_tiles` 2: `offset_b = (0 + 696 − 120, 0 + 240 − 144) = (576, 96)` — 12 tiles by 2 tiles, on the 24 grid. B's W wall footprint local `[120, 144)` lands at body `[696, 720)`, abutting A's E wall `[672, 696)`; B's hatch local `y` `[144, 240)` lands at `[240, 336)`, in line with A's. `L_seam = 2`. For this attach `offset_b_old = (0, 0)`, so `frame_shift_px = (576, 96)`: the absorbed body's section 3 moves from `(0, 0)` to `(576, 96)` in the host frame, which is the same vector D.12's cut example returns when that seam is cut again. **Reversed-argument example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: calling `attach_section(sock_b_w, sock_airlock_e, …)` — derelict B's socket first — still survives body 1 (the lower id, the home-ship), absorbs body 3, and returns the same `offset_b = (576, 96)` and `frame_shift_px = (576, 96)`; the roles `a`/`b` of the formula are the home-ship socket and B's socket in both calls. **`GEOM_OVERLAP` negative example:** a B bumper rect local `[96, 120) × [144, 192)` lands at body `[672, 696) × [240, 288)` and overlaps A's E wall `[672, 696) × [240, 336)` (`672 < 696`, `672 < 696`, `240 < 336`, `240 < 288`), so the verdict is `GEOM_OVERLAP`. A socket `start_px` of 150 never reaches this formula: it fails `GRID_24` at load.

### D.7 Capacity lint

The capacity lint formula is defined as:

`capacity_error(n, cap) = n > cap` · `capacity_warn(n, cap) = n × 100 ≥ cap × (100 − warn_pct)` · `subject_ok = max_subject_id < 2^20`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n` | int | 0 … 8192 | template (`nodes_in_template`, `edges_in_template`) | counts a template contributes to the Graph |
| `cap` | int const | 4096 nodes, 8192 edges | registry (`MAX_GRAPH_NODES`, REG:1805, edges folded into its unit string) | the Graph's `CAPACITY` limit |
| `warn_pct` | int | 10 | the Graph's warn margin (LSG:787) — cited | WARN band below the cap |
| `max_subject_id` | int | `< 2^20 = 1,048,576` | the Graph, LS-9 (LSG:123; LSG:915) — cited | highest id a subject may carry |

**Output Range:** ERROR (`CAPACITY_NODES` / `CAPACITY_EDGES` / `SUBJECT_ID_RANGE`) above the cap, WARN inside the band. The Graph's 800-node MVP bound (LSG:915) is Authoring's CONTENT lint, WARN at most, and is not enforced here.

**Example:** nodes = 3,700: `3,700 × 100 = 370,000 ≥ 4,096 × 90 = 368,640` → WARN; nodes = 4,097 → ERROR. Edges = 7,400: `740,000 ≥ 8,192 × 90 = 737,280` → WARN. The home-ship template at about 40 nodes passes silently; at 801 nodes it draws only Authoring's content WARN.

### D.8 Anchor polyline length and head clearance

The anchor polyline and head-clearance formula is defined as:

`edge_len_px = Σ_i ( |x_{i+1} − x_i| + |y_{i+1} − y_i| )` over an axis-aligned polyline · `edge_len_px ≤ TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` · `clear_ok(anchor) ⇔ ∀ i : free(p_i, normal) ≥ head_clear_px`, with sample points `p_i` every 24 px of arc length along the floor run and `free(p, normal)` the length of the maximal interval through `p`, normal to the final run segment, that lies in `tiles ∪ sill` and outside every `SOLID` span

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| polyline points | int pairs (px) | section-local; axis-aligned only (`POLY_DIAGONAL`) | template anchor | wall part first, then floor part (LY-23) |
| `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` | int const | 1152 (range 480–1536; 24 tiles) | registry (REG:1995), confirmed as the content bound | longest polyline Layout accepts; a longer one is REJECTED, never shortened (FT:642) |
| `head_clear_px` | int (px) | 144 | Failure & Trail FT-37 (FT:211) — cited, not restated | head clearance at a sev-4-capable anchor |
| `free(p, normal)` | int (px) | `0 … 48 × SECTION_MAX_TILES` | derived | the interval reading, adopted over the centred reading (LY-8 d) |

**Output Range:** `edge_len_px ≥ 0`, lint pass/fail; `clear_ok` boolean per sev-4-capable anchor with the first failing sample index reported (`CORRIDOR_HEAD_NARROW`).

**Example:** `anc_cryo_coolant`, polyline `[(48, 240), (384, 240)]`: `edge_len_px = 336 + 0 = 336` — the value Failure & Trail assumed (FT:377), with the far wall 7 tiles from the anchor — well under 1152. Samples every 24 px of arc length at `x = 48, 72, …, 360` (**14 points**, `(360 − 48) / 24 + 1`); the final partial segment `[360, 384)` is not sampled because its end point `x = 384` lies inside the E wall's footprint `[384, 408)`, where `free = 0` (the earlier '15 points, 48 … 384' counted a sample inside the wall) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the normal is N-S. For `x` in `[240, 336)` the heater footprint `[240, 336) × [96, 144)` occupies the north part, so `free = [144, 384) = 240`; elsewhere `free = [96, 384) = 288`; minimum 240 ≥ 144, pass. A 2-tile corridor (96 px) hosting a sev-4-capable anchor fails at its first sample; a 3-tile corridor (144) passes exactly. A 1,200-px polyline fails `TRAIL_EDGE_OVER_BOUND`.

### D.9 Rect key, canonical order, leaf index and broadphase cells

The rect key formula (registry: `rect_key_order`) is defined as:

static `idx` = rank of the rect in the template sorted by `(y0, x0, y1, x1, flags)`, dense from 0 · leaf `idx` = `N_static + door_declaration_index` · **socket variant `idx` = `N_static + N_doors + 2 × h + v`**, where `h` is the socket's ordinal among the template's **hatched** sockets in declaration order and `v` is 0 for PLATE and 1 for JAMB (a hatchless socket owns none; a hatch's LEAF is its door's leaf slot) · `rect_key(section_id, idx) = section_id × RECT_KEY_STRIDE + idx` · `rects_in` and `solid_in` return ascending `rect_key` · for a **half-open** query box `[ax0, ax1) × [ay0, ay1)`: `cx0 = floor_div(ax0, BROADPHASE_CELL_PX)`, `cx1 = floor_div(ax1 − 1, BROADPHASE_CELL_PX)` (likewise `cy`), `cells = (cx1 − cx0 + 1) × (cy1 − cy0 + 1)` · **empty-box guard:** if `ax1 <= ax0` or `ay1 <= ay0` the box is empty or inverted, it touches **zero cells**, the query returns nothing (the caller's `QueryBuffer` is cleared) and raises the diagnostic `query_box_empty` — without the guard an inverted box such as `ax1 = ax0 − 200` yields `cx1 < cx0` and a formula product that can be a large positive count (two negative factors) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; G5)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: variant slots added; the closed query box is retired in favour of the one half-open input convention of LY-12 b)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N_static` | int | 0 … `MAX_RECTS_PER_SECTION` | derived at load | static rect count of a template; the leaf indices are appended after it in door declaration order |
| `door_declaration_index` | int | 0 … `MAX_DOORS_PER_SECTION` − 1 | template | order of the door in its template |
| `N_doors` | int | 0 … `MAX_DOORS_PER_SECTION` | template | number of door records (interior doors and hatch doors); each reserves one leaf slot *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `h` | int | 0 … `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` − 1 | template | ordinal of a hatched socket; reserves two variant slots (PLATE `v` 0, JAMB `v` 1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `RECT_KEY_STRIDE` | int const | `MAX_RECTS_PER_SECTION` 4096 | this GDD | key stride; `idx < 4096`; lint `RECT_STRIDE_OVERFLOW` if `N_static + N_doors + 2 × N_hatched > 4096` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `section_id` | int | `1 … 2^20 − 1` (`can_build` returns `CAPACITY` when `next_section_id > 2^20 − 1`, LY-27) | the block | so `rect_key < 2^32`; the key column is 64-bit (LY-12 e) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` | int | 48 (24–96) | this GDD | uniform-grid cell edge; a rect is inserted in every cell its span overlaps |

**Output Range:** `rect_key` in `[0, 2^32)`, unique and stable across attach, detach, restore and animation (an attach renumbers nothing); a leaf with `leaf_w = 0`, an inactive variant slot and a zero-length variant slot keep their `idx` and their keys are skipped. Lint `RECT_INDEX_DENSE` checks the union of **static, leaf and variant** indices is exactly `0 … N−1`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** `fixture_cryo_excerpt` static rects, sorted: idx 0 N wall `[24, 408) × [72, 96)`; idx 1 W wall `[24, 48) × [96, 384)`; idx 2 heater `[240, 336) × [96, 144)`; idx 3 E wall `[384, 408) × [96, 384)`; idx 4 S wall west `[24, 240) × [384, 408)`; idx 5 S wall east `[336, 408) × [384, 408)` — `N_static = 6`, so `door_cryo_s` has leaf `idx = 6`; in section 3 its `rect_key = 3 × 4096 + 6 = 12,294`. A half-open query box `[380, 400) × [100, 112)` gives `cx0 = 7`, `cx1 = floor_div(399, 48) = 8`, `cy0 = 2`, `cy1 = floor_div(111, 48) = 2`, so 2 cells; **at a cell edge** `[96, 144) × [0, 48)` touches exactly cell column 2 (`cx1 = floor_div(143, 48) = 2`) and `[96, 145) × [0, 48)` touches columns 2 and 3. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Socket slots (two fixtures):** `fixture_socket_w2` (an E-facing socket, `line_px` 696, `start_px` 240, `W` 2, on a run that is exactly the span, one hatch door) has `N_static` 0, `N_doors` 1, so LEAF `idx` 0, PLATE `idx` 1, JAMB `idx` 2 (zero-width, omitted in every state); `fixture_socket_w3` (the same socket with `W` 3 on the run `[672, 696) × [192, 432)`) has static rects `[672, 696) × [192, 240)` (`idx` 0, before the span) and `[672, 696) × [384, 432)` (`idx` 1, after it), so `N_static` 2, LEAF `idx` 2, PLATE `idx` 3 (`[672, 696) × [240, 384)`), JAMB `idx` 4 (`[672, 696) × [336, 384)`, 48 px). In section 3 the `fixture_socket_w3` PLATE key is `3 × 4096 + 3 = 12,291`. A 16 × 8 foot box moving 3 px per tick sweeps at most a 22 × 14 box (D.22), which touches at most 4 cells.

### D.10 Hash and canonical bytes

The layout hash formula is defined as:

`geometry_hash = low32( H64( canonical_geometry_bytes ) )` · `visual_hash = low32( H64( canonical_visual_bytes ) )` (the two halves of the former `template_hash`, LY-26 e; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*) · `layout_hash = block_hash(&"layout.state") = H64( canonical_bytes( layout.state snapshot ) )` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)*. `canonical_bytes` is the kernel's prefix-free TLV form (kernel D.12); Layout owns the snapshot SHAPE only (the AC-130 allow-list, arrays in ascending id order, tombstones retained, derived caches `version`, grids, leaf cache, PROJECTOR objects and `mutation_count` excluded) and writes no byte encoder for the block. `layout_hash()` calls the kernel read `block_hash(&"layout.state", uncached = true)` (new, kernel request K-2), which exists in every build because Horror HE-25 keys its path cache on it in release. The two template-local byte strings `canonical_geometry_bytes` and `canonical_visual_bytes` (and only those) are `[u32 tag][u32 length][payload]` per field in a fixed documented order (ADR-LAY-06); they are never compared with a kernel value

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `H64` | function | 64-bit | the kernel's H64 (kernel D.12 and Tuning Knobs `H64`/`mix64` row; ratified 2026-10-27 — user ruling X13 / R-17): the first 8 bytes, little-endian, of SHA-256 computed with Godot's `HashingContext`; native, no GDScript byte loop *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)* | one hash for the whole project |
| `canonical_geometry_bytes` | bytes | template-sized | template | rooms by `room_key` with `origin_px` and tile runs, rects in D.9 order, socket and door positions, hardpoint cells, anchor polylines; strings as length-prefixed UTF-8 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `canonical_visual_bytes` | bytes | small | template | kit-piece ids, art references, dialect, labels *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `canonical_bytes( layout.state )` | bytes | tens of bytes to tens of KB | the kernel's TLV encoder (kernel D.12) over the block snapshot | counters, bodies, instances, sockets, welds; Layout supplies the snapshot shape only *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)* |
| `geometry_hash`, `visual_hash` | u32 each | `[0, 2^32)` | persisted per instance | drift gate (LY-26 e) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| hash cadence | policy | n/a | this GDD | see Output Range *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `geometry_hash` and `visual_hash` are u32, `layout_hash` is u64 and travels as two int32 halves in the payload (hex only in Save/Load's header, SL-2 S-1); none is ever emitted as a JSON number. The kernel's `state_hash` (kernel D.12) consumes `layout_hash` unchanged. **Cadence** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the kernel's GDScript canonical-bytes encode of the block plus the native SHA-256 is not free (kernel D.12 'Cost': dominated by the encoder, not by H64; [UNV], spike SP-ENG-1 P5), so `layout_hash` is **never computed per tick**: it is computed (i) when Save/Load captures the block, (ii) once after `restore()`/`post_restore()` and compared with the value **stored by its owner, Save/Load's `block_state_hash` for `layout.state` in the slot header (SL-1 `blocks[]`, SL-16; optional: written in dev/test captures, absent from a release capture, in which case no comparison is made; Save/Load owns the stored copy and `layout.state` holds no `layout_hash` field, which is why the AC-130 allow-list has none)**; this value is the kernel's `block_hash` of the same block, so equality is exact (AC-181) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)* (a mismatch is a diagnostic, not a restore reason, because the kernel owns `state_hash` verification), (iii) in debug builds at the kernel's `state_hash` cadence, and (iv) on demand by the Debug Overlay; the result is memoised on a block `mutation_count` bumped by LM1-LM4, so a repeat call with no mutation costs O(1). **`mutation_count` is a non-persisted, non-hashed derived cache** (it is in no LY-2 table, in no snapshot and in no canonical byte), **reset to 0 by `restore()` with the memo invalidated**, so two equal blocks built by different histories hash equal (AC-172). `geometry_hash` / `visual_hash` are computed once per template load and cached. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the stored `layout_hash` had no owner and `mutation_count` was undeclared.)* **GDScript integer rules for the hashes** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: GDScript has signed 64-bit `int` only, and `PackedInt32Array` stores signed 32-bit values. A u32 `geometry_hash` / `visual_hash` is therefore **persisted as a signed int32** and **normalised with `& 0xFFFFFFFF` on both sides before every compare** (a stored value read back at or above 2^31 is negative until normalised, and an un-normalised compare would raise a false `LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED` on about half of all templates); `layout_hash` travels as two int32 halves `(hi, lo)` each normalised the same way. `H64` is the kernel's (see the H64 row). Layout contains no hash loop and no shift-based mixing. Template hashes call the same H64 over the template-local byte strings. A signed 64-bit `h` is split into the int32 halves `hi = (h >> 32) & 0xFFFFFFFF` and `lo = h & 0xFFFFFFFF` (the mask removes GDScript's arithmetic-shift sign extension), each then stored as a signed int32 and re-normalised with `& 0xFFFFFFFF` before every compare. The Save/Load header hex of a hash is the little-endian digest prefix (hex64, SL F-10), e.g. SHA-256("abc") gives "ba7816bf8f01cfea", never the big-endian hex of the integer. Layout must not call the built-in `hash()`, `Array.hash()` or `Dictionary.hash()` (not stable across builds; scanned by AC-8). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)*

**Example:** the `counters` dictionary `{next_section_id, next_body_id, next_room_id}` encodes in the kernel TLV as 102 bytes = 9 (DICT header: 1 + 8) + 30 (`next_body_id`: key 1 + 8 + 12 = 21, INT 9) + 30 (`next_room_id`: 21 + 9) + 33 (`next_section_id`: key 1 + 8 + 15 = 24, INT 9) — entries sort by encoded key, which orders the u64 length first, so the two 12-character keys come first (`next_body_id` before `next_room_id`) and the 15-character key last; 119 bytes including its own parent key `counters` (1 + 8 + 8 = 17). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)* A visual-only template edit changes `visual_hash` but not `geometry_hash` or any key set or tile set, so restore accepts it with one diagnostic. A socket `start_px` or wall-rect edit changes `geometry_hash` with every key set and tile set unchanged: on a welded or otherwise non-`FREE` instance restore refuses it (`LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED`); on a never-welded `FREE` instance it is accepted with `geometry_changed_fresh`. A tile-set edit is refused by the key-set comparison, not by a hash — a 2^−32 collision therefore cannot hide a structural change. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.11 Seam age

The seam age formula (registry: `seam_age_class`) is defined as:

`age_class = OLD ⇔ T − welded_at_T ≥ SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS`, else `FRESH`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int (ticks) | 0 … 2^53 | `kernel.clock` | the simulation tick |
| `welded_at_T` | int (ticks) | persisted, never a JSON number | `welds[]` row (LM2) | the only time value Layout stores |
| `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` | int (ticks) | 36,000 (10 min at `TICK_RATE` 60), range 3,600–216,000 | this GDD | FRESH → OLD threshold; sim ticks only (paused and dormant time do not count) |

**Output Range:** boolean; monotone in `T`; a restore rolls `T` and the rows back together (WORLD scope), so age never contradicts itself.

**Example:** weld at `T = 100,000`: at `T = 135,999` the bead is FRESH; at `T = 136,000` it is OLD.

### D.12 Detach re-base and components

The detach re-base formula (registry: `detach_rebase`) is defined as:

components of the ACTIVE `welds[]` forest minus the cut seam, enumerated in ascending `section_id` · `C_keep` = the component containing the body's `origin_section_id` (it keeps `body_id`) · `C_new` = the other component (it receives `body_id = next_body_id`, then `next_body_id += 1`) · `m = min(section_id) over C_new` · the new body's `origin_section_id = m` · `frame_shift = offset_old(m)` · `offset_new(s) = offset_old(s) − frame_shift` for `s ∈ C_new` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `origin_section_id = m` was unstated)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `welds[]` ACTIVE rows | list | forest (LY-14: every weld merges two bodies) | the block | the weld relation over sections |
| `origin_section_id` | int | a section id in the body | `bodies[]` | the body's frame anchor |
| `offset_old(s)` | int pair (px) | on the 24 grid, may be negative | the block | body-frame offset before the cut |
| `frame_shift` | int pair (px) | on the 24 grid | derived | returned so Body keeps the avatar's screen position (PIPE:694) — Body's concern |

**Output Range:** exactly two components (the forest argument); keep-side offsets unchanged; new offsets are differences of 24-grid values, so they stay on the 24 grid; dirty keys `S/<seam_id>`, `BODY/<kept>`, `BODY/<new>` and `SEC/<id>` for **every section whose socket state changed (both sections of the seam, the unmoved kept-side one included) and every moved section**; each of those sections also gets its `version` and `static_version` bumped (LY-12 d). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The LM3 return list is `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}` with `rects[]` exactly `detach_preview(seam_id).rects[]`, computed before the sockets change *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

**Example:** home ship section 1 at `(0, 0)` on body 1 (`origin_section_id` 1); derelict B section 3 at `(576, 96)` welded to 1; a wing section 4 at `(1056, 96)` welded to 3. Cutting seam (1, 3): components `{1}` and `{3, 4}`; `{3, 4}` gets a new body from `next_body_id`; `m = 3`, `frame_shift = (576, 96)`; new offsets section 3 `(0, 0)`, section 4 `(480, 0)`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Dirty keys: 2 `BODY` (kept body 1 and the new body) + 3 `SEC` (section 1, whose socket state changed although it did not move, and the moved sections 3 and 4) + 1 `S` = **6**, far under `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256; the earlier count of 4 missed the kept body and the unmoved section 1, whose open leaf becomes a solid plate. The new body's `origin_section_id` is `m = 3`.

### D.13 World tile ceiling and rect budget

The world ceiling formula is defined as:

`world_tiles = Σ V(room)` over rooms of the loaded set · `ERROR ⇔ world_tiles > TILES_WORLD_MVP` · `WARN ⇔ world_tiles × 100 ≥ TILES_WORLD_MVP × WORLD_TILES_WARN_PCT` · `rects(body) ≤ MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V(room)` | int | 4 … 200 | D.3 | room volume |
| `TILES_WORLD_MVP` | int | 600 | Failure & Trail table data (FT:598) — a lint ceiling, not a Layout constant | world tile ceiling |
| `WORLD_TILES_WARN_PCT` | int (%) | 90 (80–95) | this GDD | WARN line (540) |
| `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` | int | 2,000 (1,000–4,000) | this GDD, sized to the Controller's performance criterion (PCI:1078) | rect budget of one body |

**Output Range:** lint verdicts `WORLD_TILES_NEAR_CEILING` (WARN) and `WORLD_TILES_OVER_CEILING` (ERROR); a build over the rect budget fails `can_build` with `CAPACITY`.

**Example (the registry's authored roster, `mvp_template_roster`; the earlier illustrative 502 is retired):** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `home_ship` `V` total 182 (5 rooms), `derelict_a` 118 (4 rooms), `derelict_b` 149 (5 rooms): the three ships total **449**, which is 74.8 % of 600; the always-dormant `pod_cabin` (`V` 4) makes **453** (75.5 %) if counted — the figure AC-100, AC-140 and the registry all use. Both pass, well below the 540 WARN line. Rects: about 250–400 unmerged across the three ship sections (the pod adds a handful) (planning estimate), the home-ship body about 90–150, against 2,000.

### D.14 South band

The south band formula is defined as:

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier formula put a 72-px face on every south run, which contradicts Pipeline PR-5's cap-only south wall; the real case is an interior east-west partition. The rule below is arithmetic, not a spike unknown.)*

**The rule.** Under PR-5's up-screen projection a wall whose footprint is rows `[y0, y0 + 24)` occupies screen rows `[y0 − 72, y0 + 24)` (24-px cap above a 72-px face, the footprint being the bottom 24 rows of the face) and **occludes the floor of the room behind it (north of it) for 1.5 tiles = 72 rows** (PR-5; its sort-key criterion: footprint `[240, 264)`, face rows `[192, 264)`, cap rows `[168, 192)`, so the hidden floor north of the footprint is rows 168..239). That hidden floor is a Pillar 1 and Pillar 2 tell problem — a hazard or a crewmate standing there is invisible although the body, the lamp and the Graph all treat it as real — so it is settled here by rule: (1) **a HULL run on a room's south edge is built cap-only** (PR-5's kit consequence), so it has no band (lint `HULL_SOUTH_NOT_CAP_ONLY`); (2) an east-west **PARTITION run with room floor immediately north of it** bears a face over that floor unless it declares `face_variant CAP_ONLY` (the cap-only variant, built on the room-behind side) — and if it keeps the `FACE` variant, **the band floor is declared non-walkable**: every band cell is covered by a SOLID rect (a rack, a counter, a pipe run), or the template is rejected (`PARTITION_FACE_HIDES_FLOOR`); (3) the band is **clipped to the room's own rows**, so a shallow room is never charged for rows that are another room's or a wall's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`south_band(room, run) = { (x, y) : x ∈ [x0, x1) minus the columns of the run's doorways, y ∈ [ max(y_s − face_h_px, room_top(x)), y_s ) }` for each east-west PARTITION run with `face_variant FACE` and room floor immediately north of it; empty for `CAP_ONLY` runs and for HULL south runs · `y_s` is the floor's south edge at the run (the run footprint's north edge), `room_top(x)` is the top edge of the contiguous floor tiles above `y_s` in column `x` · an interactable anchor, loose slot or floor fixture inside a band cell raises `SOUTH_BAND_OCCUPIED`; a band cell not covered by a SOLID rect raises `PARTITION_FACE_HIDES_FLOOR` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `face_h_px` | int (px) | 72 (art bible 8.2: 96 wall-art rows less the 24-px footprint; with the cap fixed at 24 and the footprint at 24 the hidden depth equals `face_h_px` for any override of 48–96) | template wall run | depth of floor hidden behind a `FACE` run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `face_variant` | enum | `FACE` (default), `CAP_ONLY` | template wall run (LY-4) | `CAP_ONLY` removes the face and the band *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `y_s` | int (px) | on the 24 grid | derived from the room's tile set | south edge of the floor at the run |
| `room_top(x)` | int (px) | on the 24 grid | derived | clips the band to the room's rows (shallow rooms) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `SOUTH_BAND_SEVERITY` | — | **RETIRED**: the lints are fixed at ERROR (the geometry is arithmetic, not a spike unknown) | — | the Y-sort spike (Pipeline Open Question 2) confirms only the actor-versus-wall draw order, never whether a face covers floor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** the band is 0–`face_h_px` rows deep per column, a lint verdict only. Whether a body standing south of the wall is drawn over it is the Pipeline's Y-sort result (tree order, [UNV]) — Layout states the rows and nothing about a body's draw order. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** `fixture_two_rooms_face` (the `FACE` variant of `fixture_two_rooms_one_wall`): the PARTITION `[48, 384) × [240, 264)` has `room_a` (floor `y` 96..239) on its north side; with `face_variant FACE` its band is `[168, 240) × [48, 384)` (72 rows, no clip), and the fixture is valid only if those cells are covered by SOLID rects (or the run declares `CAP_ONLY`). **Shallow-room clip:** `fixture_shallow_north` has `room_a` with `ty` 0..0 (floor `y` 96..143) over a PARTITION `[48, 384) × [144, 168)`: the band is `[96, 144)` (48 rows), clipped from `[72, 144)` by `room_top` 96, not 72 rows. `fixture_cryo_excerpt` declares its S run `face_variant CAP_ONLY` (the excerpt has no room behind it to host a face), so it has no band and the heater anchor `(288, 144)` and the coolant run at `y = 240` are not in question; a doorway column of a `FACE` run (for example `door_cryo_s` at `x` 240–335 on a FACE variant) is excluded from the band because the 96-px door cell replaces the wall face there, and its inside face anchor at `y = 383` reports the info `SOUTH_BAND_DOOR_FACE`, not an error. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.15 Frame fit

The frame fit formula is defined as:

`rows_needed(D) = WALL_ART_ROWS + TILE_PX × D + WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` · `cols_needed(W) = 2 × WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH + TILE_PX × W` · `D_FIT = floor_div(BASE_RESOLUTION.h − WALL_ART_ROWS − WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH, TILE_PX)` · `W_FIT = floor_div(BASE_RESOLUTION.w − 2 × WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH, TILE_PX)` · `ROOM_EXCEEDS_FRAME ⇔ ¬frame_exception ∧ (D > ROOM_DEPTH_SOFT_MAX ∨ W > ROOM_WIDTH_SOFT_MAX)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D`, `W` | int (tiles) | 1 … `SECTION_MAX_TILES_H` / `_W` | derived from the tile set's bounding box | room depth and width |
| `WALL_ART_ROWS` | int (px) | 96 (cap 24 + face 72) | art bible 8.2 | north wall art rows |
| `BASE_RESOLUTION.w`, `BASE_RESOLUTION.h` | int (px) | 640, 360 | registry `BASE_RESOLUTION` 640x360 (Pipeline) — the authoring-time base field, not a runtime zoom value | the DEFAULT field; zoom-out only enlarges it, and geometry is identical at every ladder step (LY-3 e) |
| `ROOM_DEPTH_SOFT_MAX`, `ROOM_WIDTH_SOFT_MAX` | int (tiles) | defaults 5 and 12 (= `D_FIT`, `W_FIT`); ranges 4–7 and 8–16 | this GDD | lint thresholds |

**Output Range:** a WARN, never a reject; `D_FIT = 5`, `W_FIT = 12`, frame volume at most 60 tiles for a rectangular room.

**Example:** `D = 5`: `rows_needed = 96 + 240 + 24 = 360 = BASE_RESOLUTION.h` exactly (no margin). `W = 8`: 432 of 640 columns. `fixture_cryo_excerpt` is 7 wide × 6 deep: `W = 7` fits; `D = 6` gives 408 rows, 48 over, so the room declares `frame_exception` (the schema flag, used here to show the WARN silenced; the machine room is the one type expected to need it, art bible 8.2) — without the flag the lint reports `ROOM_EXCEEDS_FRAME` and still accepts the template.

### D.16 Fixture count bound (Authoring's content lint; Layout supplies `V`)

The fixture count bound formula is defined as:

`fixtures_min(room) = 1` · `fixtures_max(room) = min( 6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8) )` · `Σ fixtures ≤ 28` over the loaded set (the Emitter's world total binds first)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V` | int | 4 … 200 | D.3 | room volume |
| `FIXTURES_MIN_PER_ROOM`, `FIXTURES_PER_ROOM_CAP`, `FIXTURE_V_PER` | int | 1, 6, 8 | Authoring's content lint, co-owned with the Emitter (EM AC-58, EM:1091) — provisional | per-room bound parameters |
| world total | int | 28 | the Emitter's D.14 budget / AC-58 (EM:1091) | world fixture ceiling |

**Output Range:** `fixtures_max` in `[1, 6]`; ids `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM`, `FIXTURE_COUNT_WORLD` are Authoring's lint ids (LY-30 CONTENT paragraph).

**Example:** `cryo_bay` `V = 42`: `1 + 5 = 6`, so 1–6 (exactly one self-powered, EM AC-58); airlock `V = 4`: `1 + 0 = 1`, so exactly 1; a `V = 16` airlock: 3; a `V = 24` corridor: 4.

**Dock-room status glow (X2, D58/D70a; Layout's side only).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The docked-podcraft prop in each pod-dock room (the room named by the template's `entry_room_key`: `airlock`, `a_dock`, `b_dock`) carries a small self-powered status glow that counts as an ordinary lit fixture, not a fourth act_gate exception. For this bound that means the glow is **one of the room's fixtures**: it counts toward `fixtures_max(room)` and toward the world total of 28; it is not an extra allowance. Layout adds no field and no lint: it supplies `V` (above) and the `entry_room_key` read; the placement rule ("every pod-dock room places the glow") and its lint belong to Derelict Authoring, and the self-powered fixture class belongs to the Emitter (EM-3 / EM-11 amendment). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Fixture arithmetic (the 2×2 `fixture_airlock`, V 4; NOT the authored airlock):** `fixtures_max = min(6, 1 + floor_div(4, 8)) = 1`, so if the glow occupies that one slot the fixture room has no second ordinary lamp; a `V = 16` fixture dock room (`fixtures_max = 3`) would have two further slots. **The real dock rooms, checked against `min(6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8))` with the glow counted** (V from Derelict Authoring's room tables): the authored `airlock` is `V` 30, so `fixtures_max = min(6, 1 + 3) = 4`, the glow is 1 of 4 and 3 slots remain; `a_dock` is `V` 20, `min(6, 1 + 2) = 3`, glow 1 of 3, 2 remain; `b_dock` is `V` 20, `min(6, 1 + 2) = 3`, glow 1 of 3, 2 remain (Authoring's own count of the glow plus one authored lamp is 2 of 4, 2 of 3 and 2 of 3, all within the bound). Whether the self-powered glow is exempt from the per-room cap is the Emitter owner's call and is listed as an open hand-off (it changes no Layout number).

### D.17 Clear path

The clear path formula is defined as:

`S(i, j) = [24i, 24i + 48) × [24j, 24j + 48)` · `valid(i, j) ⇔ S(i, j) ⊂ Floor ∧ S(i, j) ∩ Solid = ∅` · `connected ⇔` a breadth-first search over `valid` squares with ±1 lattice steps links every pair of door and hatch approach squares of the room

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `i`, `j` | int | 24-grid indices | derived | a 48 × 48 square on the 24 lattice (the art bible's rule 8 made exact) |
| `Floor` | set of px cells | union of room tiles plus their doors' sills | derived | walkable area |
| `Solid` | set of px cells | union of `SOLID` occupied spans of the **static rects and the variant slots in the lint's evaluation state** (LY-30 evaluation-state paragraph): leaves OPEN (doors traversable regardless of state, matching `ROOM_UNREACHABLE`), hatched sockets `FREE` (PLATE on) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was 'leaves closed', which contradicts the Connectivity row's 'doors traversable regardless of state' and made every door approach square a dead end)* | blocked area |

**Output Range:** boolean per room, naming the failing pair (`CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED`). Exact because every obstacle edge is a multiple of 24 and a step is 24 px, so a swept box equals the union of its two end squares (no tunnelling).

**Example:** `fixture_cryo_excerpt`: the door approach square `[240, 288) × [336, 384)` is valid (inside the floor, clear of the heater `[240, 336) × [96, 144)`), and the search reaches the north-west square `[48, 96) × [96, 144)` through the columns west of `x = 240`. Adding a full-width prop `[48, 384) × [216, 264)` leaves no 48-px band across the room (rows 216..263 blocked, the free rows above and below are separate): `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED` names the two squares. A one-tile neck with a prop on it fails; without the prop it passes.

### D.18 Door assembly span

The door assembly span formula is defined as:

`assembly(door) = [ start − width_px, start + width_px )` for hinge `LOW`, `[ start, start + 2 × width_px )` for hinge `HIGH` (frame plus pocket, in run order) · requires `assembly ⊂ straight wall` ∧ `margin_non_hinge ≥ DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX` · two assemblies on one run must not overlap (abutting is legal) · **interior doors only: a socket hatch is exempt from this span** — its LOW-side pocket would lie outside a short run (a 2-tile socket fills its whole run) and is presentation only (LY-15 e) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `start` | int (px) | on the 24 grid | template `doorway_start_px` | start of the doorway along the run |
| `width_px` | int (px) | 96 | template | doorway width |
| `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX` | int (px) | 48 (0–96) | this GDD | straight wall required on the non-hinge side before a corner |
| straight wall | interval | the run minus its corner cells | derived (corner cells belong to the E-W run, LY-8 a) | where an assembly may sit |

**Output Range:** pass/fail with the reason `DOOR_AT_CORNER`, `DOOR_ASSEMBLY_OVERLAP` or `DOOR_NEAR_SOCKET` (all reported under `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR`). Minimum `start`-to-`start` pitch on one run: 96 px for pockets pointing away from each other, 192 px for the same direction, 288 px for pockets pointing toward each other.

**Example:** `door_cryo_s`: `start = 240`, hinge `LOW`, assembly `[144, 336)`. The S run is `[24, 408)`; its straight wall is `[48, 384)` (corners `[24, 48)` and `[384, 408)`). `[144, 336) ⊂ [48, 384)` passes and the non-hinge margin is `384 − 336 = 48 ≥ 48`, exactly at the limit.

### D.19 Escape-route budget (Authoring's content lint; Layout supplies `route_length_px`)

The escape-route budget formula is defined as:

`ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX(n, V) = max( 0, ( W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE − ESCAPE_REACTION_TICKS − n × (DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS + 1) ) × V )` with `n >= 1` the number of closable doors the route must close (the single-door budget is `n = 1`, `V = V_RUN`; a carry route uses `V = V_CARRY`, below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the `n` term is inside the formula, so the "n closed doors subtract" sentence no longer double-counts the one door the formula already includes; the `max(0, …)` floor stops the budget going negative when `W_MIN_WARNING_S` is 4 s or less; the raw value `<= 0` raises Authoring's ERROR lint `ESCAPE_BUDGET_NONPOSITIVE`, a proposed id routed to Derelict Authoring, and the Manhattan route length makes the lint conservative: a real route is never shorter than `Manhattan`, AC-115)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE × V_RUN` = 1,800 px spent the whole warning on running, with zero slack for noticing the warning or for the door.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int (s) | 10 | the Graph (fairness gate, REG:1665) | minimum warning before an endogenous transition |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | the kernel (REG:1375) | ticks per second |
| `ESCAPE_REACTION_TICKS` | int (ticks) | 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (range 30–180) | Derelict Authoring / game-designer (provisional; **not** Attention's `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180, a different quantity — the abortability allowance of a horror tell) | ticks between the warning becoming readable and the player starting to run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` | int (ticks) | 30 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (range 18–60) | Interaction (registry `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS`, cited) | the uniform door-attempt window before the close lands *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `+ 1` | int (tick) | 1 | the kernel's T+1 command application (the SYSTEM `set_opening` applies in P2 of T+1, IA-24) | the close lands one tick after the window *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `V_RUN` | int (px/tick) | 3 | the Controller / Pipeline (REG:1158) | run speed |

**Output Range:** `(600 − 60 − 31) × 3` = **1,527 px** (31.8 tiles; at `V_WALK` 2 the same budget covers 1,018 px). Derived, not new: Layout owns no knob here (`ESCAPE_REACTION_TICKS` is Authoring's, provisional); the lint "every anchor whose descriptor can fail endogenously has a closable door within the budget along `route_length_px`" is Authoring's (Pillar 2 content rule). The figure above is for one closable door (`n = 1`); for a route that must close `n` doors, evaluate the formula with that `n` (it already subtracts `n × (DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS + 1)` ticks, so nothing further is subtracted for the first door). **Floor examples** (recomputed from the formula; the finding's "4 s or less" threshold does not hold at the registry defaults): at `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 1 the raw tick term is `60 − 60 − 31 = −31`, so the budget floors to 0 and `ESCAPE_BUDGET_NONPOSITIVE` fires; at 2 it is `120 − 60 − 31 = 29` ticks (87 px at `V_RUN`); the floor binds earlier with larger `n` (at `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 4, `n` = 5 gives `240 − 60 − 155 = 25` ticks, `n` = 6 gives `−6` and floors to 0). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review)*

**Example:** the cryo bay's 336-px crossing is `336 / 3 = 112` ticks, 1.87 s at a run — about 19 % of the warning's 10 s and `336 / 1,527` = 22 % of the budget; the budget is nowhere near binding in the fixture, and it now has 509 ticks (8.5 s) of running time, not 600. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Carry-route feasibility (X3, D71; Layout's side only).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The Boarding ruling lengthens the decision-bearing carry in the reference derelict to about 21–28 tiles. The route is authored content and its endpoints are Derelict Authoring's and Boarding's; Layout supplies only `route_length_px` and the section caps, so this is a feasibility check, not a new knob or rule. Arithmetic at `TILE_PX` 48: `21 × 48 = 1,008 px` to `28 × 48 = 1,344 px`. **Fits the schema:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the reference carry runs from `htr_a` to the `a_dock` pod arrival in DERELICT A, not B — Derelict Authoring `REF_CARRY_LENGTH`, DA-48.)* derelict A is `size_tiles` 17 × 14, whose farthest corner-to-corner Manhattan distance is `17 + 14 = 31` tiles = 1,488 px `>= 1,344`, so a 28-tile route needs at least `28 / 31` = 90 % of the bounding-box Manhattan length (an almost straight diagonal staircase through the wreck's rooms) but no larger section (the caps `SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` 48 and `SECTION_MAX_TILES_H` 32 are untouched); `route_length_px` is never below the Manhattan distance (AC-115), so the authored route must satisfy `Manhattan(endpoints) <= route_length_px`. A 21-tile route (1,008 px) needs only 68 % of it. **Time on foot:** while a HEAVY item is carried the gait is forced to `V_CARRY` **1 px/tick with no run** (Item & Module DB carry rule, `carry_ref`; the earlier `V_WALK` 2 was wrong): `1,008 / 1 = 1,008` ticks (16.8 s) to `1,344 / 1 = 1,344` ticks (22.4 s), which is **longer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (10 s)**. **Consequence for the escape lint:** at `V_CARRY` the D.19 budget is `ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX(1, V_CARRY 1) = (600 − 60 − 31) × 1 = 509 px` (10.6 tiles), so the 1,008 to 1,344 px carry exceeds it by about **500 to 835 px** (1,008 − 509 = 499; 1,344 − 509 = 835), and a fixture that fails endogenously while the player is mid-carry cannot be outrun by walking to a single closable door at the far end; this is a real Pillar 2 fairness gap, not an arithmetic artefact (the earlier 'just fits' and '326 px' figures are retracted). The X3 ruling stands; what is open is its consequence.
