Layout changes nothing in D.19 and owns no knob here. **Handed off with an owned Open Question (OQ-LY-CARRY, owner Derelict Authoring with the game-designer):** the carry-route escape rule (a carrier is exempt from endogenous transitions on the carry route, or the route is closable in segments, or the warning is lengthened while carrying) and how many closable doors a carry route needs. **AC-170 below** pins the authored reference route against this arithmetic.

### D.20 Containment (watertightness) lint

The containment formula is defined as:

rasterise all `SOLID` rects onto the 24-px cell grid (**leaves rasterised OPEN** — no LEAF rect, so a doorway's gap cells are passable; hatched sockets evaluated `FREE`, PLATE on, JAMB off *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: closed leaves made the flood unable to reach any gap cell, so the exception set was vacuous and a door opening onto void could never be caught)*) · flood-fill 4-connected from every room tile cell through non-`SOLID` cells · `leak(cell) ⇔ reached(cell) ∧ cell ∉ (room tile cells ∪ door gap cells ∪ FREE-hatch interior cells)` → `CONTAINMENT_LEAK(cell)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| cell | int pair | 24-px grid | derived | raster cell |
| room tile cells | set | `4 × V` per room | D.3 | allowed floor |
| door gap cells | set | from door declarations | template | the doorway when open |
| FREE-hatch interior | set | from sockets | template | cells behind an unmated hatch plate |

**Output Range:** pass, or a list of leak cells. A `CUT_OPEN` hatch is `SOLID` (LY-15), so a cut can never create a leak. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* With leaves open, the flood walks through each door gap, so the exception set (door gap cells) is exactly the cells of the gap itself and anything reached beyond it is a leak: **a door whose far side is void, or whose wall is an exterior wall, raises `CONTAINMENT_LEAK`** (the vacuum-door case this lint exists to catch). An interior door between two rooms floods into the other room's tile cells, which are allowed.

**Example:** `cryo_bay` has `42 × 4 = 168` room cells; deleting the N wall run lets the flood reach the void north of it: `CONTAINMENT_LEAK` at the first reached cell. With all walls present the flood stays inside the 168 cells plus the doorway's gap cells and, through the open S door, the neighbouring room's tile cells (on `fixture_cryo_excerpt_contained`, the 16 cells of `cryo_south_stub`); on the bare excerpt the same open door lets the flood reach the void south of the gap, which is a `CONTAINMENT_LEAK` by design *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### D.21 Block size estimate

The block size formula is defined as:

`bytes ≈ 4 × ( 13·I + 10·K + 4·S + 3·B + 3 )` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the instance row is 13 ints, one more than before, because `template_hash` is split into `geometry_hash` and `visual_hash`)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `I` | int | 0 … live cap + tombstones | the block | instance rows (13 ints each; discarded rows stay as tombstones, LY-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `K` | int | `I − 1` at most per body | the block | weld rows (10 ints: ids, state, two 2-int times) |
| `S` | int | 0 … `8 × I` | the block | non-`FREE` socket rows (4 ints) |
| `B` | int | 1 … | the block | body rows incl. `DISSOLVED` (3 ints) |

**Output Range:** tens of bytes to tens of KB against `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` 32 MiB (REG:1915); excludes the template-id strings and the snapshot container overhead [UNV].

**Example:** MVP after the one weld (four sections on four bodies, the pod included; the absorbed body's `DISSOLVED` row still counts): `I = 4`, `K = 1`, `S = 2`, `B = 4` → `4 × (52 + 10 + 8 + 12 + 3) = 340` bytes. A long campaign with 24 live and 200 tombstoned sections, 200 weld rows, 400 socket rows and 208 bodies: `4 × (2,912 + 2,000 + 1,600 + 624 + 3) = 28,556` bytes (about 28 KB), still four orders of magnitude below the ceiling; `LAYOUT_BLOCK_SOFT_BYTES` 65,536 raises a diagnostic long before. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.22 Query cost model (planning arithmetic, not a rule)

The query cost model formula is defined as:

`cost(query) = t_call + cells × ( t_cell + rects_per_cell × t_dedup ) + t_merge(candidates) + candidates × t_test + N_dyn × t_bbox + t_merge_dyn(N_hit)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the dynamic leaf / variant list is not in the grid, so every query scans it and merges its hits into the key-ordered output; the earlier model omitted both terms)* and `tests_per_actor_tick = cells(swept_box) × rects_per_cell + sweep_tests` · one `solid_in` per actor per tick with the swept box `[px − 8 − v, px + 8 + v) × [py − v, py + 8 + v)` (half-open), `v = V_RUN`, then an analytic axis-separated sweep (never one query per 1-px step); the per-tick aggregate is `Σ over actors cost(solid_in) + Σ over Emitter and Lighting queries cost(rects_in)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier model counted AABB tests only and omitted the cell lookup, the de-duplication and merge of a rect that spans several cells, the call overhead, and every non-Body consumer.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cells(swept_box)` | int | ≤ 4 at cell 48 | D.9 | grid cells touched |
| `rects_per_cell` | int | about 4 | planning estimate [UNV] | broadphase density |
| `sweep_tests` | int | about 16 | planning estimate [UNV] | analytic sweep against the candidate list |
| `N_dyn` | int | about 6 (MVP body; up to `MAX_DOORS_LOADED` 48 as a stress bound) | planning estimate [UNV] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | length of the body's dynamic leaf / variant list scanned by every query (bounding-box test each) |
| `t_bbox` | µs | about 0.5–1.5 | **[UNV]** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | one bounding-box reject test per dynamic entry (same order as `t_test`) |
| `t_merge_dyn(N_hit)` | µs | about 1 + 0.2 × `N_hit`, `N_hit` about 0–2 | **[UNV]** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | inserting the dynamic hits into the key-ordered output |
| per-test cost `t_test` | µs | about 0.5–1.5 | **[UNV]**, NOT SOURCEABLE (no GDScript cost data in the engine reference) | one AABB test *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| call overhead `t_call` | µs | about 1–3 | **[UNV]** | one GDScript call, buffer `clear()` and the 64-bit key column writes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| cell lookup `t_cell` | µs | about 0.5–1 | **[UNV]** | one grid-cell index computation and read *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| de-dup `t_dedup` | µs | about 0.3–0.8 | **[UNV]** | one generation-stamp check per candidate (a rect spanning k cells is met k times) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| merge `t_merge(c)` | µs | about 1 + 0.2 × c | **[UNV]** | ordering the candidates by `rect_key` (insertion into the sorted output) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Emitter-sized query | box | 288 × 288 px (lamp radius `R_DRAW_MAX_PX` 144) → up to 7 × 7 = 49 cells at cell 48, about 100–200 candidate rects | planning estimate [UNV] | a `rects_in` call from the Emitter's static-layer rebuild or a segment test; amortised by the `static_version` cache (LY-12 d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| actors per tick | int | 4 seats + the horror entities in range (about 6–8 in the MVP) | planning estimate | the aggregate-actor case *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Output Range:** about 32 tests plus the cell, de-dup, merge and call terms, about 0.028–0.072 ms per actor per tick from the static table (low: 1 + 6.8 + 4.2 + 16 = 28 µs; high: 3 + 16.8 + 4.2 + 48 = 72 µs, at 4 cells and 16 candidates), plus the dynamic terms at `N_dyn` 6 and `N_hit` 1 (3–9 µs scan plus 1.2 µs merge), so **about 0.033–0.082 ms, rounded to 0.03–0.08 ms** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier '0.04 to 0.08' did not follow from the table)* — above the Controller's budget of 0.05 ms P99 over 2,000 rects (PCI:1078) at the pessimistic end, which is why the pass lines are spike measurements (group K), not rules; the aggregate for 8 actors is then 0.26–0.66 ms of the 16.6 ms frame (**AC-164's aggregate line of at most 0.6 ms is the budget; AC-136's 0.05 ms P99 per actor is the target**, and the pessimistic end of the model exceeds the line, which is why group K is a spike) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and an Emitter-sized query is about 0.1–0.3 ms, paid only on a `static_version` change. The model is planning only. **Fallback ladder (cheapest rung first, each a data-layout step):** (1) tune `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` (24/48/96); (2) replace the sort in `t_merge` with per-cell pre-sorted arrays and a generation-stamp de-dup; (3) cache results keyed by `(cell set, static_version)` for static consumers (the Emitter's static layer already is); (4) merge collinear abutting wall rects at load (fewer rects, same occupied cells); (5) only then a GDExtension or C# rung, which needs technical-director sign-off and is out of the project's GDScript-only stack until granted. The weld's `GEOM_OVERLAP` test is the same arithmetic over the grid (`n_B × c_cell` probes, about 800 for a 200-rect section against brute force's 40,000), memoised for a preview on `(socket_a, socket_b, static_version_a, static_version_b)` (LY-14).

**Example:** a body of 3 sections, 200 rects, an actor mid-room: the swept box `[px − 11, px + 11) × [py − 3, py + 11)` touches 1–4 cells, about 16 candidate rects, about 32 tests in total.

## Edge Cases

Format: **If [condition]**: [exact outcome — a reason code, dirty key, diagnostic or lint id]. [rationale]. Reason codes are the closed sets of LY-29 (`layout.<REASON>` through `VALIDATION_FAILED`); lint ids are LY-30; formulas are D.n. "Defect" means a programmer error that the kernel treats as a `FAULTED` event or a failed assert, never a player-reachable state.

### Zero, maximum and out-of-range inputs

- **If `leaf_w` evaluates to 0** (`area = area_max`): the leaf rect is omitted from `rects_in` and `solid_in`, its `idx` stays reserved, and `leaf_version(section_id)` (so `version`, never `static_version`) bumps once (D.5, D.9, LY-7, LY-12 d). A fully open door must renumber nothing, or every later key in the section would shift. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If `area` is 0**: `leaf_w = width_px` (96) and the leaf covers the doorway; `gap = 0`, so no foot box passes (D.5). The closed state needs no special case.
- **If the Graph reports `area` above `area_max`** (the Graph clamps to `area_max`, LSG:808, so this is a Graph defect): Layout derives with `area` clamped to `[0, area_max]`, raises the diagnostic `leaf_area_out_of_range` and never emits a negative `leaf_w`. A bad upstream value must not produce a rectangle with `x1 < x0`.
- **If a template door declares `area_max` 0**: lint `DOOR_AREA_MAX` rejects the template whole in BOOTING. D.5 divides by `area_max`, so a zero must never reach the runtime.
- **If a room has fewer than 4 or more than 200 tiles, or an `exposed_tiles` outside 0–80**: lint `V_RANGE` or `EXPOSED_RANGE` rejects the template in BOOTING and the boot aborts with that id; nothing is clamped (LY-24). The ranges are the Graph's (LSG:299, LSG:452); a clamped `V` would silently change atmosphere volume.
- **If a template's `size_tiles` exceeds `SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` / `_H`, a room count exceeds `MAX_ROOMS_PER_SECTION`, a section exceeds `MAX_HARDPOINTS_PER_SECTION` or `MAX_DOORS_PER_SECTION`, or any field is out of its type range**: `SCHEMA_TYPE_RANGE` rejects the template whole. A partial template would give the Graph ids that disagree with the file.
- **If a template carries a negative section-local coordinate, or a stored (non-leaf) rect with `x1 <= x0` or `y1 <= y0`**: `SCHEMA_TYPE_RANGE`. Local coordinates are `>= 0` by authoring (LY-3 b); only a derived leaf may be degenerate (LY-7).
- **If `rot_q` is non-zero in a template or in a saved instance row**: `LAYOUT_ROT_UNSUPPORTED` at lint and at restore. There is no rotation or mirror (LY-3 d); a nonzero value can only come from a hand-edited file or a future format.
- **If a socket's `width_tiles` is 1 and it declares a hatch**: lint `HATCH_WIDTH` (a hatch needs `width_tiles >= 2`). A 1-tile socket without a hatch is legal and bulkhead-only: the weld joins lanes and `openings[]` is empty (LY-14).
- **If a socket's `width_tiles` is 3 with one hatch**: legal; the 96-px hatch plus 48 px of bulkhead fill the 144-px span (LY-14). Oddness of the width has no rule. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The 48 px of bulkhead is the JAMB variant slot, active only while the socket is `WELDED` two-sided; in every other state the PLATE covers the whole 144 px. At `width_tiles` 2 the JAMB is 0 px and is omitted in every state.
- **If a hatched socket's HULL run is exactly the socket span** (the 2-tile airlock): the before-span and after-span pieces are both zero-length and omitted (LY-15 b); the run contributes no static rect and the socket's slots own the whole span. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If `room_at` is called with a point inside a wall footprint or outside every room**: it returns `NONE`; a point on a door's sill returns the door's `sill_room` (LY-5). `NONE` is only ever answered where nothing is walkable, so no walkable point has no room.
- **If `floor_tile_at` is called on a door cell or a doorway**: it returns `NONE`, and an authored loose item on a door cell is the lint error `LOOSE_ON_DOOR` (LY-11). A dropped item must never rest inside the footprint of a leaf that could close on it.

### Doors and the leaf hook

- **If two leaves in one section change in the same tick**: `layout.doors` bumps `leaf_version(section_id)` (and so `version`) exactly once for that section and `static_version` not at all (LY-10, LY-12 d). The Emitter's static layer is untouched and only its dynamic leaves are patched, at most once per section per tick. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If an M13 `set_opening_area` and the `layout.doors` run fall in the same tick**: the P2 command applies first and the P3 hook reads the new `area`, so a closed leaf blocks and an open one does not in the SAME tick its state changes (LY-10; mirror of Controller PC-18 test 7). That is only true because `area` of a projected opening changes in P2 alone.
- **If a PLAYER-origin close would put the leaf over a foot box** (`leaf_would_overlap` true for the NEW `area`): the verdict is the verb owner's immediate reject (`OBSTRUCTED`, Interaction IA-24), `area` is unchanged and no dirty key is marked (LY-11, D.5). The check runs for the new `area` of every close write; under the MVP's binary door that is the one jump to `area` 0 (a stepped slide, if one is ever introduced, would check every step because the leaf grows each step). `opening_ref` is the opening's `edge_id`, so for a mated hatch both leaves are tested and a foot box overlapping only the far section's leaf still refuses the close. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a SYSTEM-origin close (a cascade) would overlap a foot box**: the owner enters `CLOSING_BLOCKED`, `area` holds at its last non-overlapping value, the command is re-processed at T+1 and the body is never displaced (LY-11, PCI:650); the retry is a P2 command, never an M13 call from `interaction.step` (LY-10 CONFLICT). The simulation is not a believer; it cannot shove a body out of a door.
- **If the body then stands in the doorway for a long time**: *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: under the MVP's binary door the leaf is never "partly open")* the door stays fully open, `area` held at its last non-overlapping value (`area_max`), and the Graph's gas keeps flowing through the OPENING at that area; Layout adds no special case. A body jamming a door open is a legal strategy with a real cost (the breach keeps venting), not a free safe room — Pillar 1.
- **If the foot box fits exactly**: it passes iff `gap >= F` (D.5): at `area_max` 64 an E-W doorway admits a 16-px-wide box from `area` 11 and a N-S doorway admits an 8-px box from `area` 6. At `area_pass_min - 1` the sweep halts at the leaf; nothing is displaced. (Partial areas do not occur in the binary MVP door; this case is the D.5 formula's property, tested directly, and the live case if a stepped slide is ever introduced.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a mated hatch pair has `area_max_a != area_max_b`**: the weld creates ONE OPENING with `area_max = min(a, b)` (LY-15) and both leaves use that OPENING's `area_max` as `A` in D.5, not their template values. Two leaves must leave the same gap or one leaf would cover the other's opening (two truths from one `area`).
- **If a hatch template declares a hinge**: lint `HATCH_HINGE_AUTHORED`. The pocket side is fixed by the socket's axis (LY-9), and that rule is sound only because welds are pure translation.
- **If `opening(edge_id)` returns nothing for a projected door or hatch edge** (an unknown or tombstoned edge id — a defect, since the read is published and the edge is Layout's own template reference): *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier case "the ask is unanswered" is closed, OQ-3)* the leaf stays at the template default (closed), the diagnostic `opening_unknown` is raised once, and `post_restore` reports `LAYOUT_RESTORE_DANGLING` if it happens during verification (LY-26 d). Layout never invents an `area`.
- **If a breach hole or seam weep exists** (a VIRTUAL OPENING of the Graph, LS-5): Layout produces no rect for it and `layout.doors` never reads it; the hole's gas is the Graph's and its trail is Failure & Trail's. "Area changes only in P2" is deliberately scoped away from these.
- **If `layout.doors` runs over more than `MAX_DOORS_LOADED` doors**: the build that loaded them was refused earlier by `can_build` with `CAPACITY`; if reached anyway (a defect), the hook logs once and processes all doors rather than skipping any, because skipping a leaf would fork collision from the Graph. `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold, not a cut-off.

### Sockets, welds, cuts and bodies

- **If `can_attach` fails for several reasons at once**: the verdict is the FIRST failing reason in the fixed order `SOCKET_UNKNOWN, SOCKET_NOT_FREE, SAME_BODY, FACING_MISMATCH, WIDTH_MISMATCH, CLASS_MISMATCH, LANE_MISMATCH, GEOM_OVERLAP` (LY-14). Cheap checks run first and a caller sees one stable reason.
- **If either socket is `WELDED`, `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`**: `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, before any geometry work; the Graph's `SOCKET_OCCUPIED` stays the port-level reason behind it (LY-14).
- **If both sockets belong to the same body (including the same section)**: `SAME_BODY`. Every weld merges two bodies, so the weld relation is a forest and a cut always splits a body into exactly two components.
- **If the translated footprints overlap anywhere** (a bumper rect, a prop, a room tile of the other body): `GEOM_OVERLAP`, and `socket_plan` returns empty `pairs[]` and `openings[]` (D.6 negative example).
- **If two welded hulls touch outside the mated socket**: allowed. Rects abut without overlapping, `GEOM_OVERLAP` stays silent and the diagnostic `HULL_CONTACT_OUTSIDE_SOCKET` counts it; there is no traversal across it because it is exterior. Rejecting every touch would forbid any weld between irregular hulls.
- **If an attach would leave a body over `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY`**: the weld is not rejected (the reason set is closed); Layout raises the diagnostic `rect_budget_exceeded` and the grid stays correct. The content lint and the MVP world budget (D.13) are the guards; a `BODY_FULL` reason is an open question (Open Questions).
- **If a seam is cut and the first (only) home-ship socket becomes `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`**: no free socket remains and `can_attach` answers `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` for every later attempt; nothing returns a socket to `FREE` (LY-13). Accepted for the one-weld MVP slice; the socket economy is an open question.
- **If a cut is requested on an unknown seam or on one already cut**: `can_detach` returns `SEAM_UNKNOWN` or `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE`; a second cut command fails cleanly, marks no dirty key and changes no state.
- **If a cut would allocate a body past `MAX_BODIES` live bodies**: `can_detach` returns `CAPACITY` in validate, so no state changes, no dirty key is marked and `apply` is never reached (LY-29). The Lighting ceiling `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4 is not a reject: past it Lighting draws stand-ins and the simulation is unaffected; the MVP's worst sequence ends at 4 bodies (zero margin against `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4, reported as `BODIES_LIVE_MARGIN`; the margin against `MAX_BODIES` 8 is 4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: 'zero margin against both' was wrong)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a cut is applied while the mated hatch is open** (`area` > 0, for example 32): the Graph removes the opening in `cut_detach`; in the same Layout apply the kept-side section's LEAF slot goes inactive and its PLATE goes active, and that section gets a `version` and `static_version` bump and `SEC/<id>` although it did not move (LY-17). `rects_in` and `solid_in` then return the plate at once, so the Emitter's version-keyed static layer stops passing light through what is now a solid wall. The same holds on the moved side. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If that cut materialises a SOLID PLATE on an actor's foot box standing in the open hatch** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: Layout does not check (it reads no actor state, LY-18) and places the PLATE; the CHANNEL and CASCADE paths are vetoed or deferred by Assembly (MSA-21, MSA-22) and Body's BM4 refuses any residual call with `BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` (BDY-24 iii); no overlap can be created by a cut; an overlap observed anyway is a Body defect, never a sorting case (AC-28, AC-174) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.
- **If `attach_section` is called with the two sockets in the reverse order**: the same result as the normal order — the lower `body_id` survives, the other is absorbed, `offset_b` and `frame_shift_px` are computed for the absorbed body relative to the survivor (D.6 example), so a mix-up of arguments can never dissolve the home-ship's body. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a weld joins a hatched socket to a hatchless one** (`hatch_one_sided`): the hatched side keeps its PLATE active, the bare side has no slots, `openings[]` is empty and the preview payload carried `hatch_one_sided: true` before the player committed (LY-14). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a cut is RAGGED**: both sockets keep their rows, the mated hatch is a solid, light-blocking `SOLID | OCCLUDES_LIGHT | HULL` plate and the Graph owns the leak (`A_CUT` 24, hull stub `hp` 2500) (LY-15). No avatar can leave the hull through it and nothing is lit beyond it.
- **If the avatar's section ends up in the new component of a detach**: Layout allocates the new `body_id` and the new offsets and returns `frame_shift_px`; Body performs the `BODY_TRANSFER` and keeps the screen position (PIPE:694). Layout never reads the avatar's position.
- **If a discard names a section with a `WELDED` socket**: `can_discard` returns `SECTION_WELDED`. A discard of an unknown or already discarded section returns `SECTION_UNKNOWN`. Weld-history rows that name a discarded section stay (Pillar 5) and its surviving mate keeps `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN`.
- **If a discard removes the last live section of a body**: the body row becomes `DISSOLVED` (an id is never reused), the grid for it is dropped and `BODY/<id>` plus `SEC/<id>` are marked.
- **If `anchor_geometry` is asked for an anchor of a discarded section**: it returns nothing, without an error (FT:703-704). A trail draw must not crash on a section that no longer exists.
- **If a floor-run polyline would cross a doorway or room boundary**: that is the authoring lint `FLOOR_RUN_CROSSES_BOUNDARY` (LY-8 e), never a runtime clip; a run reaching a sill stops at the sill. A wall fitting with no run record is `WALL_FITTING_NO_RUN`.
- **If a polyline is longer than `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX`**: `TRAIL_EDGE_OVER_BOUND` rejects the template; nothing is shortened (FT:642). A shortened trail is a lie about how far the frost reaches.

### Capacity, ids and the conservative error

- **If a build would exceed `MAX_SECTIONS` live sections, `MAX_BODIES` live bodies, a room/rect cap or the Graph's own capacity** (`can_join`/M1 `CAPACITY`): `can_build` returns `CAPACITY` before anything is allocated; no id is consumed and no Graph call is made. Validate-then-apply (CR-9) leaves no half-built section.
- **If a template alone exceeds `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 or 8192 edges**: `CAPACITY_NODES` / `CAPACITY_EDGES` ERROR in BOOTING; within 10 % it is a WARN (D.7). The Graph's 800-node MVP bound is Authoring's content WARN and Layout does not enforce it.
- **If `next_section_id` would exceed 2^20 − 1**: `can_build` returns `CAPACITY` before anything is allocated (D.9: `rect_key = section_id × 4096 + idx` must stay below 2^32; unreachable in practice: tens of live sections times a few hundred hops). `next_body_id` and `next_room_id` are bounded by 2^31 − 1 and checked the same way. Counters are never wrapped and ids are never reused. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier bound of 2^31 − 1 for every counter contradicted D.9's 2^20 for section ids.)*
- **If a template's rect count (`N_static + N_doors + 2 × N_hatched_sockets`) exceeds `RECT_KEY_STRIDE` 4,096**: lint `RECT_STRIDE_OVERFLOW` rejects it whole; otherwise a section's keys would collide with the next section's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If tombstones accumulate over a long campaign**: rows stay (ids are consumed, history is geometry); live caps count non-discarded rows only. The block grows by 52 bytes per tombstoned section and 40 per weld row (D.21), so `LAYOUT_BLOCK_SOFT_BYTES` 65,536 raises a diagnostic long before `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` 32 MiB (Save/Load's) matters. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a weld joins sections whose rooms were counted `exposed_tiles` from the exterior**: the count is unchanged and the closed hatch still radiates as hull (LY-24, the R17 conservative error). For the fixture airlock this is 2 of its 6 exposed tiles (D.4): one small room radiates 2 tiles' worth too much until the Vertical Slice re-base. It is bounded, one-directional (never too little heat loss) and routed to the Graph owner.
- **If the world loads rooms whose volumes sum above `TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600**: `WORLD_TILES_OVER_CEILING` ERROR; at or above 540 `WORLD_TILES_NEAR_CEILING` WARN (D.13). Failure & Trail's patch tables are sized to 600.

### Save, restore and drift

- **If a save names a `template_id` the catalogue does not hold**: `LAYOUT_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_TEMPLATE`; the pre-restore state is untouched and the load is refused loudly (LY-26).
- **If a saved instance's template changed structurally** (any `room_key`, `socket_key`, `hp_key` or `node_ref` set or any room's tile set differs): `LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED`. **If its `geometry_hash` differs while every key set and tile set is equal** (a socket moved, a wall rect edge edited): `LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED` when the instance has any non-`FREE` socket, a `welds[]` row, or shares its body with another section; otherwise restore succeeds with the diagnostic `geometry_changed_fresh`. **If only `visual_hash` differs**: restore succeeds with one diagnostic (D.10, LY-26 e). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a geometry edit used to classify as visual-only, so a welded save restored with broken abutment.)*
- **If the stored `schema_version` is unsupported**: `LAYOUT_RESTORE_SCHEMA`, never a silent default. **If a counter is not greater than its largest id**: `LAYOUT_RESTORE_COUNTER`. **If a weld, socket or body reference is missing**: `LAYOUT_RESTORE_DANGLING`.
- **If `post_restore` finds a `graph_ref_base` that does not resolve in the Graph** (node, edge or compartment counts differ from the template): `LAYOUT_RESTORE_DANGLING`, and the world refuses to enter `RUNNING` (the kernel owns that consequence). Verification only; Layout repairs nothing.
- **If a restore happens while a door is open or in its attempt window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the MVP door is binary and has no mid-travel state)*: between `restore()` and `post_restore()` the leaf is the template default (closed) and reading it raises `stale_leaf`; `post_restore` re-derives the leaf from the restored `area` and bumps `version` 1 → 2 for that section; the Emitter's W0 read then rebuilds on tick 1 (LY-26 c). **If every door is closed**: no section's leaf set differs from the default, no bump happens and `version` stays 1.
- **If `version` after a restore numerically equals a value a consumer cached before it**: consumers still rebuild, because a restore is an unconditional rebuild signal (LY-12 d); `version` is not persisted.
- **If the player dies and the game restores the last autosave**: `layout.state` is WORLD-scoped and rolls back with the snapshot — welds, scars, offsets and `welded_at_T` return together, so seam age never contradicts itself (D.11). The ship the player cut away is back; the knowledge of what that taught them is not Layout's (Pillar 5).
- **If a template is renamed or a `room_key` changes before the first build given outside the project**: saves are disposable (SL-14). After that point a migration step in the owner is required (LY-6 e); the closed restore reasons make a missed migration loud, not silent.
- **If the Graph's `next_section_id` disagrees with Layout's** (the Graph advances it to `max(section_id) + 1` on M1, LY-6 d, so a mismatch is a restore-time defect only): Layout's counter wins for Layout ids; a mismatch is `LAYOUT_RESTORE_DANGLING` through the `graph_ref_base` check. Layout never edits the Graph. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### Hallucination interplay (Pillar 4)

- **If the Perception Filter shows a phantom door, wall or room**: it has no Layout id, no row and no rect; `rects_in`, `solid_in`, `room_at` and every other query are unchanged (LY-34). The player falsifies it by walking into the space or by their lamp's beam: a phantom wall does not stop either.
- **If the Filter shows a real door as open while its leaf is closed (or the reverse)**: the leaf rect is the truth — the body stops, the light stops, `leaf_would_overlap` answers from the real `area`. Layout has no "perceived `area`" and exposes none.
- **If a teammate who is hallucinated as a horror stands in a doorway**: their foot box is a real caller argument; the close is refused by the verb owner exactly as for any body. Layout knows nothing about who looks like what, or about lethal friendly harm.
- **If a phantom loot item is shown on a loose slot**: `loose_slots` returns the authored geometry only; no item instance exists in Layout, and the real instance table is Interaction's, so a phantom cannot be picked up. Nothing in Layout is a lie to be told.
- **If a code change proposes a Layout flag named hallucinated, fake or perceived, or a per-player variant of any table**: struck on sight (LY-34); the AC grep for those tokens fails the build.

### Degenerate strategies

- **If a player welds, cuts and re-welds the same two sections to refresh the bead's freshness**: impossible in the MVP — a cut leaves both sockets terminal (`CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN`), so a second weld needs a different, `FREE` socket. If the socket-economy decision later frees sockets, `welded_at_T` resets and the old scar stays visible as history.
- **If a player spams a door to bump `version`**: each toggle bumps once per section per changed tick; a door action is a few ticks of travel (Interaction's knob), so the Emitter's rebuild rate is bounded by the number of doors in motion, not by input rate.
- **If a thrown item or beam crosses a closing leaf**: `clip_to_footprints` and the occlusion test both read the same leaf rect for that tick, so the throw stops where the beam stops; there is no second opinion.
- **If an author stuffs a room with props to hide a route**: `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED` (D.17) and `ROOM_UNREACHABLE` reject the template; connectivity treats doors as traversable regardless of state.

### Co-op later hooks

- **If two bodies stand in one doorway and a third player closes it**: `leaf_would_overlap` takes ALL foot boxes the caller passes and, for a mated hatch, tests the leaves of BOTH sections; the close is refused (verb owner's token) if any box overlaps any leaf. Layout has no concept of a player (LY-11). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a client's template catalogue differs from the host's**: `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY` replicates the instance table plus `geometry_hash` and `visual_hash`, never geometry; a `geometry_hash` mismatch is the same drift the restore policy rejects (a `visual_hash` mismatch alone is a diagnostic), so the client must refuse the join rather than simulate different walls. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If two players weld at once**: commands apply in P2 in the kernel's deterministic order; the second `can_attach` sees `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` or `SAME_BODY` and fails cleanly. Layout adds no lock.

## Dependencies

Status vocabulary: **designed** (a complete GDD on disk), **in-flight** (being designed concurrently in this run; no complete GDD on disk, at most a draft or a stage brief), **undesigned-provisional** (no file and no stage in this run; every contract is provisional). Status was checked with a Glob of `design/gdd/` at authoring time (Interactions, 3.0) and re-checked in the design review on 2026-10-27: **every row below is `designed` except Procedural Derelict Generation**, and no row is `in-flight` any longer (the earlier labels "in flight" and "undesigned-provisional" were stale for every other system). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Direction reads "Layout → X" when Layout depends on X, "X → Layout" when X depends on Layout. "Hard" means the dependent cannot function without the interface; "soft" means it degrades.

| System | Direction | Hard / soft | Interface (the exact names) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** | Layout → kernel | Hard | block registry (CR-8: `block_id`, `class`, `persist_scope`, `scope`, `repl_hint`, `restore_after`), `WriteScope` (CR-9), step-hook registry (CR-3, hook `layout.doors`), `PROJECTOR` subscriber class and dirty-key batching (CR-10), `kernel.clock` `T`, H3 one writer per fact, H10 scene-tree free | designed |
| **Life-Support Graph** | Layout ↔ Graph (mutual at data level; acyclic by restore order: `graph.state` is `restore_after: [layout.state]`, Layout is `restore_after: []`) | Hard | Layout → Graph: M1 `instantiate_section` called at build (returns `{node_base, edge_base, comp_base}`); `opening(edge_id) -> {area, area_max, door_state}`, `seam_view(seam_id)` and `weld_join`'s `{seam_id, opening_ids[]}` (published; the earlier ASK of LY-10 is closed, OQ-3 / OQ-4); `read_view` (`compartment_at(room_ref)`, `neighbors(cid)`, `nodes_in(cid)`, `section_of(id)`); `can_join(plan)`; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `weld_join` / `cut_detach` / M4 `discard_section` ordering. Graph → Layout: `room_id`, `V`, `exposed_tiles`, polygon, anchors, `socket_plan` data, section templates. The ownership clause of LY-1 | designed |
| **Item & Module Database** | Layout → Item DB | Hard | `compatible_defs(hardpoint)` (F-6), `cell`, `dialect`, `socket_tag`, `MAX_PORTS_PER_DEF` 8; Layout gives hardpoint declarations (accepted `node_kind`, ordered port-type list, optional accepted subtypes) and plate positions; door motors are LOAD nodes `mod_door_motor` (resolved) | designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| **Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query** | Emitter → Layout | Hard | `OccluderSource` (`rects_in(aabb)`, `version(section_id)`, `occludes_light`), fixture anchors, `subject_anchors`, door-leaf rectangles; `body_of(section_id)`, `section_offset(section_id)`, `static_version(section_id)`, `changed_region(section_id)` (the Emitter derives its `body_id` and origin from them and stores none, *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*); E1 `instantiate_section_emitters` is called by the orchestrating apply, not by Layout | designed (index edge to add; EM:736) |
| **Failure & Trail System** | F&T → Layout | Hard | `anchor_geometry`, `room_surface`, `section_offset`, corridor clearance, floor-run start/direction, `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` content bound, `wall_face_rows` | designed (index edge to add; FT:720) |
| **Save/Load & Versioned Format** | Save/Load → Layout (Layout is the restore root) | Hard | block `layout.state` (snapshot / restore / default_state / post_restore), `schema_version`, SL-2 type rules, SL-14 migration, SL-18 `init_world`, `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | designed (index edge to add; SL:847) |
| **Player Controller & Input** | Controller / Body → Layout | Soft (the controller runs against a `collide` fixture until Layout lands) | footprint rectangles via `solid_in`; the verb owner's reject token (`OBSTRUCTED`, IA-24; PCI:650's `BLOCKED` is a routed wording refresh) / `CLOSING_BLOCKED`; `body.integrate` `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]` (LY-10; resolved by Ship Body BDY-8, PC-14 / PC-15 read the same) | designed |
| **Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera** | Layout → Pipeline | Soft | PR-5 layer stack, z-band table, Y-sort key D.12, `entity_id` tie-break (LY-31; was `creation_id`, *amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*), `TILE_PX` 48, `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 | designed |
| **Ship Body & Interior Physics** | Body → Layout | Hard | `solid_in`, `section_at` (BDY-29), `body_of`, `section_offset`, `frame_shift_px`; Body `restore_after: [layout.state]`; Body's per-body root `Node2D` parents Layout's section nodes (BDY-33) | designed (GDD on disk; refreshed in the 2026-10-27 consistency pass) |

| **Web Map & Transit** *(own Hard row, Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: it is the sole caller of LM1 and LM4 and was filed under soft reads)* | Web Map → Layout | **Hard** | `build_section` (LM1) at `init_world` only, `discard_section_geometry` (LM4, the only discarder), `can_build` / `can_discard`; the world cannot be built without LM1 | designed |
| **Chart Table, Web Map & Menus** *(row added 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | **none (explicitly excluded)** | none | The Chart Table is host-local, owns no state block, hook or mutator (CT-27) and draws `web.view` only; it makes no Layout call and Layout supplies it nothing. Any Layout consequence of a pod hop or berth reaches Layout through Web Map's LM1/LM4 calls, so the Hard edge is Web Map's. No Layout interface is added for it | designed |
| **Boarding & Salvage; Attention; Horror Entities; Perception Filter / Sanity; Debug Overlay** | each → Layout (soft reads) | Soft | `loose_slots` / `entry_room_key`; `room_surface` / `room_adjacency` / `door_passage` / `floor_tile_at` / `hull_segments_near` / `behind_wall_footprint` (Horror; Horror's door_passage ask answered 2026-10-28); the truth-channel grant (PF-5 row 11); DIAGNOSTIC reads | designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| **Adaptive Audio & Tells** *(row added in the design review)* | Audio → Layout | Soft | `anchor_geometry`, `section_offset`, `room_at`, `segment_at`, `plate_positions` (the `hull_anchor` inputs, LY-21) | designed |
| **In-World Instruments & Inspect View** *(row added in the design review)* | Instruments → Layout | Soft | the instrument placement record and `subject_anchors` `kind_hint INSTRUMENT` (LY-4, LY-23, IW-31) | designed |
| **HUD & Suit Layer** *(row added in the design review)* | HUD → Layout (through Interaction) | Soft | `subject_anchors` via Interaction's `anchor_o` (prompt placement); the weld/cut cursor outline's screen treatment (UI Requirements) | designed |
| **Procedural Derelict Generation (Vertical Slice)** | Procgen → Layout | Soft | the same schema; `template_ref` `source GENERATED` and story slots reserved | undesigned-provisional (the one system without a GDD) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| **Art bible 6.3 / 8.2** | Layout → art bible (authoring input) | Hard (content) | kit pieces and footprints; door cell 96×96; wall cell 48×96; props; machinery nodes | approved |

**Bidirectional consistency (coordination rule: dependencies must be bidirectional).** The designed GDDs that already name Layout: the Graph (LSG:265, LSG:849, `restore_after: [layout.state]`), the Emitter (EM:331, EM:736), Failure & Trail (FT:306, FT:720), the Item DB (IDB:466, IDB:860), the Controller (PCI:218), the kernel (KRN:197, KRN:198, KRN:693) and Save/Load (SL:274, SL:472, SL:847). Each row above is the counterpart of one of them. Their "provisional (Layout unwritten / undesigned)" cells become stale when this GDD is written; the refresh is a routed edit to each owner (Cross-References), not a unilateral change.

**Edges missing from the systems index — to be added by the index owner (routed, never silently fixed):** Ship Section & Layout Data Model is a **Hard** dependency of Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (EM:736), Failure & Trail System (FT:720), Save/Load & Versioned Format (SL:847), the Command & Simulation Layer (KRN:693 "No — add edge"), Procedural Derelict Generation, 2D Lighting & Occlusion (Hard, LO-11 / C-L1) and Interaction System (Hard, IA-24/IA-28/IA-29), plus Suit & Equipment → Layout (Hard, SE-3). The index lists this system's own dependencies as Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph and Item & Module Database (IDX:83); those three are unchanged.

**Acyclicity argument.** Restore order: `layout.state` (root, `restore_after: []`) → `graph.state` (`restore_after: [layout.state]`) → `body.state` (`restore_after: [layout.state]`, BDY, designed) → `emitter.state` (`restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]`) → `interaction.state` (`restore_after: [layout.state, controller.state, graph.state, body.state, emitter.state]`, IA-36, designed; IA-36 lists `body.state` directly because IA-37's `post_restore` reads restored body positions, and the chain `layout.state -> graph.state -> body.state -> emitter.state -> interaction.state` is unchanged in order, so the argument holds *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; *amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "in flight"*). Layout reads no other block in `restore()`; its cross-block reads (the Graph's `area`, Item DB `def_id`s) happen only in `post_restore` and in P3, after every `restore()` has completed. The Layout ↔ Graph data-level mutuality (Layout calls M1; the Graph keys compartments by `room_id`) is therefore never a restore cycle. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

## Tuning Knobs

**★ marks a registry candidate** — a cross-system fact other GDDs or code read (Cross-References C records the handling; none is registered by this stage). Unmarked Layout knobs are internal to this GDD's lint and caps. **Cited** rows are owned elsewhere; this table points at the source of truth and never restates a different value (G2). Every Layout knob is data, in `assets/data/` or a lint configuration, never hardcoded (coding standard). A knob that is a template field (`area_max`, `width_px`, `face_h_px`, `hinge`) is authored per door or wall and has its default and range here.

### Layout-owned knobs

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `LAYOUT_BLOCK_SCHEMA_VERSION` | 1 | int, append-only | integer, never lowered | n/a (a bump needs a migration, SL-14) | n/a | Save/Load `FORMAT_VERSION`; LY-26 restore reasons |
| ★ `SECTION_SCHEMA_VERSION` | 1 | int, append-only | integer, never lowered | n/a (a bump needs a loader path and a template migration) | n/a | LY-4; Authoring files; Procgen |
| ★ `MAX_ROOMS_PER_SECTION` | 8 | rooms | 4–12 | Graph node/compartment pressure; longer lints; `room_id` ranges grow | the 5-room home ship cannot be authored | `ROOM_TILES_MAX` (cited); the Graph's 12-compartment weld bound (LSG:228) |
| ★ `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` | 4 | sockets | 1–8 (never above `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION` 8, cited) | exceeds the Graph's seam limit; seam beads crowd the hull | too few weld options; the socket economy fails sooner | `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION` (LSG:916, cited); `SOCKET_SPACING_MIN_TILES`; socket economy (Open Questions) |
| ★ `MAX_HARDPOINTS_PER_SECTION` | 32 | hardpoints | 12–64 | prompt clutter; lint cost; rect growth | 24 fails `home_ship` by 3 (`SCHEMA_TYPE_RANGE`); the MVP module defs may not all have a slot | Item DB `compatible_defs`; `MAX_PORTS_PER_DEF` (cited); raised from 24 for Derelict Authoring DA-12 / C-4 (`home_ship` declares 27, world 67, registry `mvp_fixture_counts`) |
| `MAX_DOORS_PER_SECTION` | 24 | doors | 8–48 | `layout.doors` cost; leaf version churn | rooms cannot connect | `MAX_DOORS_LOADED`; D.9 leaf indices |
| ★ `MAX_DOORS_LOADED` | 48 | doors | 24–96 | `layout.doors` cost (≈ 20 loaded in the MVP) | `init_world` refused with `CAPACITY` | `layout.doors`, `STEP_BUDGET_MS` (cited) |
| `MAX_RECTS_PER_SECTION` (`RECT_KEY_STRIDE`) | 4,096 | rect slots (static + leaf + socket-variant), power of two | 2,048–4,096 | key overflow past `2^32` with `section_id < 2^20` | cannot author a large section | D.9 `rect_key`; lint `RECT_STRIDE_OVERFLOW` (`N_static + N_doors + 2 × N_hatched_sockets <= stride`); `MAX_DOORS_PER_SECTION`, `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

| ★ `MAX_SECTIONS` | 24 | live (non-discarded) section instances | 6–48 | block size; Graph capacity | the world cannot hold the home ship plus 3 derelicts | `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` (cited); tombstones do not count (D.21) |
| ★ `SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` | 48 | tiles | 24–96 | uniform-grid and rect arrays grow; int range | forces splitting the home ship (the fixture airlock socket sits at x = 696 px, 14.5 tiles) | `ROOM_WIDTH_SOFT_MAX`; D.15 |
| ★ `SECTION_MAX_TILES_H` | 32 | tiles | 16–64 | as above; wall-art sort rows grow | forces splitting | `ROOM_DEPTH_SOFT_MAX`; D.15 |
| `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` | 48 | px | 24–96 (a divisor/multiple of 24) | more rects per cell, slower candidate scan | more cells per rect, more bookkeeping | D.9, D.22; `TILE_PX` (cited) |
| ★ `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` | 36,000 | sim ticks (10 min at `TICK_RATE` 60) | 3,600–216,000 | a bead never reads as history | every bead looks old before it is seen fresh | D.11; PROJECTOR |
| ★ `SOCKET_CLASS` ids | 1 = `DOCK_STD` | id, append-only | add ids, never renumber | n/a | n/a | LY-14 `CLASS_MISMATCH`; Assembly; Authoring |

| `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX` | 48 | px of straight wall on the non-hinge side | 0–96 | wasted wall; rooms must be wider | doors jam against corners; the approach square fails first | D.18; `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR` |
| ★ `ROOM_DEPTH_SOFT_MAX` | 5 (= `D_FIT`, D.15) | tiles | 4–7 | deep rooms leave the frame; the far wall hides behind the 96 wall-art rows | rooms feel cramped | D.15; Pipeline base resolution |
| ★ `ROOM_WIDTH_SOFT_MAX` | 12 (= `W_FIT`) | tiles | 8–16 | side walls leave the frame | cramped | D.15 |
| ★ `HULL_RUNS_MIN` (outer rooms) | 2 | disjoint exterior runs | 1–3 | impossible on small rooms | one breach spot per room | LY-21; the Graph's 7 HULL_SEGMENTs (LSG:228) |
| ★ `HULL_SEGMENT_FACE_TILES` | 2 (1–4 per segment) | tiles | 1–4 | one segment spans a wall | a tiny breach whose trails do not out-contrast | LY-21; Failure & Trail |

## Visual/Audio Requirements

**Layout draws nothing and plays nothing itself.** It is scene-tree free (kernel H10) and owns no sprite, no sound and no light. What it owns is the *data* that the PROJECTOR (LY-31) turns into sprites, and every visible or audible thing below is a consequence of that data. The rendering GDD is the Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (its PR-5 layer stack and z-band table, Y-sort key D.12, `entity_id` tie-break at PIPE:553 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was `creation_id`)*); the lighting and occlusion consequences belong to the Emitter State GDD and 2D Lighting & Occlusion (Layout's rectangles are their input, never their output). No `spec-art-director.md` existed in the stage directory at authoring time, so the kit-piece art itself, and the projection convention for the double bulkhead of an N/S mate (PIPE:794), are routed to the art-director (Open Questions OQ-15) rather than specified here.

**What the player can see and hear of this system.**

| Event | Visual feedback | Audio feedback | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| A section is built (world load, pod hop) | The PROJECTOR fills the PR-5 stack: floor at z 0 (never Y-sorted), floor marks, then ONE Y-sort `Node2D` holding in tree order wall-face, wall-mounted, doors, props, machinery and actors, with wall caps above. Every placed sprite object carries a Layout-namespaced `entity_id` (LY-31); tiles carry none *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The 48 × 96 wall cell, 96 × 96 door cell and machinery nodes are the art bible 8.2 pieces; the room kit is art bible 6.3. | none owned | MVP |
| A wall, jamb, hull plate or prop is in the way | The drawn **contact line and horizontal extent** equal the rectangle that stops the body and the lamp's beam (LY-7, LY-9): one rect set for sprite, foot and light. **Height is presentation:** a wall's 24-px footprint is drawn as 96 rows of cap and face (PR-5), so the sprite's height is never compared with the rect, only where it meets the floor and how far it runs. A low prop blocks feet but not light and is drawn low (LY-7). The same wording applies to door leaves (contact line and horizontal extent equal the leaf rect) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "drawn extent equals the rect" was false for walls and would have failed a correct art build)*. | none owned (impact and scrape sounds are Body's and Adaptive Audio's) | MVP |
| A door or hatch leaf moves | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Option A, binary.)* Under IA-24's single M13 jump the leaf is binary (LY-9, LY-11): after the attempt window the leaf sprite, sized from the Layout leaf rect of that tick (D.5), is covering the doorway in one tick and absent in the next, and **no tween may give it an extent different from the rect**. Any slide-into-pocket art is the retraction of a leaf that no longer has a rect; it may not outlast the tick the rect disappears, and a stepped slide would be a Vertical Slice decision (OQ-14 c). The four leaf art states of art bible 6.5 (closed, open, cut, welded) map to derived states: `COVERING`, `ABSENT`, `CUT_OPEN`/`CAPPED` plate, `WELDED` leaf. | the door motor and slide sound are the Graph's `door_state` consequence, played by Adaptive Audio; Layout emits no event | MVP |
| A section is welded on | A 4-px **Hull Grey** (`#8C949C`) seam bead runs along the floor and up the wall at the seam line, bright while FRESH. Straight, corner and T variants exist in fresh and old forms. Two hatch leaves project one opening. The bead is on the wall-mounted layer and is **never hidden** by a prop or a lamp (art bible 6.1). The bead is symmetric: it marks **where the join is**, not which side is the original ship. Which side is original comes from the donor's own wear and dust plus the doubled bulkhead (see the donor-wear row below; ASK OQ-25). | the weld sound belongs to Modular Ship Assembly and the Graph's seam events; Layout owns none | MVP |
| A wreck's own wear is read against the home-ship (the "which side was original" cue) | **ASK to the art-director (OQ-25), an MVP requirement for experience target X-1.** Wear is carried by the art of the kit pieces a template uses (art bible 6.1 "Wear and age"), not by any Layout field, flag or runtime state: Layout adds no per-section wear datum and the PROJECTOR picks art by template. Layout asks that, at MVP with Foundry only, the donor (wreck) templates' kit pieces ship in a visibly worn/dusty variant (scuffs, dust, patches, sooting in the sane palette, no flesh or violet) that is clearly distinct from the home-ship's maintained pieces, so that standing at the seam with the doubled bulkhead in view the player can say which side is the original ship. If the art-director cannot commit this for MVP, X-1 is downgraded to Vertical Slice (when three dialects exist) rather than left unsupported. | none | MVP (ASK) |
| A seam ages | At `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` (36,000, 10 minutes of ship time) the bead swaps to its old variant, dulled once lived with (D.11). Age is derived from `welded_at_T`, never a flag. | none | MVP |
| A section is cut away | A SEALED cut leaves a cap plate and nothing beyond it (art bible 6.1); a RAGGED cut leaves a ragged hull piece, solid and light-blocking. The old bead stays beside the scar: history is geometry (Pillar 5). | the leak's sound is the Graph's `A_CUT` hole and Failure & Trail's; Layout owns none | MVP |
| A module is salvaged from a hardpoint | The pedestal or mount rect stays (the `MACHINE` footprint remains, LY-20); what is drawn on it is Item DB / Authoring art. | none owned | MVP |
| Zoom ladder changes | Nothing in Layout changes: every rectangle is base px and identical at every step (LY-3 e); the PROJECTOR output is integer-snapped by the Pipeline. | none | MVP |
| Debug view of the rectangles and ids | A developer-only overlay (Debug Overlay, True Values) may draw every rect, `room_id` and `layout_hash`; absent from release builds, never a player surface. | none | Dev only |

**Audio summary.** None owned. Every sound that accompanies a Layout fact is the consequence of another system's event (the Graph's `door_state`, the weld command, a failure). This is deliberate: Layout publishes no event, so no sound can be keyed to geometry that lies.

**Art-bible sections that bind this note:** 3.3 environment geometry and dialect rules (the graft keeps its donor dialect; at MVP every template is `foundry`, so dialect contrast is *not* promised at MVP and donor-wear differentiation for the hot wreck's pieces is an explicit MVP ASK to the art-director, OQ-25, on which experience target X-1 depends), 6.1 architecture and the seam and cap plate "never hidden", 6.3 the room kit (rules 1-8, which are CONTENT lint, Authoring's), 6.5 the door leaf states, 8.2 the pixel-size table (cells, footprints, the 4-px seam bead), 8.6 texture-atlas organisation (where the kit sheets live; Layout names no atlas). No colour in the flesh or violet band appears on any Layout piece: the sane palette is inviolable (P1) and Layout has nothing to leak.

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:ship-section-and-layout-data-model` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions, and generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

Optional for a `Core` data system, and written as a real note because the one thing the player feels of Layout is *solidity*: **the wall is the one thing in the game that never lies.** The feel of acting on the ship (the weld cursor, the cut, the door press) belongs to Modular Ship Assembly and Interaction; the feel of walking belongs to the Player Controller & Input and Ship Body GDDs. Layout owns no input, no animation clock and no impact effect, and says so in each part below.

### Feel Reference

Project Zomboid's walls and doorframes: a wall stops the body, the eye and the thrown thing at one line, and a door is exactly as open to the foot as it is to the eye. The quality borrowed is *legibility by consequence* (one rectangle set for collision, light and drawing, LY-9) with the consequence arriving in the same tick the door moves. Anti-reference: the "I can see through it but cannot walk through it" glass pane and the "invisible wall" — the secondary design test of the Player Fantasy, and a Pillar 1 failure.

### Input Responsiveness

Layout owns no input, so it adds no latency. The only timing it fixes is that a door state change lands in the geometry the same tick it is made: a command applies in P2, `layout.doors` re-derives the leaf in P3, and the sweep, the Emitter's segment test and the drawn leaf all read the new rect in that tick (LY-10; the mirror of Controller PC-18 test 7).

| Action | Max input-to-response latency | Frame budget (60 fps) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Door open or close, leaf geometry | 0 ticks beyond the Graph's `area` change (same tick) | 0 frames added by Layout | The latency from the press to the `area` change is Interaction's and the Graph's. |
| Weld or cut recorded in geometry | 0 ticks beyond the command's `apply` | 0 frames added by Layout | The weld command's pacing and any bar are Assembly's. |
| Rebuild of the scene after a weld | up to the PROJECTOR build (`BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME` 256, ≤ 3 frames for the MVP world, spike) | ≤ 3 frames | the Pipeline holds the plate until "ready"; `ARRIVING` itself is `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` 45 (LY-25). |

### Animation Feel Targets

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Option A, binary; the earlier text promised a leaf that moves an integer number of pixels each tick.)* Layout has one changing thing, the door leaf, and it is not an animation clip: the leaf is a rectangle whose length `leaf_w` is a whole number of pixels (D.5) and, under the MVP's binary door (IA-24, LY-11), it takes exactly two values — `width_px` (covering) and 0 (absent) — switching in a single tick at the end of the `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` window. There is no sub-pixel position and no in-between frame to shimmer. **The leaf sprite equals the rect: no tween, ease or interpolation may give it an extent that differs from the rect of the same tick**, because the foot and the lamp beam read that rect. The motor whine during the attempt window is Adaptive Audio's. Startup, active and recovery frame data are therefore not Layout's: the leaf has no windup and no recovery, and it never changes back unless the Graph's `area` does. A stepped slide is a Vertical Slice option that would name its owner (OQ-14 c).

| Animation | Startup | Active | Recovery | Feel goal | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Door leaf change (covering ↔ absent) | the attempt window (`DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` 30, Interaction) with the leaf unmoved | 1 tick (the M13 jump) | 0 | Binary under IA-24's single M13 jump (LY-9, LY-11). Mechanical and honest: the gap you can see is the gap you can use, in the tick it exists | Frame data owned by Interaction; extent owned here; no tween may differ from the rect *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Seam bead fresh to old | n/a | one swap at `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` | n/a | History, not an event: no flash, no sound | Presentation piece swap |

### Impact Moments

Layout has none, by design. No hit-stop, shake, camera impact or rumble is owned here; a body stopping at a wall is the sweep's result (Body), the thud is Adaptive Audio's, and a breach is the Graph's and Failure & Trail's. The weld and the cut are the biggest moments in the ship's life and their punctuation belongs to Assembly, the Graph and the audio GDDs; what Layout contributes is permanence: the bead and the cap plate are still there in the next session.

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight:** heavy and fixed. Geometry never moves on its own: a weld translates a whole absorbed body by one exact vector on the 24-px grid in one atomic step, and a cut re-bases the other component the same way.
- **Player control:** high, because the player can always predict the result: a wall edge is where it looks, and the foot box legal against a rect is legal everywhere.
- **Snap quality:** crisp and binary for rects (a cell is inside or outside an occupied span) and, under the MVP's binary door, for the leaf too: it is covering or absent, never in between *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **Acceleration model:** none; Layout contains no motion model.
- **Failure texture:** a refused weld or cut is a stable reason code from a closed set (`SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`, …); a door that will not close on a body is refused (the verb owner's token, OQ-14), and the body is never shoved (LY-11). The "why" is always a geometric fact, never a hidden number. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A blocked close and an unpowered door would otherwise both be only a motor whine, so Layout supplies the geometric why of the first (`leaf_obstruction`, LY-11) for a rect-honest "obstructed" cue that an unpowered door can never produce; its form, and whether it may show during `CLOSING_BLOCKED` without becoming a power oracle, are Interaction's and the art-director's (OQ-31), and the playtest criterion below checks that players can tell the two apart.

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] No playtester reports seeing through a wall or door they could not walk through, or the reverse, across a full MVP session (Player Fantasy experience target X-2; mirrored by the same-tick door criterion in Acceptance Criteria group D).
- [ ] After one weld and one session, a playtester can walk to the seam and say which side was the original ship without prompting (X-1). Gate: this criterion is BLOCKED-ON the art-director's donor-wear commitment (OQ-25); the seam bead alone is symmetric and does not satisfy it. If OQ-25 is declined, X-1 is a Vertical Slice criterion, not MVP.
- [ ] A playtester who cut the graft can point to where it was in a later session (X-4).
- [ ] Asked "can you rearrange your ship?", playtesters do not ask for a build mode (X-3, MVP form); the fuller answer "no, but we choose what to weld on and cut off" is a Vertical Slice criterion, because one socket and one mate leave no choice of *what* to weld *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- [ ] Protocol for every criterion above: at least 5 first-time playtesters, a scripted session, transcripts coded by two reviewers; X-1 passes at 4 of 5, X-2 at 0 of 5 reports, X-3 and X-4 at 4 of 5 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- [ ] With a door blocked by a crewmate in the doorway and a door with no power in the same session, at least 4 of 5 playtesters correctly say which is which without being told, and none says "the door is broken" of the blocked one (AC-148). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; blocked on the Interaction / art-director cue, OQ-31)*
- [ ] No reviewer uses the words "invisible wall", "sticky door" or "clipping" about a wall or door (the door-leaf evidence frames in Acceptance Criteria group I show leaf, body stop and beam stop coincident).

## UI Requirements

**No UI of its own; surfaces owned by Modular Ship Assembly, Interaction, the HUD & Suit Layer and the Debug Overlay.** Layout is a data model and names no HUD shape, no map, no minimap, no room outline and no overlay of the ship: the game's rule is that the world is shown in the world, never as a meter (Pillar 3), and a "map of the ship" would be both a meter and a step toward the freeform-builder anti-pillar (guard rail G3). The player meets Layout as walls, doors and seams. What Layout does is *supply the data* that other systems' surfaces need, and this section lists exactly that so the owners of those surfaces can ask for it.

| Data Layout supplies | Read | Consumed by (surface owner) | Update frequency | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weld and cut cursor outline: the socket reference point `pos_px`, the facing, `width_tiles`, the translated hull footprints of a candidate mate, and the verdict reason from `can_attach` (`SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`, …); **`hatch_one_sided`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, shown diegetically in the outline as a closed bulkhead with no hatch ring on the bare side ("no passage here"), so the irreversible socket is never spent on a graft that has no passage the player could see coming | `socket_plan`, `can_attach`, `can_detach`, `section_offset` | Modular Ship Assembly (the preview cursor is Assembly's; designed) | on cursor change; memoised on `(socket_a, socket_b, static_version_a, static_version_b)` (a door animating on either body must not invalidate it, AC-160 (e), AC-138; *amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*) | an Assembly tool is held at a socket |
| Socket highlight: the anchor of each `FREE` socket and its state (`FREE`, `WELDED`, `CAPPED`, `CUT_OPEN`) | socket-state table, `subject_anchors` | Assembly and Interaction highlight, drawn in the world on the wall-mounted layer | on change of the socket table (`SEC/<id>`) | a weld or cut tool is held; never a persistent marker |
| Prompt anchors: two `DOOR_FACE` anchors per interior door (one inner-face anchor per hatch, LY-23; *amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*), hardpoint, socket, slot and console anchors | `subject_anchors(section_id)` | Interaction (names the kind, registers the subject) and the HUD & Suit Layer (prompt placement) | on `SEC/<id>` | the avatar is within reach of an anchor |
| Door-close verdict for a refused press (`OBSTRUCTED`, Interaction's token) and the geometric why of a blocked retry | `leaf_would_overlap(opening_ref, …)` through Interaction's validate; `leaf_obstruction` for the cue's target *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Interaction feedback, mapped from the reason code to a cue without any display string in Layout; the obstructed cue is Interaction's and the art-director's (OQ-31) | on a press | a body stands in the doorway |
| Diagnostic reads: every table, `layout_hash`, `version`, `stale_leaf` and `leaf_area_out_of_range` flags, `rect_budget_exceeded`, `hatch_one_sided` | all LY-28 reads, read-only | Debug Overlay (True Values) as a `DIAGNOSTIC` subscriber | per tick when open | developer builds only; never an accessibility feature |
| Seam age and scar state as shown in the world | `welds[]`, `age_class` (D.11) | the PROJECTOR (a presentation piece, not a UI surface) | on `S/<seam_id>` and at the age threshold | always |

**Rules for those surfaces (binding on their owners, stated here so they are not rediscovered).** (1) The player never sees a number, a percentage or a progress ring for Layout state: seam freshness, volume `V` and `exposed_tiles` are not displayed. (2) No Layout read has a perceived variant, so a surface that shows a door or wall state shows the real one; a hallucinated door is the Perception Filter's presentation, not a Layout surface. (3) Reason codes become feedback in the surface owner's table (CR-5 of the kernel: display strings never enter a simulation system). (4) The weld and cut cursor outline and the socket highlight use the art bible's UI shape grammar and "the HUD is a suit, the ship is an instrument" rule (art bible 3.4 and 7.0, 7.3 for the three motions) and are drawn in the world space of the avatar's body frame, never in a corner panel. Any such screen or flow is specified by `/ux-design` (Assembly owns the weld and cut tool surface; the HUD & Suit Layer owns prompt placement), not here.

*(No UX flag: nothing in this GDD is a player screen. If Modular Ship Assembly later asks Layout for a new displayable datum it is added to the table above through that GDD's Cross-References, not by editing the schema.)*

## Cross-References

Every explicit dependency on another GDD's mechanic, value or rule, for `/review-all-gdds` Phase 2c. Nature ∈ {Data dependency, State trigger, Rule dependency, Ownership handoff}. Line numbers (`LSG:74` etc.) are the files as they stand on 2026-10-26 and may drift; the named element is authoritative. Section A lists what this GDD references; B the routed edits owed to neighbours (wording refreshes, amendments, index edges); C the registry handling (Phase 5 of the orchestrated run — recorded here, not performed by this stage).

**A. What this document references.**

| This Document References | Target GDD | Specific Element Referenced | Nature |

| `weld_offset` | `expression`: `a` is the survivor-side socket (the body with the lower `body_id`), never the first argument | D.6, LY-16 |
| `rect_algebra_closed_span` | Add the single convention: input boxes half-open, `rects_in` and `solid_in` output the occupied span | LY-12 b |
| `socket_state` | Add the variant-slot activity matrix (PLATE / JAMB / LEAF per state) as an attribute; `reason_set_can_detach`: `SEAM_UNKNOWN, SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE, CAPACITY` | LY-15, LY-29 |
| `MAX_BODIES` | No value change; the note that it is the hard cap of `can_detach` `CAPACITY` and `can_build` `CAPACITY`, and that `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` is a reported margin only | LY-29 |
| `mvp_template_roster` (Derelict Authoring's) | No Layout change requested; D.13 now uses its V totals (182 / 118 / 149 / 4 = 453) | D.13 | 

## Acceptance Criteria

Criteria are GIVEN / WHEN / THEN and each cites the rule (`LY-n`) or formula (`D.n`) it proves. **Tags** (a closed set): **(auto)** an automated test under `tests/unit/layout/` (qualifier *integration* = `tests/integration/layout/`, a stub of a neighbour is allowed); **(lint)** the structural lint (LY-30) run by the template loader in BOOTING and by the offline tool, over a fixture; **(review)** a reviewer reads this GDD or the code against a quoted text; **(manual)** a scripted play session with a screenshot retained in `production/qa/evidence/`; **(spike)** a measured prototype with a stated pass line. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Runner class:** an AC that needs a rendered frame, a GPU, a draw-call count or a pixel probe is tagged **(windowed)** and is evidence-only until a windowed runner exists (CI runs `--headless` with the dummy renderer, so such an AC cannot run there); every AC not so tagged is headless-capable. A (doc-lint) or (review + auto) AC that reads a file lives under `tests/lint/layout/`, not `tests/unit/`. `[UNV]` marks a claim that cannot be sourced from `docs/engine-reference/godot/` (no scripting, core, tilemap or 2d module exists there). **BLOCKED-ON** marks a criterion that cannot pass until a named neighbour amendment or answer lands, so a red result is not misread as a Layout defect. Numbers are the Formulas' worked examples verbatim.

**Test-plan conventions.** (1) Where a criterion lists several lint ids it is *table-driven*: one fixture and one expected id per row, one assertion per id, so a failure names one id (the criterion passes when every row passes). (2) Test files follow `.claude/rules/test-standards.md`: `layout_[feature]_test.gd`, functions `test_[scenario]_[expected]`. (3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The Graph now publishes `opening(edge_id) -> {area, area_max, door_state}` (LY-10; the ask is closed), so the criteria formerly tagged BLOCKED-ON *Graph ASK* are **runnable against the real Graph read**; the harness may still substitute the opening-read double of the test-double table below for a unit-level test that must write `area` directly, and the same criterion must then also pass against the real read in the integration run. (4) Criteria tagged BLOCKED-ON *Controller amendment* need `body.integrate after: [controller.step, layout.doors]`, now registered by Ship Body BDY-8 (PC-14 / PC-15 read the same); until Body's registration lands in code the harness registers that hook order itself and the criterion proves only Layout's half.
