### A. Determinism, units, hash and data purity

- **AC-1** (auto) [D.1, LY-3 f] GIVEN `tile(x)` WHEN evaluated for `x` in `{−49, −48, −1, 0, 47, 48, 407, 432}` THEN the results are `{−2, −1, −1, 0, 0, 1, 8, 9}` with `px(tile(x)) <= x < px(tile(x)+1)` for each; AND `half(432) = 18`, `px(9) = 432`, `half(−1) = −1`.
- **AC-2** (auto) [LY-3 a, LY-26 a, SL-2] GIVEN the whole `layout.state` snapshot WHEN a typed walk visits every value THEN no float, `Vector2`, `Vector2i`, `Rect2`, `Color` or `Resource` appears **in the snapshot or the dev dump**, and no float appears in any Layout query result; query results are integers, integer arrays, bools, enums, String ids, records of those, and polylines of integer pairs. **In memory** a `Vector2i` (or one pinned integer-pair representation) is allowed as the storage type of a query result, because banning it would force a heap allocation on hot paths and contradict LY-12 e; `Vector2` (float), `Rect2` and `Color` stay banned everywhere. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: 'integer or integer array' failed against bools, enums, records and polylines.)*
- **AC-3** (auto) [LY-2] GIVEN a template loader fed the JSON numbers `48`, `48.0`, `48.5`, `-1`, `1000000000` and the string `"48"` for a non-negative `TILE`-typed field WHEN loaded THEN `48` and `48.0` load as the integer 48 (`typeof == TYPE_INT`), and `48.5`, `-1`, `1000000000` (over range) and `"48"` are each rejected with `SCHEMA_TYPE_RANGE`. (The test asserts the loader's own explicit cast and bound check, which passes whatever the engine's JSON parser returns; the engine's float behaviour itself is a probe in the SP-ENG-1 spike group, AC-162, not a Logic assertion.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-4** (auto) [LY-2] GIVEN the snapshot of the MVP world WHEN its keys are listed THEN none is named `rects`, `tiles`, `rooms` or `walls`; it holds instance rows, socket rows, `welds[]`, bodies and counters only (a template is never in a snapshot).
- **AC-5** (auto) [LY-33, D.10] GIVEN two runs of the same command stream from `default_state()` WHEN `layout_hash()` is read after each command THEN the two sequences are identical, and a change to a derived cache alone (`version`, grid, leaf cache) never changes `layout_hash`.
- **AC-6** (auto) [D.10] GIVEN the counters `next_section_id`, `next_body_id`, `next_room_id` WHEN canonical bytes are produced THEN the `counters` dictionary's contribution is 102 bytes = 9 + 30 + 30 + 33 (kernel TLV, 119 including the key `counters`), and swapping the array positions of two table rows without changing their ids changes `layout_hash`. (Golden block vectors are the kernel's, frozen with an independent reference at the kernel spike, kernel Gaps.) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)*
- **AC-7** (auto) [LY-26 a, SL-2] GIVEN `welded_at_T` and `cut_at_T` of 2^40 and 2^53 + 1 WHEN snapshotted, restored and re-snapshotted THEN the values and `layout_hash` are exactly equal and each 64-bit value is stored as two int32 halves `(hi, lo)` in the snapshot; AND WHEN the snapshot is rendered by the dev JSON dump and parsed back THEN each 64-bit value appears as an exact-width 16-character lowercase hex string (never a JSON number) and the snapshot rebuilt from that dump restores to the same `layout_hash`; AND a typed walk finds **no Dictionary with an integer key** anywhere in the snapshot (every table is parallel packed arrays in ascending id order). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the float-parser failure itself is probed in AC-162.)*
- **AC-163** (auto) [D.10] GIVEN a world with no mutation between calls WHEN `layout_hash()` is called twice THEN the second call recomputes nothing (the kernel's instrumented `canonical_bytes` encode counter for `layout.state`, KRN-PERF-01 / KRN-HASH-03, is unchanged and no `block_hash` call is made) and returns the same value; AND after any of LM1-LM4 the next call recomputes once; AND no per-tick path (a 600-tick run with doors only) performs a `layout.state` encode or `block_hash` call; AND `geometry_hash` / `visual_hash` are computed once per template load. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: hash cadence)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)*
- **AC-172** (auto) [D.10, LY-26 a, LY-33] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: owner of the stored hash, `mutation_count`, and the signed-int32 round trip)* GIVEN (a) two equal blocks (same instances, sockets, welds, bodies, counters) built by different histories (for example a build, weld and cut sequence versus the equal end state restored from a snapshot) THEN `layout_hash` is equal and `mutation_count` appears in no snapshot key, no allow-list entry and no canonical byte; (b) a block restored from a snapshot THEN `mutation_count` is 0 and the memo is invalidated (the first `layout_hash()` recomputes); (c) a template whose `geometry_hash` is at least 2^31 (the fixture forces `0xDEADBEEF`) persisted through a `PackedInt32Array` and read back WHEN compared after `& 0xFFFFFFFF` normalisation THEN restore succeeds with no `LAYOUT_RESTORE_TEMPLATE_CHANGED`, and the same value compared without normalisation is shown negative by the harness (the pitfall is real); (d) GIVEN H64 (the kernel's) of the ASCII bytes "abc" THEN the 32-byte SHA-256 digest begins ba7816bf8f01cfea; the integer (first 8 bytes read little-endian) is 0xeacf018fbf1678ba = −1527000031757436742 as a GDScript int; the split is hi = 0xeacf018f, lo = 0xbf1678ba, stored in a `PackedInt32Array` as hi = 3939434895 − 4294967296 = −355532401 and lo = 3205920954 − 4294967296 = −1089046342, and normalised with `& 0xFFFFFFFF` they read back 3939434895 and 3205920954; the header hex is "ba7816bf8f01cfea" (not "eacf018fbf1678ba"). Both halves are at least 2^31, so the vector also exercises (c). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)*
- **AC-181** (auto, integration) [D.10, LY-33] *(Added 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)* GIVEN the MVP fixture world captured by a Save/Load stub in a dev build WHEN the stub records the kernel's `block_hash(&"layout.state")` as the header `block_state_hash` and Layout's `layout_hash()` is read THEN `hex64(layout_hash)` equals `block_state_hash` exactly; AND after `restore()`/`post_restore()` cadence (ii) raises no mismatch diagnostic; AND a one-bit change to a derived cache alone changes neither value; AND `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` changes iff `layout_hash` changes (AC-119).
- **AC-155** (auto) [LY-26 a] GIVEN a captured `snapshot()` WHEN the live block is then mutated by each of LM1-LM4 THEN the captured snapshot is byte-identical to what it was at capture (`snapshot()` deep-copies; no packed array is shared with the live block); AND GIVEN a snapshot passed to `restore()` and then modified by the caller THEN the restored block is unchanged. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: aliasing)*
- **AC-8** (lint) [LY-33] GIVEN the Layout module source WHEN scanned THEN there is no `for … in` over a `Dictionary` (the exact patterns are listed in `tests/lint/layout/stale_tokens_test.gd`) and no call to `rng_derive`, **no call to the built-in `hash()`, `Array.hash()` or `Dictionary.hash()` (their output is not stable across engine builds and is banned for any persisted or compared value; the only hash is the kernel's H64 (SHA-256 truncated, through `HashingContext`), and no GDScript loop accumulates a hash over bytes (lint: no loop body combining a running 64-bit accumulator with multiply, xor or shift over a byte array))** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ruling consequence of X13 / R-17, kernel D.12)* and **no `DirAccess` scan in the template loader (the catalogue comes from the committed manifest, LY-2)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; AND (auto) GIVEN the same world built with the template catalogue loaded in two different key-insertion orders THEN `layout_hash` and the outputs of every read in LY-28 are identical.
- **AC-9** (auto) [LY-28] GIVEN each read in the LY-28 table WHEN called twice with the same arguments and no mutation between THEN the results are byte-identical and no read changes `layout_hash`.
- **AC-10** (auto) [LY-3 c, LY-28] GIVEN each geometry read WHEN its frame is checked THEN every read whose LY-28 Frame column is SECTION returns section-local px, every read whose Frame column is **BODY** (`rects_in`, `solid_in`, `clip_to_footprints`, `leaf_would_overlap`, `leaf_obstruction`, `section_at`, `section_offset`, `weld_offset`, `detach_preview`, and any other row whose Frame column reads BODY or body, which the test enumerates from the LY-28 table rather than from a hand list) returns body-frame px, and no read returns a world-frame value. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the exemption is now 'LY-28 Frame column = BODY'; the earlier two-name list omitted six BODY-frame reads.)*
- **AC-11** (auto) [LY-3 e] GIVEN every Layout test and query WHEN the source and inputs are scanned THEN no `k`, `s`, `field_h` or zoom value is an input or constant, and every result is identical when the harness changes the Pipeline's zoom step across the whole ladder `k = 1 … k_auto + 2`.
- **AC-12** (auto) [LY-3 f, D.9] GIVEN a section left at a negative body-frame offset by a detach re-base WHEN `rects_in` runs over a box straddling `x = 0` and the broadphase cell is computed for `x = −1` and `x = −48` THEN the rect set equals a brute-force scan and both cell indices are −1 (floor division, not truncation).
- **AC-13** (auto) [LY-26 e, States 2.1] GIVEN `default_state()` WHEN read THEN there are no bodies, sections, welds or sockets and the three counters are 1; the first `build_section` returns `section_id` 1 and `body_id` 1; AND `snapshot() → restore() → snapshot()` is byte-identical with an unchanged `layout_hash` for the MVP world, for a world containing a cut, and for `default_state()`.
- **AC-14** (lint) [Tuning Knobs, coding standard] GIVEN the Layout-owned knobs WHEN the module source is scanned THEN none appears as a literal outside the default-config file (the stride literals `RECT_KEY_STRIDE` 4,096 and `2^20` are asserted at their **default** only, since they are also structural bit widths of the `rect_key`, D.9); AND (auto, **table-driven**: one row per knob **of the six listed in the table below**, one assertion per cell, a failure names the row; the remaining range-bearing knobs are covered by AC-171) each knob loaded at its range minimum and at its range maximum starts the module without error and produces exactly the expected value below; an out-of-range value (one step beyond either end) is rejected loudly with the knob's name and never clamped. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "the documented behaviour follows" had no fail condition.)*

| `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` | 3,600: weld at 100,000 is FRESH at 103,599 and OLD at 103,600 | 216,000: FRESH at 315,999 and OLD at 316,000 | 3,599 and 216,001 rejected |

### I. PROJECTOR and scene-tree freedom

- **AC-120** (auto) [LY-31, KRN H10 — the headless no-Node test] GIVEN the Layout data module, its queries and mutators WHEN loaded and fully exercised in a headless run with no scene tree THEN it executes without error, **adds no node under the tree root and the orphan-node count is unchanged after the run** (the observable form in Godot; a literal '0-node scene tree' is not observable) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and a static scan finds zero references to `Node`, `Node2D`, `TileMapLayer`, `SceneTree`, `Resource` subclasses or any engine physics type in the module; only the separate PROJECTOR class references them, and the dependency is one-way.

(b) GIVEN a constructed set of PROJECTOR objects (the `ProjectorRecorder` fixture) with equal `sort_y` and `class_rank` 2 (a door leaf, a prop and a machinery node with `entity_id` values chosen out of construction order) WHEN the Pipeline comparator `(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)` sorts them THEN they come out in ascending `entity_id` regardless of construction order, and a `class_rank` 3 actor at equal `sort_y` sorts after all of them. (c) GIVEN two objects built in the same section THEN their `entity_id` values differ only in `kind_ordinal` / `template_local_idx`, and a Layout id never equals a Body or Interaction stub id with the same low bits. (d) (windowed, GPU runner, evidence-only) GIVEN a wall run, WHEN an actor stands one px north and one px south of it THEN a pixel probe shows the actor hidden behind the face in the first case and drawn over it in the second, with the cap above both. ### K. Performance budget

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Headless versus windowed.** CI runs `--headless` (dummy renderer). The CPU-only rows (**AC-136**, **AC-137**, **AC-164**, **AC-138**, **AC-139**, the AC-141 estimator) are **headless-capable** and keep **provisional lines, pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK** (a run on the developer's machine is indicative only and records the machine's specification) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*. The render-dependent rows (**AC-140** draw-call and build frames, **AC-165** weld frame, **AC-123 (d)** pixel probe, the visual rows AC-124 and AC-126) are **windowed / GPU**: they are evidence-only until a windowed runner exists, and never gate. **Reference machines (OQ-2 / X14, closed 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12):** **RM-DESKTOP** (Windows 10/11 64-bit; 6-core/6-thread CPU of the Intel Core i5-9400F / AMD Ryzen 5 2600 class; 8 GB RAM; GPU of the GTX 1050 Ti 4 GB / RX 570 4 GB class, Vulkan 1.2+; SATA SSD; 1920x1080 at 60 Hz; P99 CPU frame-time ceiling 16.6 ms) and **RM-DECK** (Steam Deck LCD 2022, SteamOS stable, 1280x800 at 60 Hz, 30 fps target; ceiling 33.3 ms), recorded in `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`. **Build type for every wall-clock figure:** a Godot 4.7.2 release export template (not the editor, not a debug template), renderer `forward_plus`, vsync on, the pipeline's warm-up of 120 frames for render-dependent rows, with the startup read-back (renderer, driver) recorded with each result. Pass needs P99 <= 90 % of the ceiling (headroom >= 1.7 ms desktop, >= 3.3 ms Deck). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* **AC-164's aggregate (8 actors per tick at most 0.6 ms) is the budget; AC-136's 0.05 ms P99 per actor is the target.** Reference machines: `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec` names RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK, so the pass lines below bind on those machines at the spike (a value not yet measured is "pending spike", not blocked). Each run **records the machine specification, the startup read-back and the measured P99 and mean as retained evidence** (`production/qa/evidence/layout-perf-<criterion>-<machine>.md`; a measurement from the developer's machine is indicative only, never a pass). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* If a measured rung misses its line, the fallback ladder of D.22 is climbed one data-layout step at a time; a GDExtension or C# rung needs technical-director sign-off. The cases cover D.22's terms: a single actor, **8 aggregate actors per tick**, and an **Emitter-sized query** (a 288 × 288 px box). Every measurement uses a Godot 4.7.2 release export, not the editor-debug build, a warm-up of 100 calls (CPU-only micro-probes; render-dependent rows use the pipeline's 120-frame warm-up), and at least 10,000 calls (or 600 ticks × 2 actors).

- **AC-136** (spike) [LY-12 c, D.22, PCI:1078] GIVEN a body of 2,000 rects and a swept foot box WHEN one `solid_in` plus the analytic sweep runs per actor per tick THEN P99 <= 0.05 ms, the GDScript cost [UNV] recorded; if it misses, `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` is tuned before the budget is renegotiated. Gated on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK, pending spike measurement. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*
- **AC-164** (spike) [LY-12 c, D.22] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the aggregate-actor and Emitter-box cases)* GIVEN a body of 2,000 rects WHEN (a) 8 actors each issue one `solid_in` plus the analytic sweep in one tick, and (b) one `rects_in` over a 288 × 288 px box (up to 49 cells at cell 48) is issued as the Emitter does on a `static_version` change, each with a caller-owned `QueryBuffer`, THEN the measured P99 and mean for (a) per tick and for (b) per call are recorded as evidence with the machine (AC-136's rules), including the split into call overhead, cell lookup, de-dup and merge terms where the profiler allows; the pass lines bind on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK at the spike, pending spike measurement *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* (planning expectation: (a) at most 0.6 ms, (b) at most 0.3 ms).
- **AC-137** (spike) [LY-12 e] GIVEN 10,000 `rects_in` and `solid_in` calls after a warm-up of 100 WHEN evaluated THEN net static-memory growth over the last 9,000 calls is 0 bytes (monitor behaviour [UNV]; fallback: a code review finding no fresh `Array` or `PackedArray` construction inside the two functions).
- **AC-138** (spike) [D.22] GIVEN the MVP weld (about 200 rects per side) WHEN `can_attach` runs a `GEOM_OVERLAP` preview THEN <= 2 ms, and a repeat memoised on `(socket_a, socket_b, static_version_a, static_version_b)` is O(1) and survives a door animating on either body; the committing `apply` may exceed `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 (a logging threshold only). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-139** (spike) [LY-10] GIVEN `MAX_DOORS_LOADED` 48 doors all animating WHEN `layout.doors` runs THEN its cost is <= 0.2 ms (5 % of `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4) and `leaf_version` (so `version`) is bumped at most once per section and `static_version` never. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-140** (spike, windowed, evidence-only until a windowed GPU runner exists) [LY-31, ADR-LAY-03 input; prerequisite: SP-YSORT-1, LY-31] GIVEN the MVP world (**453 tiles**: 182 + 118 + 149 + the 4-tile pod, registry `mvp_template_roster`, equal to D.13 and AC-100; the `TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600 ceiling is the worst case) WHEN the PROJECTOR builds it at `BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME` 256 THEN the build completes in 3 frames or fewer with no frame over the ceiling (16.6 ms on RM-DESKTOP, 33.3 ms on RM-DECK; pending spike measurement) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*, and the Pipeline holds the plate until the PROJECTOR reports ready (`ARRIVING` itself is `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` 45); the per-section versus per-body `TileMapLayer` draw-call count and the Y-sort behaviour under reparent are recorded as inputs to ADR-LAY-03 (no pass line until the weld spike produces one, see Gaps). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-141** (auto) [D.21] GIVEN the estimator WHEN evaluated THEN `(I, K, S, B) = (4, 1, 2, 4)` returns exactly 340 and `(224, 200, 400, 208)` returns 28,556 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: 13 ints per instance row; was 324 and 27,660)*; the serialised MVP snapshot's measured size is **recorded as evidence** (a number in `production/qa/evidence/layout-snapshot-bytes.md`), with the soft expectation "at most twice the estimate plus the container overhead" being a spike observation, not a pass line, until the container format is measured [UNV]; GIVEN 200 tombstones (28,556 estimated bytes) THEN no size diagnostic fires, and a state estimated at 65,536 bytes or more (`LAYOUT_BLOCK_SOFT_BYTES`) raises it exactly once.

**Group M. Engine probes and spikes that gate other criteria** *(added in the design review, 2026-10-27; a bold group label, not a new heading)*

*These assertions depend on engine behaviour that is not sourceable from `docs/engine-reference/godot/` (it has no scripting, core, tilemap or 2d module). They were mixed into BLOCKING Logic criteria; they are now a spike story with exit criteria, a **Config/Data ADVISORY** gate, and the Logic criteria above assert only Layout's own encoding and conversions.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

- **AC-162** (spike, SP-ENG-1) [LY-2, LY-26 a, LY-27, LY-12 e, D.10, D.1] GIVEN a Godot 4.7.2 release export WHEN a probe script runs each of (P1) `JSON.parse_string` on `48`, `48.0` and `2^40` — records whether the result is `TYPE_INT` or `TYPE_FLOAT`; (P2) the 4.6→4.7 packed-array element-write change — records that writing `welds_state[2] = v` does not call the property setter (so the explicit dirty marking of LY-27 is required, and an accidental setter-based flag is detected); (P3) `var_to_bytes(dict, false)` determinism — the same snapshot encoded 100 times in two processes yields identical bytes; (P4) GDScript integer `/` and `%` on negative operands — records truncation versus floor (so the `floor_div` / `posmod` spelling of D.1 is confirmed necessary); (P5) the cost of `PackedInt64Array.resize`, `clear` and element writes at the AC-137 scale and of the kernel's canonical-bytes encode plus `HashingContext` SHA-256 over a 30 KB `layout.state` snapshot (the `layout_hash` cost, D.10), split into encode time and hash time; (P6) *(added 2026-10-27 — re-review)* the H64 engine facts: `HashingContext` SHA-256 of "abc" returns the digest ba7816bf8f01cfea… in 4.7.2 and its first 8 bytes read little-endian give −1527000031757436742, the arithmetic-versus-logical `>>` behaviour of GDScript on a value with bit 63 set, and a `PackedInt32Array` round trip of a value at or above 2^31 (records the sign-extension AC-172 guards against) THEN each result is recorded in `production/qa/evidence/layout-engine-probes.md` with the engine build string; **exit:** every probe has a recorded value and each [UNV] tag it resolves is removed or corrected in this GDD and ADR-LAY-03 / ADR-LAY-06 (template byte order); a probe that contradicts an assumption raises a Layout defect, it does not fail a Logic gate. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-165** (spike, windowed, SP-YSORT-1) [LY-31] GIVEN the Y-sort / PROJECTOR scene-shape spike of LY-31 WHEN options B (no reparent) and A (per-body root, reparent on weld) are built in the spike room with two bodies THEN the five exit criteria of LY-31 are each recorded as pass / fail with a screenshot of the weld frame and the draw-call count, B first; **exit:** ADR-LAY-03 is written from the result before the PROJECTOR story starts. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### Gaps / untestable as written

These are recorded, not hidden. None is a criterion; each has an owner in Open Questions.

- **Foot-box vertical placement — RESOLVED by Ship Body BDY-14 (registry `foot_box`).** The foot origin is the box's TOP row, centred in x: box `[pos.x − 8, pos.x + 8) × [pos.y, pos.y + 8)` (`FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `FOOT_BOX_H` 8), so the first legal row south of a wall `[y_0, y_0 + 24)` is `y_0 + 24` and the rest row north of one is `y_1 − 8`. Layout's criteria stay stated on rectangles and caller boxes (boundary-fixture criterion); only the Controller's PCI:103/447/651/908 'foot-centred' wording remains to refresh (Controller owner).
- **Contracts with neighbours that own the other half.** The restore order of `body.state`, `frame_shift_px` consumption by `BODY_TRANSFER`, the `body.integrate` `after:` order (registered by Ship Body BDY-8) and the Authoring lint hand-offs are untestable inside Layout (all those neighbours are now designed; the half each owns is tested in its own GDD); the host-body rule is Layout's and is tested (AC-150). Only Procedural Derelict Generation's contract is still provisional. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **Reference machines.** RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK are named (delegated ruling R12, OQ-2 closed); the spike pass lines of group K are pending spike measurement on them. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*
- **Template byte order and golden layout vectors.** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The field order of `canonical_geometry_bytes` / `canonical_visual_bytes` waits on ADR-LAY-06; H64 and the block byte form are the kernel's (X13 / R-17, kernel D.12) and the golden block vectors wait for the kernel spike; the criteria assert equality, determinism and the 102-byte counter contribution, not golden values.
- **Detach can run out of bodies — RESOLVED in the design review.** `MAX_BODIES` (8) counts live bodies and a cut allocates a new body; `can_detach` now answers `CAPACITY` in validate (LY-29, AC-158), so the cut is a clean reject rather than a `FAULTED` apply. What stays open is only `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` / `BODY_FULL` (OQ-8). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **`route_length_px` expected integer for the fixture.** D.19 supplies only the budget; the criterion is property-based until the expected integer for a named pair is supplied.
- **PROJECTOR batching and Y-sort tie rule.** `TileMapLayer` batching, the Y-sort tie rule (tree order) and `var_to_bytes` determinism are NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference; they are verified by the weld spike and by the snapshot round-trip criterion of group A before ADR-LAY-03 and ADR-LAY-06 are accepted, and have no pass line of their own.
- **Perception Filter and Interaction answers.** `clip_to_footprints` and `sync_section` are asks (the truth-channel tag is granted, Perception Filter PF-5 row 11), and so is the art-director's donor-wear commitment (OQ-25, which gates Feel AC / experience target X-1 and is also tagged BLOCKED-ON there); the criteria that need them are marked BLOCKED-ON (after this re-review only AC-148 and the Feel X-1 criterion keep the tag; AC-53 and AC-118 were unblocked).

### Story-type mapping (for `/qa-plan` and `/story-done`)

| Story type | Groups | Location | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Logic | A, B (except integration rows), C, D (except integration and manual), E, F, H (except integration rows), K estimator, **and every (lint) row that runs over a committed fixture** (groups C, D, E, G, and AC-142 in L: containment, south-band, schema, socket, hardpoint and anchor lints) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: these were mapped Config/Data ADVISORY, so a containment or south-band regression could ship)* | `tests/unit/layout/` | BLOCKING |
| Doc-lint (reads this GDD or a sibling GDD) | AC-16, AC-66, AC-133 and every other (doc-lint) or (review + auto) row that reads a file | `tests/lint/layout/` (moved out of `tests/unit/`, which must not read files: Independence rule) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | ADVISORY while a sibling GDD is mid-revision; a failure on a sibling GDD is routed as a doc-sync task to that GDD's owner; a deliberate stale reference carries an explicit `stale-ok` marker on its line, which the scan honours |
| Integration | the rows tagged *integration* in B, D, F, H, I, J | `tests/integration/layout/` | BLOCKING |
| Visual/Feel | the two (manual) rows in I and the playtest rows in J and D (AC-148) | `production/qa/evidence/` | BLOCKING (lead sign-off recorded) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Engine probes (spike) | group M (AC-162, AC-165) | `production/qa/evidence/` | ADVISORY (a probe result corrects this GDD; it never fails a Logic gate) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Config/Data | every (lint) row against **authored content** (not a committed fixture): AC-143, AC-167, AC-168, AC-170 | smoke check `production/qa/smoke-[date].md` | ADVISORY (BLOCKING for `/smoke-check` build health) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| Performance | group K (spike) | `tests/performance/layout/` | CPU-only rows (AC-136, AC-137) keep provisional lines, pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK; render-dependent rows (AC-138 weld frame, AC-140, AC-123 (d) pixel probe, AC-165) are evidence-only until a windowed GPU runner exists, then gated on the same two machines (OQ-2 / X14 closed, R12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* |

**Seeded-random convention** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the Testing Standard says 'no random seeds', and ACs 34, 39, 71, 81 and 115 use seeded corpora)*. A seeded criterion is deterministic, not random: the seed is a constant **committed in the test file** (`SEED_AC34 = 0x4C41_0034`, `SEED_AC39 = 0x4C41_0039`, `SEED_AC71 = 0x4C41_0071`, `SEED_AC81 = 0x4C41_0081`, `SEED_AC115 = 0x4C41_0115`, named after the criterion), the generator is the project's own integer PRNG (never the engine's global one), and the test asserts the generated corpus **by count** (and its first and last element) before asserting any property, so a generator change fails loudly and is never silent. A criterion that cannot name its seed is rewritten as a fixed table.

## Open Questions

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier preamble said no user ruling touches Layout; that was stale.)* The earlier binding decisions (zoom ladder, lethal friendly fire, the chest-mounted lamp and glowsticks, restart from last autosave) touch Layout only indirectly, but the 2026-10-27 user rulings do land on it: **X1 / D52** (the cut is in the MVP, OQ-30, resolved), **X2** (the dock-room status glow, D.16, OQ-22), **X3** (the 21–28 tile reference carry, D.19, AC-167, OQ-34), **X7 / D17** (weld and cut autosaves, OQ-32, resolved), **D31** (static spill, Lighting's) and **X14** (the reference machine, OQ-2, closed 2026-10-28 by delegated ruling R12). Rows OQ-2, OQ-30, OQ-32 and OQ-35 are **closed** (their rulings are recorded in the rows); OQ-1 and OQ-9 are decided (R21, R20). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* The rows closest to an open call come first: the socket economy (decided, scheduled with the second weld) and the target hardware (closed). The re-review added OQ-33 to OQ-37. Owner is `user`, `agent` (named) or `GDD` (named); target is `playtest`, `next GDD` or `ADR`. **ASK** rows are additive, non-value requests to a neighbouring owner (coordination rule 5: never edited here). Every row says when it reopens.

| # | Question | Owner | Target | Reopen when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OQ-1 | **Socket economy.** With exactly one home-ship socket (LSG:228) and `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN` terminal (LY-13), the first weld-then-cut leaves no free socket for the rest of the MVP slice. Free sockets on the player's body `F`: a weld of a derelict with `s_b` free sockets changes `F` by `s_b − 2` (the used host socket is lost, the derelict's other `s_b − 1` are gained); a cut-and-discard changes it by `−(s_b − 1)`; the **net cycle is −1** while sockets burn. MVP: `F = 1`, weld derelict B (`s_b = 1`) gives `F = 0`, and cutting it leaves `F = 0`. Three options: **(A)** a re-cut verb that reopens a `CAPPED` socket while leaving its history visible (old bead plus cut ring), net cycle 0; **(B)** more sockets on the home ship; **(C)** every attachable derelict brings at least 3 sockets (`s_b >= 3` makes each weld `dF >= +1`, but the net cycle is still −1, so C only delays the dead end while a derelict stays attached). A lint `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` (content, Vertical Slice) would flag an authored set in which attach-and-cut alone reaches `F = 0` before the sector goal, unless A or C holds. Acceptable for the one-weld MVP slice. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Review recommendation: if this is resolved, use option A** (the only option that keeps the scar the fantasy is built on); until then experience target X-3 is Vertical Slice (Player Fantasy) and the "choose what to graft" claim is a Vertical Slice claim. **Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21 (user delegated all remaining calls):** option A, the review recommendation and the option that keeps the scar the fantasy is built on; the MVP slice is unchanged (one weld, `F = 0` acceptable) and the re-cut verb is scheduled when a second weld enters scope. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)* | agent: modular ship assembly owner (with game-designer) | next GDD (Modular Ship Assembly) | a second weld enters scope, or Vertical Slice scoping |

**Resolved during authoring (recorded so they are not reopened by accident).** (1) Ids start at 1: `default_state()` counters are 1 (States 2.1). (2) `frame_shift_px` on an attach is defined in D.6 (the absorbed frame origin in the host frame; `(576, 96)` for the `fixture_airlock` weld of D.6; the authored MVP pair is `(696, 384)`, registry `mvp_template_roster`). (3) A hatch on one socket only is legal bulkhead-only (`hatch_one_sided`, LY-14). (4) The persisted section `kind`, `SOCKET_FACE_PHASE_PX`, `BODY_FULL` and `OFFSET_OFF_GRID` were dropped or deferred by the checkpoint rulings (LY-29 records them).
